# Catastrophic Extrapolation of the Fitted Potential-Energy Surface

*The force-spike problem of DPA4 / SeZM models: what it is, why it happens, what was tried, and what the trials showed. Closed record, 11 September 2026.*

Every number in this document was measured; the scripts live in `debug/curvature_reg/` and are listed in Appendix A. Measurements ran on RTX PRO 6000 GPUs in float32 with TF32 disabled unless stated. The structural changes that the research produced and that were adopted into the package — the fixed radial basis types, the single cutoff envelope and the descriptor-declared AdamW routing, together with the `v20260911` presets — are documented with the code (`doc/outisli/dpa4.md`, `doc/model/dpa4.md`) and are not repeated here; they appear below only where a result cannot be understood without them.

## Contents

- 0\. Status at closure

## 0. Status at closure

The exploration ended on 11 September 2026 by the user's decision. Its outcome is a diagnosis, a set of instruments, one training-time method with a settled scope and a settled ceiling, and a set of structural components of which some were adopted and some were not.

Adopted into the package (on the `outisli` branch): the fixed radial basis types, the single cutoff envelope and the automatic AdamW routing of the two layers that read the radial basis, with presets that carry them. These remove the three failure modes that were traced to parameters no datum constrains inside the radial machinery: the inward collapse of isolated pairs through the innermost radial channels, the tear storms on compressed contacts, and the growth of the response to untrained element embeddings.

Not adopted, kept as formal code on the branch `self/spike`: the reference-consistency term (the "anchors" or "safeguard") with its hand-over schedule, the pair fade, the radial seed gate, and the isolated-atom energy reference. Each of them buys one of the criteria below at a price the user did not accept for the main branch: the fade and the seed gate meet the tail criterion by moving the release of an isolated pair's energy inside 4.5 Å, which distorts the 3–4.5 Å region; the isolated-atom reference pins the dissociation limit at an energy cost of 1–2 % that closes slowly; the reference term removes holes and tears during pretraining but cannot hold the tails, and in the fine-tune scenario it only delays the collapse of the isolated pair unless the anchors contain isolated pairs.

Open at closure: whether the tail criterion should judge the whole range beyond the principal well with the unit-charge Coulomb bound (§4, §6.6), which would reject the fade explicitly and rank the reference models as they are; how dimer data with real labels could be added to the training set (the user's preferred route, §5.5); and the outer barrier beyond the well of the molecular pairs, which every DeePMD model of the family shows and which needs labelled low-coordination data or a decision on the criterion.

## 1. The phenomenon

A neural potential is a function from coordinates to an energy; forces are its derivative. Where the training data are, the function is smooth and accurate. In regions the data do not cover it develops, as training goes on, features of enormous curvature: the energy rises by hundreds of eV over 0.1 Å and the force reaches 10³–10⁵ eV/Å where physical forces are a few eV/Å. A molecular-dynamics run that reaches such a feature is destroyed in one step, and a validation set that contains one such frame reports a force RMSE a thousand times its MAE. The defect appears in fine-tuned and in pretrained models, and it grows with training length and model size.

### 1.1 Case A — the hydrogen fine-tune failure

The system: 64 hydrogen atoms in a cubic cell of edge 4.658 Å (1.579 Å³ per atom, about 250 GPa) at 1000 K, 0.2 fs time step, LAMMPS with the exported model `HH-DPA4-Neo.pt2` (DPA4-Neo, cutoff 6 Å, 16 Bessel functions, 2 blocks, 32 channels). The model was fine-tuned from `DPA4-Neo-OMat24` for 10⁶ steps on 14702 frames of 64-atom hydrogen cells (1.33–2.4 Å³ per atom) and 608 frames of 128-atom cells; its validation errors were normal (0.96 meV per atom, 52.5 meV/Å).

The run died at step 17097 after 17092 healthy steps: a one-step pressure excursion at 17093 (pressure +34 %, one virial component +62 %, both returning) and then the loss of control at 17097, where the temperature doubles within one 0.2 fs step. The energy barely moves (5 meV per atom at 17093) while the virial jumps, because the energy is a sum over atoms that dilutes one atom's error by the atom count whereas the virial takes a single large pair force at full weight with the pair distance as lever arm. A virial spike with a quiet energy is therefore the signature of a few atoms carrying wrong forces.

The failure reproduces exactly from the checkpoint in eager float32 (`repro_spike.py`): largest force 49 eV/Å at 17093 and 318 eV/Å at 17097, against 4–5 eV/Å on control frames. The defect is a non-physical peak of the fitted surface: scanning the 49–48 bond of frame 17097 gives an energy +285 eV above the frame baseline at 0.64–0.71 Å with radial forces up to 4450 eV/Å, while the same atom's other short bond (49–33) is a normal soft well over the same separations. The peak is a many-body artefact of the environment, not a property of the pair distance (both pairs are at 0.60–0.94 Å, above the training minimum of 0.51 Å). It sits exactly at the working distance of the dynamics, and the pair entered its outer flank seven steps before the failure. Such peaks are not a single accident: a 40-evaluation hill climb within a 0.05 Å ball raises the largest force from 28 to 8043 eV/Å on the late frames (282×) and from 5 to 17 eV/Å (3.3×) on control frames; defects concentrate in the high-pressure, high-coordination region where each atom has about 570 neighbours inside the cutoff. They are invisible from the data: on 2941 training and 1838 validation frames the force RMSE is 0.073 and 0.130 eV/Å and no curvature ratio exceeds 0.40.

The isolated H₂ dimer of the same model is a second face of the same failure: the pretrained models bind H₂ by 4.38 (Neo) and 4.14 eV (Pro) at 0.75 Å, the fine-tuned model reads −4·10¹² eV with forces of 10¹⁶ eV/Å at 4 Å. The dimer is not the cause of the crash, but it is a fast, deterministic, label-free instrument, and it shows that the failure is progressive: the well depth of a plain hydrogen fine-tune runs from −11 eV at 20000 steps to −10⁹ eV at 120000.

### 1.2 Case B — the OMat24 pretraining checkpoint

`Pro/N_6-l_5-c_64x2/model_ema.ckpt-788000.pt` (6 blocks, lmax 5, 64×2 channels, 788000 steps at 2·10⁻⁴ → 6·10⁻⁵, HybridMuon, mixed precision) has a force MAE of 0.107 eV/Å on the 10 % validation subset (107612 frames) and a force RMSE of 61 eV/Å. One frame, 98341 (Li₃Ge₄Au₄, 11 atoms), carries 99.9998 % of the squared force error with a predicted force of 118259 eV/Å against a label of 27; the value is numerically unstable (107256–125279 eV/Å across three float32 evaluations that differ only in batching), so the subset RMSE reads 61–123 eV/Å. The frame has an Au–Li contact at 0.916 Å; among 100000 random training frames no heavier-element contact is shorter than 1.0 Å (the shortest Au contact is 1.275 Å), so the geometry is extrapolation. The defect grew over training: at 200000 steps the model is wrong but smooth there (a flat profile at −30 eV over 0.6–1.1 Å, forces below 70 eV/Å); at 300000 the curvature ratio crosses 1 with the error unchanged; at 400000 the profile oscillates with ±170 eV/Å and a 0.1 Å period; from 450000 the error grows two orders of magnitude and fluctuates by a factor 40 between checkpoints. The curvature criterion led the error by about 150000 steps.

### 1.3 The production models on isolated pairs

Scanning isolated element pairs (energy zero at 7 Å, spacing 0.005 Å) on the released models gave the same picture at the top of the family. The Air/Pro release model (`Release/Air/N_3-l_3`, 469000 steps) fails the tail criterion on 8 of 8 surveyed pairs with spurious forces peaking at 4.9–5.4 Å whatever the elements (H–H 0.10, O–O 1.98, Au–Au 3.43, Fe–O 16.2 eV/Å) and carries a spurious extremum beyond the bond on every pair. The Max model (`N_8-l_6-c_96x2`, 8 blocks, lmax 6, 432000 steps) reads energy MAE 9.58 meV per atom, force MAE 42 meV/Å and force RMSE 359 meV/Å (a ratio of 8.5 against 1.25 for a near-Gaussian error distribution), holes three of eight pairs (Cu–Cu −22 eV at 0.25 contacts, Ni–Ni −38 eV at 0.64 Å, Au–Au −37 eV at 0.76 Å) and has the largest tail of the research on Fe–O (20.7 eV/Å with seven extrema). The user's own rule of thumb — the force RMSE moves first, then the force MAE and the energy, while the training loss stays normal — is this population of rare frames carrying the square.

### 1.4 The three faces, and the type axis

Every failure observed belongs to one of three faces, which the research eventually traced to one mechanism (§3): holes, i.e. the inward collapse of an isolated pair's wall below the data's shortest contacts (a rim hole is a resolved minimum near 0.35–0.6 covalent contacts behind a wall; an edge dive is an energy still falling at the scan's inner edge); tears and needles, i.e. training batches whose per-atom force error exceeds 1000 eV/Å (tears) or 100–1000 eV/Å (small needles) on compressed contacts of data frames, which come in storms and precede the holes; and tails and outer barriers, i.e. spurious forces of an isolated pair beyond its well, out to the cutoff.

A fourth observation was recorded as information, not as a criterion: the ten elements absent from OMat24 (He, Ne, Ar, Kr, Xe, Rn, Po, At, Fr, Ra; Ne, Ar, Kr, Xe have about 500 frames each, the others none) develop isolated-dimer wells of −10² to −10⁴ eV in every model, because their embedding rows never move while the shared network learns to read embeddings that have grown threefold. On 8 September the user decided that zero-data extrapolation is not an acceptance criterion ("类型轴不作为验收标准，可以"); the all-element homonuclear scan stays an instrument.

## 2. What it is not

Five hypotheses were excluded on Case A and B, each by a measurement. It is not a discontinuity: along a straight line in coordinate space through the failing frame the energy and force and their first and second differences are smooth — continuous, differentiable, and the wrong shape. It is not pair-distance extrapolation: the minimum distances of the failing frames (0.63–0.64 Å) lie above the training minimum, and the failing frame is not even the closest. It is not one module: hooking every leaf module, the largest ratio of late to early activation change is 1.08. It is not the edge-frame chart switch: the roll discontinuity of the local frame is real (up to 90° near the equator) but everything downstream is equivariant under rotation about the local z axis, so a sweep shows no jump. It is not neighbour-list truncation: both neighbour builders keep every pair inside the cutoff, and `sel` does not act on this path.

Four candidate mechanisms were also excluded at the weight level. Attention saturation: logits within ±1.9 and a largest attention weight of 0.03 per destination atom, identical on healthy and pathological frames. Radial-frequency drift: the trainable Bessel frequencies stay at nπ/r_c. Frobenius weight growth: the total norm fell during fine-tuning; what grows is the operator norm — the largest singular value of every weight matrix grows monotonically over the Pro training (1.3–2.1× per module family, radial embedding 9.6 → 20.0), which bounds the curvature of the composition by a product that grows two to three orders of magnitude, the order of the measured growth. Novel activation directions: pathological frames do not leave the data subspace (energy outside it 0.0096 against 0.0092 on healthy frames).

Two more were excluded later. The data are not the cause of the storms: silencing the loss of the contradictory frame family (frames with a pair below 0.5 covalent contacts and both label forces below 60 eV/Å, 0.055 % of the set) brought the storm 3000 calls earlier and the O₂ hole 10000 steps earlier, and the hydrogen testbed has no contradictory frames yet holes on every plain run. Depth alone is not the cause: a 4-block hydrogen model holes as the 2-block one does, at better accuracy. And the optimizer sets the rate of the drift, not its existence: the unwhitened momentum direction at a thirtieth of Muon's step still digs the hole, one checkpoint later and shallower.

## 3. The mechanism

### 3.1 Empty regions roughen with training

Neither defect sits at a training configuration: the hydrogen peak is a narrow, directional pocket inside a well-sampled thermodynamic state that isotropic sampling almost never enters, and the Pro defect lies outside the sampled region. Both were smooth early and became rough. Nothing pins the function where there are no data, so as the parameters move to fit the data elsewhere, the function there accumulates high-frequency structure. Training longer makes empty regions rougher, and a larger model has more of them. Two consequences shaped the research: a remedy must act in empty regions while the training loop visits only training frames, so every strategy reaches an empty region from a training frame; and labels cannot help there, so only label-free constraints remain — a physical one (a real surface has bounded curvature) and a historical one (the function the run started from).

### 3.2 The amplification chain

Distance enters the network multiplicatively, many times in depth. Measured on the plain OMat24 twin at a needle site, from the bond to 0.2 Å: radial MLP ×6, SO(2) message ×12 per block, residual into the read-out ×8.4, read-out feed-forward ×73 (the square, because the read-out's grid product is quadratic in an input read without a norm). The parameter sensitivity of a contact atom's force is ×29 at 20000 and ×65 at 38000 steps; along one 2000-step stretch the contact force runs 31 → 68 → 571 → 4694 → 1.4·10⁴ eV/Å while a bulk atom stays at 1.4–2.0. An exact curvature split (`curvature_split.py`, verified against autograd to 8·10⁻¹³) placed the amplifier in the trunk, not the head: the head's curvature per unit input is 0.0006–0.075 eV per squared descriptor unit while the descriptor's own derivative reaches 309–473 per Å on the metal wells; no normalization factor exceeds 1.95× its far-field value. The torn state of a training batch is three-body: at the tear weights an isolated Li–H pair at 0.687 Å reads 82 eV/Å and any third atom inside the cutoff raises it to 10²–10⁴ eV/Å, so the isolated-pair survey and the averaged weights are blind to it.

### 3.3 One cause: a parameter no datum constrains is moved as fast as the constrained ones

The three faces are one mechanism. The innermost radial channels (the Gaussian centres at 0.0, 0.2 and 0.4 Å, or the Bessel functions' small-r behaviour) reach 10⁻⁵–10⁻² of their peak at the data's contacts, so the rows of the first radial layer that read them receive 10⁻¹⁵–10⁻²⁰ of the largest row's gradient on ordinary frames and an O(1) gradient on a rare compressed one, while the optimizer's orthogonalized momentum moves them at full rate anyway; zeroing those rows on trained checkpoints removes the holes with tails and bulk energies unchanged. The embedding row of an absent element never moves while the shared network's gains grow, so the response to it grows faster than linearly. A frame tear is the visit of a geometry that activates a direction of parameter space that no datum constrains: the 29394 tear of one run reproduces in float64 from the saved live weights, the weight displacement from the last checkpoint is ordinary drift, and along the straight line to the tear weights the frame error rises smoothly through 1.9 → 64 → 1.5·10⁴ → 3.5·10⁶ eV/Å — a ridge in the low-dimensional subspace the optimizer travels. This is what the adopted structural changes act on: remove the unconstrained parameter (freeze the basis) or move it with a bounded, per-row step (route the radial-reading layers to AdamW). Validation accuracy and the extrapolation criteria decouple: a run's energy MAE falls monotonically from 16.6 to 15.5 meV per atom while its holed pairs grow from 2 to 19 and its first tears appear.

### 3.4 The isolated-atom limit, the hump and the missing wells

The atomic energy is E_i = b(t_i) + f(x_i): b the per-element least-squares bias fitted on the training energies, f the fitting network on the descriptor. An atom without neighbours has the isolated descriptor x_iso(t), which no training frame contains, so f(x_iso, t) is an untrained constant and b(t) + f(x_iso, t) is an arbitrary dissociation limit. On the E118 checkpoint the bias sits 3.5 eV below the functional's isolated-atom energy E0 averaged over 89 elements, and f(x_iso) recovers only part of it, leaving the limit 0.20 eV (H) and 1.15 eV (Au) per atom too low. The consequences are the missing Au₂ well (−0.03 eV against −1.6 to −2.3 eV in the reference models), the hump beyond every molecular pair's well (+0.26 to +0.44 eV on H₂ in every DeePMD model, zero in MACE), and the descent to the too-low limit that the surveys read as a force at 3–6 Å. MACE writes E_i = E0(z_i) + Σ readouts with a per-element table, bias-free read-outs and features that vanish without neighbours, so an isolated atom returns E0 exactly; UMA references every training energy to per-element isolated-atom energies and trains on single-atom frames. Pinning the limit in the model (E_i = E0(t_i) + f(x_i) − f(x_iso, t_i), §5.5) removes the hump and restores the wells; supplying it as data (isolated-atom frames in the training set) is the route the user prefers.

### 3.5 Envelope compensation, and the tail of an ionic pair against a 6 Å cutoff

With the single C³ envelope of exponent 5 (0.43 at 4 Å, 0.25 at 4.5 Å, 0.07 at 5 Å) the network learns raw edge energies large enough to reproduce the crystals' second-neighbour interactions through the envelope's tail, and an isolated pair then shows that raw energy until the envelope collapses. Measured on the 16-function ladder at 8000 steps: without a fade the isolated K–F pair carries E(4.5 Å) − E(7 Å) = −2.05 eV (a unit-charge pair carries −2.3 eV) and releases it inside the envelope's last ångström, with forces −2.8, −2.0, −0.09 and 0 eV/Å at 4.5, 5.0, 5.5 and 6.0 Å; Na–Cl −1.8/−2.0/−0.09, Ca–O −1.6/−0.5/−0.04, the metals Au–Au, Al–Al, Ag–Ag −0.8 to −1.0 eV/Å at 4.5 Å. The reference models release the same energy gradually: MACE-OMAT-0 holds K–F at −0.69 eV at 4.5 Å with −0.81/−0.60/−0.49/−0.44 eV/Å at 4.0/4.5/5.0/5.5 Å, UMA-s-1p2 −0.57 eV with −0.80/−0.56/−0.37/−0.36. The DPA4 no-fade tails are therefore three to five times the reference models' at the same separations, and they grow with training. A pair fade cuts the compensation at 4.5 Å and moves the release to 3.5–4.5 Å, which is where the fade's damage sits; the seed gate halves the tail without holding it; the reference term cannot see it, because the hand-over freezes a reference that already carries the failing tails and its 1 eV/Å tolerance is above the whole tail scale. The ionic tail runs physically to the ion–neutral crossing near 15 Å, so no cutoff model can be right there; what can be asked is that the release be smooth and no stronger than the Coulomb force, which the reference models nearly achieve.

### 3.6 The fine-tune scenario

A fine-tune differs from pretraining in two ways: the reference is a trained model rather than a random initialization, so the reference term pins the anchors to a physical function from the first step and no hand-over exists; and the architecture is inherited from the base checkpoint. Every geometric anchor generator — Gaussian shell, collision, dilation, deletion, and their composition — starts from a data frame and never produces an isolated pair at a compressed or stretched separation, so on the 64-atom hydrogen cells they delay and do not prevent the collapse of the isolated H₂ (§6.4); an anchor that constructs that geometry and holds it to the frozen reference's own curve keeps the pair through 120000 steps.

## 4. Criteria and instruments

The isolated-pair survey (`dimer_survey.py`): 24 element pairs (H–H, O–O, N–N, C–C, Li–H, Li–O, Na–Cl, K–F, Mg–O, Ca–O, Ti–O, Fe–O, Cr–Cr, Mo–Mo, Ni–Ni, Cu–Cu, Ag–Ag, Au–Au, Al–Al, Si–O, Zn–S, Ga–As, Pb–H, O–H) in float64 at a maximum spacing of 0.005 Å with the energy zero at 7 Å, on every 2000-step checkpoint (averaged weights), listing every local extremum beyond 1.5 Å with a 1 meV topographic prominence. Readings per pair: the principal well (position, depth), holes inside 0.6 covalent contacts (rim hole or edge dive), the hump beyond the well, additional extrema, the tail. The all-element homonuclear scan (94 pairs at 0.01 Å) and the compressed-trimer probe (`diagnose/trimer_probe.py`: a pair at 0.45 contacts with one third atom 2 Å away, beside the pair alone) join every evaluation; the user's scanner (`/nas/outisli/Research/aisi_workspace/scripts/dimer_scan.py`, isolated pair in a 30 Å box) produces the two figures in the repository root.

Contact exclusions: a separation below 1 Å or a covalent-contact ratio below 0.6 makes a short-range observation informational; criteria are judged outside both. On 6 September the user placed the region below about 0.35 contacts inside the future ZBL range: a softened surface there is an accepted trade-off of a model without ZBL and is not counted as a failure; a hole is.

A1, the tail. Original form: the largest absolute spurious force in 4–7 Å must not exceed 0.05 eV/Å. This form is unphysical for ionic pairs (the Coulomb force of a unit-charge pair at 4.5 Å is 0.71 eV/Å; MACE-OMAT-0 fails it on 22 of 24 pairs) and can be met only by a model that has already flattened the curve inside 4 Å. Revised form (9 September, at the user's request to move the edge to 4.5 Å): from 4.5 Å to the cutoff the energy must approach the limit monotonically (no extremum above 5 meV) and the force must nowhere exceed 14.4/r² eV/Å. Under it MACE fails 3 pairs (Cr–Cr and Mo–Mo bumps of 6–21 meV; K–F's Coulomb tail slightly above the bound near 5.5 Å) and UMA 14. Its blind spot is the window below 4.5 Å, where the fade puts its damage; the whole-range form proposed at closure (§6.6) judges monotonicity from the principal well outward and the Coulomb bound from max(1.5 covalent contacts, 3 Å) to 7 Å.

A2, no hole: the lowest energy inside 0.6 contacts must not lie more than 0.05 eV below the lowest energy outside. A3, the well: H₂ at 0.74 ± 0.03 Å and −4.5 ± 0.5 eV; other pairs within 20 % of the depth and 0.05 Å of the position of a healthy reading. A4, one minimum beyond the wall. Tears and needles: per-step largest per-atom force error from the batch log (`--log-batch-error`), tears above 1000 eV/Å and small needles at 100–1000 eV/Å, with the contact geometry of every large-error atom; a tear is eligible when its damaged atoms lie outside both exclusions even if its trigger sits inside; the whole event history to the current step is the statistic, compared at matched steps; same-seed scatter is about 25 needle batches over 10000–40000 steps and about 30 % between draws. The compressed-probe gain (`gain_probe.py`) and the inner band of the isolated dimer (sign changes of the force inside 0.62 Å) are leading indicators; the live weights lead the averaged weights by thousands of steps.

Accuracy: the complete `sub001val.lmdb` set (21742 frames, 416677 atoms), energy MAE per atom, force-vector MAE and RMSE, every frame reported; budget 2 % relative to the matched control on the same seed; same-seed scatter of the mean metrics about 0.5 %; the force RMSE is frame-dominated. The hydrogen testbed uses all 3675 validation frames (`h_accuracy.py`). Earlier criteria (B5 one pair one force, C9 the thirteen hardest contacts, the curvature ratio and ceiling of §5.1) remain as diagnostics.

Evaluation rules that were learned the hard way: place a candidate beside its baseline at the same step before calling it a failure; a quiet tail with no well is not a pass; no cell is accepted on a same-weight intervention alone; the survey must run at every checkpoint, since holes appear and vanish within one 2000-step interval; an apparent coarse-grid pass needs refinement; out-of-memory fallbacks record NaN, so a validation on a card shared with another job is not a reading.

## 5. Remedies tried, by family, and what each showed

### 5.1 The curvature criterion and penalty (28 August – 2 September)

A label-free criterion: the curvature of the energy along the force direction, c = û^T H û with û = F/|F|, in eV/Å². Healthy configurations reproduce the physical stiffness (40 and 35 eV/Å² on control hydrogen frames against the H₂ stretch constant of 36); pathological ones exceed it 700–1900× with a clear gap, and the signal appears six MD steps before the failure and nowhere earlier. A ceiling κ_i = 300 + min(|F_i|, 100)/0.1 eV/Å² (an exponential wall with decay length 0.1 Å) separates every healthy set measured (largest ratio 0.83 on 21742 OMat24 frames) from every pathological frame. The force-probe estimator g = [F(x) − F(x + εû)]/ε survives training precision; the energy probe does not. The penalty λ Σ [max(0, log |g_i|/κ_i)]² is zero below the ceiling. As a repair it works: on the seven pathological hydrogen frames the ratio falls below 1 within 10 steps, the largest force from 318 to 5.2 eV/Å, the hill climb from 8044 to 13, with healthy frames moved by 0.2 meV per atom; on the Pro checkpoint the frame-98341 error falls from 119399 to 511 eV/Å with 32 synthetic Au contacts transferring to the held-out frame. As prevention it fails: anchors at the training frames or 0.03 Å from them never open the hinge (largest ratio 0.29 on the training data); the hydrogen continuation shows that amplitude, not penalty weight, sets the effect and that the defect wanders within a band set by how often generators land on it; sticky anchors with curvature ascent (strategy 10) remove the residual only when started while the defect is large — a tracker, not a finder; the operator-norm cap costs accuracy (3.4 against 1.3 meV per atom) and prevents nothing. Both were recorded as fallbacks (§5.8).

### 5.2 The reference-consistency term (anchors, "safeguard")

Principle: labels constrain the potential at sampled configurations, and updates change it elsewhere; a frozen copy of a reference function resists that change on geometries generated from training frames. Every `freq` = 10 steps the fetched batch is transformed (one generator drawn per frame: Gaussian shell 0.1–0.2 Å, collision against the force by up to 0.3 Å, isotropic dilation in [1, 2], deletion of up to 90 % of atoms), the reference predicts forces on the transformed frames without parameter gradients, and the hinge max(0, |F_model − F_ref| − 1 − 0.1|F_ref|)² averaged over real atoms with |F_ref| ≤ 100 eV/Å is back-propagated before the batch forward so that one optimizer step applies the sum; a non-finite anchor gradient is discarded. Cost about 13 % of training time by operation count and no measurable accuracy cost on the bulk data (within 1 % through 100000 steps on the production pair). The implementation in the package (`deepmd/dpmodel/utils/safeguard.py`, `deepmd/pt_expt/train/safeguard.py`, both trainers, tests including two-rank DDP) is formal code; it stays off the `outisli` branch and lives on `self/spike`.

What the term establishes. In the fine-tune scenario with a pretrained reference it keeps the dimer physical where the plain fine-tune collapses (hydrogen, production rate: plain −2.0·10⁹ eV at 120000 steps, with the term −2.43 eV at 0.91 Å at equal accuracy), it learns a wall the reference lacks (the ZBL-relabelled hydrogen data), and its merged form costs nothing (55.4 against 55.4 meV/Å). In pretraining from a random initialization the reference has no physics: dense anchors next to the data cost about 10 % of force accuracy whatever the tolerance, sparse anchors (dilation ≥ 1.3, deletion ≥ 50 %) match the plain run but flatten the isolated wells to half depth and, on the hardest contacts, teach an environment-dependent pair force that ends in a spike. Hence the hand-over: the random reference with sparse anchors for the first 10000 steps, then one hand-over to a frozen copy of the model itself, after which the collision generator (step 0.6 Å, reaching below the data's shortest contacts) and, optionally, the dense set join. With the hand-over the production twin (`neo_rf10k1D`) holds every probed contact through 660000 steps where the plain run tears at 38000 and 110000, at bulk accuracy within 1 %; its O₂ pair holes at 680000 after 660000 clean readings.

What the term cannot do, each shown by a run. A continuously refreshed reference legitimizes whatever the unreached region has drifted to (hydrogen: −49/−916 eV holes for refresh 10000, −135/−7.5 for refresh 2000, against −0.27 eV fixed). A closed tube on far anchors is harmful (holes sooner); a weak closed tube delays and then deepens. Anchors teach the reference's function wherever they are placed: a pair squeeze to 0.30–0.55 Å removes the isolated O₂ wall within 10000 steps because the reference is untrained there. The hand-over reference carries defects of its own (an extra Cu–Cu well; the outer barriers), which the term then preserves. The hardest thirteen contacts of the validation set stay at 1.0–1.6 eV/Å against the plain run's 0.35–0.45 because the shell anchors hold the rarest contacts inside the tube of a reference that is wrong there by up to 20 eV/Å; releasing those atoms (label-widened tube) lowers this by 20–50 % at the price of a swinging two-atom limit and needles. The term's tube (1 eV/Å + 10 %) is far above the tail scale, so it cannot hold the tails, and it can freeze a hole already present at the hand-over. The two-atom limit is held on the stiff side by the deletion anchors and swings soft when they are released; no anchored twin holds the isolated pair to its frame value within 20 % over a whole run. Coverage: periodic deletion retains images (minimum coordination 37 after deletion on OMat24 cells), so "sparse" anchors do not reach the isolated-pair limit; finite-cluster sources reach coordination-one atoms but those atoms sit inside the tolerance and contribute no penalty (E77–E83). Every variation of the placement — composed generators, undilated composition, descent anchors, worst-of-K, reference ensembles, second hand-overs, pair terms, contact-scoped tubes, hard-contact replay, stratified replay, type substitution — is on the ledger (§8) with its reading; the settled placement is one generator per frame with the hand-over and the 0.6 Å collision step, and the dense set optional (§6.2).

### 5.3 Structural bounds on the amplification

Normalizations and knees. A soft RMS norm with eps 1 at every SO(2) unit's output (E28) or at the read-out's input (E30) attenuates the tears from 10⁵–10⁸ to about 10² eV/Å but does not prevent them; both together (E31) pass every criterion at 40000 steps on three draws and storm on both long draws (78600 and 82334 steps, with the isolated O₂ holed at 80000 and 160000). The user's observation that with eps 1 these layers are learnable constant scalings on the data, not normalizations, was confirmed; they were renamed bounds. A knee at the data's message scale (m₀ = 0.3, E37) passes at 40000 and storms at 77000 and 101400 on both draws; the read-out knee (E48) lets the tail grow; a post-add stream norm (E45) storms at 45359; knees on the per-node pre-norms (E60) or removing the feed-forward pre-norm (E61) hole eight pairs at the hand-over or tear at step 9381 — the hard per-node pre-norms are what protects the sparse limit early. On the anchors, the two eps-1 bounds (E43) were the main line of 5–6 September: the cleanest needle window of the ledger and every criterion at 40000, then draw-to-draw variance on the isolated metal pairs (one draw clean through 130000, the repeat with five wells of −8 to −33 eV at 1.4–1.8 Å at 100000).

Attention and aggregation. Depth-attention residual forms (E13–E15, eleven arms) all failed by 20000–30000 on hydrogen; bounding the aggregation logits by 8·tanh(L/8) (E16) is a continuity fix that changes nothing about the collapse; envelope-weighted aggregation without the learned denominator (E72) raises the H₂ tail to 25 eV/Å and costs 5.7 % force RMSE; the envelope-mass attention normalization (E87) costs 2.3 % and prevents nothing; the output (message) envelope (E73) reduces a Gaussian-basis tail from 14.1 to 0.60 eV/Å on the same weights but fails the hydrogen screen (+10.9 % energy) and the OMat24 RMSE (+4.9 %).

Heads and inputs. A tanh fitting head bounds the head's output but not the coordinate force (161649 → 1820 eV/Å on the captured frame, while a healthy checkpoint worsens); the four fitting architectures (E84: GLU or MLP × SiLU or tanh) all fail A1 on 24 of 24 pairs at 40000; the head without its gate (E66) was closed by the curvature split, which puts the amplifier in the trunk; a fitting-input RMS normalization (E74) removes the frame tear on the same weights but costs 58.9 % energy MAE on hydrogen and loses the well; the scalar read-out (E62) and the read-out without its GLU (E63) were running when the norm line was superseded. A C³ floor on the pair distance seen by the descriptor (E49) stormed at 29600 — earlier than any twin — because its transition zone became a needle band. Shortening the cutoff to 4 Å (E76) removes the outer force by construction and keeps inner false barriers at a large accuracy cost (39 against 16 meV per atom).

Radial basis and its normalization. A Bessel basis normalized to a fixed norm (E38, E41) tears at 29413 and 29612; 64 Gaussians (E40) tear at 35031 with 27 needles before step 3300 (encoding speed 7.4 against 1.8 for 16 Gaussians); 16 trainable Gaussians (E39) alone hole hydrogen at 40000 and creep on the O₂ tail. What made the Gaussian basis useful — fixing its centres, confining or gating its support, and the single envelope — is the adopted part and is documented with the code; on the Bessel basis the single envelope and the seed gate move nothing (E93: 19–24 failing tails in every arm of the 2×2).

### 5.4 Gates and the pair fade (not adopted)

Three gates multiply parts of the edge input by the other-neighbour fraction g_ij = (S_i − w_ij)/S_i (with w_ij the edge's envelope mass and S_i the destination's total), which is zero on an isolated pair and close to one inside a crystal, or by a radial signal. The edge-type gate (E88) replaces type_src + type_dst by g_ij(type_i + type_j), so that the type channel — which is added to every degree of the radial features with no radial or envelope factor and therefore feeds the attention scores even where the radial features vanish — is silent on an isolated pair; standalone it costs 4 % RMSE and fails 24 tails. The outer radial gate (E95) multiplies the radial channels centred beyond 3 Å by g_ij: 0 of 24 failing tails at every checkpoint on two seeds, at −6.9 % energy MAE against the confined-basis base, but it compresses the descent to the untrained dissociation limit into 0.4 Å at 3.0–3.4 Å (a step of 1.7–1.9 eV/Å on H₂ and Au₂, up to 9.4 eV/Å on Fe–O; 20–22 of 24 pairs above 1 eV/Å in 3–4 Å), which is why it was dropped once the limit was understood. The radial seed gate (E89) evaluates the environment seed's first layer as SiLU(radial)·sigmoid(types) instead of SiLU(radial + types), so that the seed vanishes when its radial projection does; it costs nothing measurable, its apparent protection against collapse did not replicate (E90), and on its own it does not hold the tails without a fade (the 16-function ladder: 19–20 failing pairs on one seed and 2–5 on the other at 12000–16000, K–F up to 6.1 eV/Å; §6.5).

The pair fade (E132 onward) multiplies every radial basis value and the type feature of an edge by f_ij = g_ij + (1 − g_ij)s(r_ij), with s a quintic smoothstep from one at 2.5 Å to zero at 4.5 Å: an isolated pair presents no input beyond 4.5 Å and reaches the limit continuously. It meets the revised A1 exactly (tail force zero from 4.5 Å at every survey of E147) and costs nothing on the least-squares limit (E132's accuracy 6 % better on RMSE than E118). Its defect is where it puts the release: the isolated pair's energy reaches the limit at 4.0–4.5 Å with a force bump at the fade's edge (H–H a −0.5 eV/Å dip at 4.0 Å below a 0.17 eV secondary well at 3.9 Å; Au–Au a −2 eV/Å dip at 3.8 Å; on the ionic pairs the release of −1 to −3 eV squeezed into 3.5–4.5 Å with forces 1.2–1.7 times the unit-charge bound), while MACE's and UMA's curves are still rising at 6 Å. Without the seed gate, the fade's edge carries the seed network's type-only output out to the cutoff, which is learned into the ionic attraction as training proceeds (E142: K–F tail 1.29 eV/Å at 4.78 Å by 80000 steps, growing); with the gate (E147) the tail is exactly zero. The user's judgement of 11 September: the fade cannot be used.

### 5.5 The isolated-atom limit: pinned in the model, or supplied as data

Pinned (E133–E152, `isolated_reference.py`): the bias of every tabulated element is overwritten with E0 after the from-scratch statistic and the fitting output is measured from its own isolated value, E_i = E0(t_i) + f(x_i) − f(x_iso, t_i), with f(x_iso, t) evaluated in the same forward from one atom of every type with an empty edge list (two auxiliary atoms joined by one edge keep the kernels valid). The subtraction is constant in the coordinates (forces and virials unchanged) and differentiable in the parameters; at freeze time it folds into the bias. Cost 0.7 % per step compiled. The table is UMA's omat isolated-atom energies (89 elements; MACE-OMAT-0's table agrees to 1 meV on main-group elements and differs by 0.1–3.5 eV on 62 open-shell elements — a per-element constant). Effect: the hump vanishes on all 24 pairs, the wells return (Au₂ −2.0 to −2.3 eV at 2.5 Å against −0.03), the K–F tail is exactly zero with the fade. Price: an energy cost of 4–10 % at 40000 steps that closes by about a fifth per 20000 steps to +1.0/+1.8 % (E142) and +1.2/+2.0 % (E147) against E118 at 100000, or +2.5–3.0 % against the same routing on the least-squares limit (E137); the force RMSE is 12 % lower. The cost is not concentrated on rare elements (the ratio to E118 is 1.04–1.10 in every frequency group); it is a convergence cost, because every per-type constant direction of the network cancels in f(x_i) − f(x_iso) and each element's cohesive energy must be learned through the shared environment-dependent directions. Under the pinned limit a fitting network on the AdamW path dives at extreme short range (17–23 dived pairs before the hand-over on every such arm; E139 with the fitting network on Muon never exceeds 2), and the reference term stays necessary (E143 without it collapses on one seed at 38000).

As data (E158 withdrawn, E159): the user's proposal is to put isolated atoms, and later dimers, into the dataset as ordinary frames. E158 appended one isolated-atom frame per batch inside the trainer and was rejected as a change of the training pipeline ("在数据集中加入以后其他就自然交给dataloader来了"). E159 appended 186900 frames (2100 copies of each of the 89 tabulated elements, one atom in a 20 Å cube, energy E0, zero force and virial) to a copy of the OMat24 training LMDB (`/nas/outisli/Datasets/LMDB/OMat24_iso/trn.lmdb`, `append_isolated_atoms.py`), 0.19 % of the frames, about one per batch under the `mix:10000` rule; it ran at the ladder's pace and was stopped with the research at 1000 steps. Dimers as data need labels the research does not have: UMA computed its diatomic curves with DFT and found (appendix G of arXiv 2506.23971) that its OMat curves were qualitatively smooth without them and that the diatomic data were added for accuracy (OMat diatomic force MAE 279.6 → 52.5 meV/Å).

### 5.6 Anchor placement and data-side terms

Finite clusters as anchor sources (E77, E79, E81, E83): reach coordination-one atoms that periodic deletion misses, but those atoms sit inside the 1 eV/Å tolerance and contribute 0.002–0.2 % of the squared excess; earlier cluster coverage does not prevent the false barriers. Replay of complete OMat24 frames during a hydrogen fine-tune (E80) suppresses catastrophic drift but does not preserve source accuracy. Hard-contact replay (E6) costs 39–44 % accuracy at any threshold and 1–6 % in its importance-weighted forms; objective-preserving stratified replay (E23, E94) buys nothing at 40000. Type substitution (E99) — a fifth of the atoms in a quarter of the anchor frames re-typed at random — is the only arm that held the type axis, at +13–95 % small needles. Pair terms holding the closest pair alone to the label's in-frame radial force (E2, E20) were closed in every form: the in-frame radial force is a generalized force, not a pair interaction. The monotone-repulsion inequality on compressed pairs (E11) is never violated and prevents nothing; the repulsion inequality on isolated pairs (E58) lies inside the ZBL range. Isolated-pair anchors in the fine-tune (E152, `pair_anchors.py`): half the anchor frames replaced by an isolated pair of the frame's own atoms at a log-uniform separation over 0.4–6 Å, held to the frozen pretrained reference's own curve — the first arm on which the fine-tuned model's isolated H₂ stays the pretrained model's (−5.1/−4.3 eV at 0.75 Å through 120000 steps at the lowest validation error of any arm).

The tail term (E67): 24 isolated vacuum pairs per anchor event at 4–6 Å whose radial force is driven to zero by 10·f_r². On hydrogen it met the original A1 by a factor 40 (tail 0.0013 eV/Å) at no accuracy cost and without displacing the error into 3–4 Å (the detached response fell from 0.0487 to 0.0023). The user set it aside on 6 September as a patch on the symptom, because the freedom that produces the symptom — a response the data never constrain between 4 Å and the cutoff — is left in place. At closure it is the form that would act where the envelope compensation happens if its target were the unit-charge Coulomb bound rather than zero.

### 5.7 The optimizer

The optimizer's orthogonalized momentum is the rate-setting factor (§2): ridge-regularized orthogonalization at four strengths and the unwhitened control all collapse the hydrogen dimer, later and shallower. Learning rate, gradient clipping, weight decay, dropout and schedule were ruled out as factors by the user. The routing of chosen tensors to the AdamW path was admitted as an experimental factor on 8 September and became the adopted form documented with the code.

### 5.8 Fallbacks recorded, not pursued

A geometric trust mask excluding anchor atoms inside the repulsive wall (treats where the contradiction shows, not the contradiction); crash-and-distil (requires the failure first); dynamics-generated anchors (reach what the dynamics reach, at the cost of the dynamics); the operator-norm cap (bounds amplitude, prevents nothing, costs force accuracy); the curvature hinge with the sticky search (the repair tool of choice, not prevention); a slowly moving reference (bounds only the drift rate); ZBL and pair-scaled zone bridging (E27, E29, withdrawn on the no-ZBL rule; the pair-zone patch is kept for the later ZBL phase); learned repulsion channels with a pinned sign (E35, E36, symptom-directed); DeNS direct-force pretraining then conservative fine-tuning (E21, catastrophic on both testbeds).

## 6. The converged recipe and its evidence

### 6.1 The recipe of record (E147) and its Occam form (E150)

E147 (`neo_e0_route_fade_seedgate`, two seeds, 100000 steps, 10 September): the pinned isolated-atom limit, the fixed 31-centre Gaussian basis with the single envelope and the radial-only AdamW routing (the adopted structural part), the reference term with the hand-over at 10000 and both after-hand-over generators (dense set and collision step 0.6 Å), the pair fade 2.5–4.5 Å and the seed radial gate. Readings at 100000: 0 revised-A1 failures at every one of 50 surveys, tail force zero beyond 4.5 Å on every pair, 0 tears, needles 827/793 (4 % fewer than E118), no flagged pair, 6–7 pairs with extra extrema, H₂ −4.45/−4.50 eV at 0.74 Å, Au₂ −2.06/−2.10 eV at 2.5 Å; validations 16.73/17.01 → 15.74/15.90 → 15.18/15.27 → 14.82/14.84 meV per atom at 40000–100000 (+1.2/+2.0 % against E118's 14.64/14.55), force RMSE 0.213/0.213 (12 % lower). E150 (`neo_e0_final_collision`) drops the dense set: 14.82/14.93 at 100000, 0 revised-A1 failures at every survey, no edge dive on either seed, needles 836/807. E148, which drops the collision step instead, produced three transient edge dives (Au₂ at 56000, Ga–As at 60000, O–O at 88000) and is out.

### 6.2 The ablations under the pinned limit (E143–E146)

Without the reference term (E143) one seed collapses from 24000 (intermittent edge dives, 12 of 24 pairs flagged at 38000 with Au₂ −7.15 eV at 0.68 Å) although its early readings were the cleanest of the family. Without the hand-over (E144: a random reference for the whole run) is the only degrading arm — the random reference pins compressed pairs to the random function the hand-over exists to release (16 flagged pairs at 30000 on one seed, three short-contact tears). Without the dense set (E145) is slightly worse on needles than E142 and acceptable; without the collision step (E146) a rim hole appears at 100000 (Mg–O −3.6 eV at 1.09 Å behind a 25 eV wall) — the collision step guards the extreme short range. The earlier three-family ablations at 40000 (E119, E121, E125 on E118/E120/E124) gave the same verdicts on the shared ingredients: the reference term, its hand-over, the seed gate, the type gate and the outer gate necessary for the criteria of that time; the dense set and the collision step undecidable at 40000.

### 6.3 Accuracy against the references and the field

At 100000 steps: E118 (the three-gate candidate on the least-squares limit) 14.64/14.55 meV per atom; E137 (radial-only routing, least-squares limit) 14.43/14.38 with RMSE 0.229/0.212; E147 14.82/14.84; E150 14.82/14.93. On a 3000-frame sample of the OMat24 validation subset MACE-OMAT-0 medium reads 16.87 meV per atom and RMSE 0.139, UMA-s-1p2 9.90 and 0.097. On the programme's instruments MACE holes no pair, has wells on all 24, carries 5 extra extrema and fails the original A1 on 22 pairs and the revised one on 3; UMA holes one pair (Li–O), carries 15 extra extrema and fails the revised A1 on 14; E118 fails neither A1 form but has 16 extra extrema, no Au₂ well and a limit 0.2–1.2 eV per atom below E0. Every DeePMD model of the family is within the reference models' own spread at the well (H₂ 0.0–0.5 eV from MACE) and 0.2–2.6 eV too deep at 7 Å.

### 6.4 The fine-tune scenario (E126–E129, E149–E152)

Hydrogen fine-tunes of the E95 base (whose H–H had collapsed on one seed) collapse on every arm at the accelerated rate (plain −4406 eV at 10000 steps; the package safeguard −46552; safeguard + collision 0.6 Å −100701; safeguard + routing −25.5 at 10000 and −4.4·10⁶ at 30000) and at the production rate (all arms −10⁴ eV by 40000–120000). Fine-tunes of the E147 checkpoints at 40000 (E149) fine-tune orders of magnitude more safely and still lose the pair: plain −595 eV at 100000 steps, the package safeguard −185 and −24 eV, safeguard + routing −8.2 and −29.9 eV, with dense-region readings normal throughout and the limit at twice the pinned hydrogen energy. Composed generators (E151) delay by about 20000 steps. Isolated-pair anchors (E152) keep the pair: −5.16/−4.38 eV at 0.75–0.77 Å at 100000 and −5.12/−4.32 at 120000, inner band 68–108 eV/Å with no sign change, validation force MAE 55–56 meV/Å (the lowest of any arm). The fine-tune scenario therefore closes by the same mechanism as pretraining: hold the isolated pair where it is, with an anchor that contains it.

### 6.5 The 16-function ladder (E153–E157) and the tail reading of 11 September

At the user's request every arm was rerun at the package's 16 fixed Gaussians (centres 0–6 Å, width 0.4 Å) on E147's basis, two seeds each, stopped by the research's closure at 16000–18000 steps. Revised-A1 failures of 24 pairs at 6000/8000/10000/12000/14000 (seed 42 / seed 43): E153 (E147 itself) 0/0 at every survey; E154 (no fade, seed gate kept) 4/2 → 11/3 → 18/2 → 20/2 → 19/4; E155 (no fade, no gate) 4/3 → 13/7 → 16/12 → 18/14 → 16/18; E156 (pinned limit and reference term with hand-over only) 3/4 → 7/11 → 15/16 → 16/18 → 15/19; E157 (least-squares limit) 0/3 → 4/4 → 7/7 → 6/8 → 4/7 with 21–23 pairs carrying spurious extrema beyond 1.5 Å at every step. Every no-fade arm fails the revised tail criterion on most pairs from the hand-over on, as the 31-function no-fade arms E136 and E139 did, and the hand-over freezes a reference that already carries the failing tails. Pair scans of the failing pairs (§3.5) showed what the tails are made of: the physical ionic energy held up to 4.5 Å and released inside the envelope's last ångström, three to five times the reference models' forces at the same separations. Needle batches over 2000–7400 steps: 133–153 on the 16-function arms against 169–208 on the 31-function E147/E150 runs.

### 6.6 The criterion question at closure

Scoring every scanned model on a whole-range rule — monotone approach to the limit from the principal well outward (no extremum above 5 meV) and |F| ≤ 1.1 × 14.4/r² from max(1.5 covalent contacts, 3 Å) to 7 Å — on ten pairs (K–F, Na–Cl, Ca–O, Li–O, Mg–O, Au–Au, Al–Al, Ag–Ag, Mo–Mo, H–H): E153 (fade + gate, 6000) exceeds the bound on 5 of 10 at 3.2–3.9 Å (K–F 1.3×, Ca–O 1.7×, Li–O, Mg–O, Al–Al 1.2–1.3×), with no secondary extrema; E155 (no fade, 8000) on 8 of 10 at 4.4–4.8 Å (K–F 6.2×, Na–Cl 4.4×); E156 on 6 of 10 (5.1×, 3.5×) with 2 extrema; E157 on none but with secondary extrema on 6 of 10; MACE-OMAT-0 on 1 (K–F 1.2× at 5.8 Å, its own cutoff) with 1 extremum; UMA-s-1p2 on 2 (K–F 1.1×, Al–Al 1.8× at 5.8 Å) with 4 small extrema. E147/E150 at 100000 fail it on Au–Au (2× at 3.8 Å) and H–H (a secondary well at 3.9 Å). This rule ranks the models as the user's reading of the figures does; whether to adopt it, with what tolerance, is the user's decision.

## 7. Conclusions and open decisions

The defects are smooth features of the learned energy in regions the data do not cover, produced by parameters that no datum constrains and that the optimizer moves as fast as the constrained ones, and amplified by the trunk's radial chain. Validation averages and short dynamics do not see them; the isolated-pair survey, the batch-error log and the compressed-probe gain do. The structural remedy — remove or bound the unconstrained parameters of the radial machinery — was adopted. The reference-consistency term is a sound method for the fine-tune scenario with a pretrained reference and for holding contacts during pretraining after a hand-over, with a settled ceiling: it holds values, it does not learn them, and it cannot reach the isolated pair unless an anchor contains one. The tails of an isolated ionic pair are a property of any finite-cutoff model and can be made smooth and bounded but not correct; the fade meets the tail criterion by moving the artefact into the 3–4.5 Å region and was rejected on that reading. The dissociation limit is the one quantity the data never teach and the reference models fix by construction; pinning it costs 1–2 % in energy that closes with training, supplying it as data is the route the user prefers and E159 was built for it.

What lives where: the `outisli` branch carries the fixed basis types, the single envelope, the routing and the presets. The branch `self/spike` carries, as formal code, the reference term with its hand-over schedule, the pair fade, the seed radial gate and the isolated-atom energy reference. The research patches in `debug/curvature_reg/` remain the record of every experiment.

Open decisions: the form and tolerance of the tail criterion (§6.6); the source of labels for dimer frames if the data route is pursued; the outer barrier beyond the molecular pairs' wells (present in every DeePMD model at the hand-over and preserved by the reference term; needs labelled low-coordination data or a criterion decision); whether a ZBL phase follows, in which the pair-zone bridging patch (E29) is the starting point.

| E143–E146                                                                                             | ablation of E142                                                                                  | reference term and hand-over necessary; dense set droppable; collision step guards the short range             |
| E147 / E150                                                                                           | E142 + seed gate / without the dense set                                                          | recipe of record (10 September); Occam form                                                                    |
| E148                                                                                                  | E147 without the collision step                                                                   | out on three transient edge dives                                                                              |
| E149, E151, E152 fine-tunes of E147                                                                   | package safeguard, composition, isolated-pair anchors                                             | only the pair anchors keep the dimer                                                                           |
| E153–E157                                                                                             | 16-function ladder around E147                                                                    | fade arms 0 failures; every no-fade arm fails the revised A1 from the hand-over                                |
| E158 / E159                                                                                           | isolated atoms per batch (withdrawn) / in the dataset                                             | E159 built and started; stopped with the research                                                              |

## 9. Record — the phenomenon in detail

The measurements behind §1, condensed from the original log; every number is the original's.

#### Phenomenon

**General (§1).** Where data exist the function is smooth; in uncovered regions it develops, as training goes on, features of enormous curvature: energy rises by hundreds of eV over 0.1 Å, force reaches 10³–10⁵ eV/Å where physical forces are a few eV/Å. An MD run reaching one is destroyed within one step (one atom gains enough velocity in a single 0.2 fs step to double the cell temperature); a validation set with one such frame reports force RMSE a thousand times its MAE. Appears in fine-tuned (Case A) and pretrained (Case B) models; expected to grow with model size and training length.

##### Case A — hydrogen fine-tune failure (§3)

**System (§3.1.1).** 64 H atoms, cubic cell 4.65817 Å = 101.076 Å³ = **1.579 Å³/atom**, 1000 K, NVT, 0.2 fs timestep, pressure ≈ 2.5 Mbar (250 GPa). `HH-DPA4-Neo.pt2`, AOTInductor export, LAMMPS `pair_style deepmd`. Descriptor: `rcut` 6.0 Å, `channels` 32, `n_radial` 16 (Bessel), `lmax`/`mmax` 3/1, `n_blocks` 2, `edge_norm` true, `precision` float32, training `min_nbor_dist` 0.5108 Å. `rcut` exceeds the cell edge (image shells to ±3); neighbour count in cutoff **562–583/atom**, mean 574.

**Timeline (§3.1.2)** (`thermo_style custom step vol temp press density pe ke etotal enthalpy pxx pxy pxz pyy pyz pzz`) — step: Temp, Press, PotEng, Pyy, Pzz — 17092: 1121, 2533734, −114.61, 2527144, 2400396; **17093: 1100, 3392632, −114.96, 3477766, 3883042**; 17094: 1155, 2533099, −114.75, 2521455, 2278420; 17096: 1197, 2582268, −114.99, 2673248, 2227048; **17097: 2464, 4651021, −113.76, 4003995, 7303895**; 17098: 8263, 3299290, −113.64, 3029458, 3343577; 17102: —, 1.5e+11, 172527. 17093 is a one-step excursion (pressure +34%, Pzz +62%, both return); 17097 is the loss of control (temperature doubles in one 0.2 fs step, never recovers). Abort at 17102 with a CUDA device-side assert `index out of bounds` in an Inductor kernel — a consequence (energy 1.5e11, atoms scattered, ghost indices out of range), not the cause.

**Virial moves, energy does not (§3.1.3).** At 17093 energy changes 0.35 eV over 64 atoms = **5 meV/atom** while Pzz changes 62%. Energy is a sum over atoms (one atom's error diluted by `N`); the virial `W_ab = Σ_{i<j} r_{ij,a} f_{ij,b}` takes a single large pair force at full weight with the pair distance as lever arm. **A virial spike with a quiet energy is the signature of a few atoms carrying wrong forces.**

**Reproduction (§3.2, `repro_spike.py` from `model_ema.ckpt.pt`, eager fp32, compilation and TF32 off)** — frame: E (eV), F_max (eV/Å), F_rms, tr(W)/3 — control_s5000: −113.47, 5.06, 1.69, 152.7; control_s12000: −114.34, 3.84, 1.48, 154.2; s17091: −114.15, 7.99, 3.12, 158.6; s17092: −114.65, 14.07, 5.27, 153.7; **s17093: −114.95, 49.41, 17.02, 210.3**; s17094: −114.79, 18.06, 6.16, 153.4; s17096: −115.04, 28.55, 7.85, 156.2; **s17097: −113.54, 318.02, 82.93, 286.4**; s17098: −113.66, 5.48, 1.97, 163.2. Reproduces exactly: the cause is in the model, not the export, LAMMPS, or reduced precision.

**What it is not (§3.3).** (1) *Not a discontinuity*: `repro_spike.py --interp`, straight line in 192-dim coordinate space, s17096→s17097, 30 samples — t=0.00 E=−115.035 |F|max=28.55; 0.33 −115.865 38.29; 0.63 −116.467 6.96 (energy minimum); 0.80 −116.037 100.53; 1.00 −113.536 318.01; across 70 samples on two segments the first and second differences of energy and force are smooth — continuous and differentiable, simply the wrong shape. (2) *Not pair-distance extrapolation*: minimum distance (minimum image) control_s5000 0.6447 Å (29,52); s17093 0.6430 (45,53); s17094 0.6324 (45,53); s17097 0.6434 (45,53) — all 0.61–0.68 Å, above the training minimum 0.5108 Å; s17093 is not even the closest. (3) *Not one module*: `locate_amplifier.py` hooks every leaf module and compares an early low-change segment with the late tenfold-growth segment — largest ratio over all modules **1.08**, almost all 1.00. (4) *Not the edge-frame chart switch*: `quaternion_nlerp` aligns sign via `dot < 0`, the unnormalized inner product is `-2x`, so the sign flips on the plane `x = 0`; `frame_continuity.py` — z: Δquaternion, Δrotation, ΔWigner-D, alignment residual — 0.0: 1.414, **2.828**, **4.000**, 3.3e−14; 0.5: 1.995, 0.391, 0.952, 1.4e−14; 0.9: 2.000, 6.4e−7, 1.6e−6, 5.7e−15; the jump is in the frame's *roll* about local +Z, up to 90° near the equator, and `frame_jump_force.py` gives largest/median step ratio **1.0–1.1**, no jump (everything downstream is equivariant under rotation about local z). (5) *Not neighbour-list truncation*, excluded by construction: `build_neighbor_list` with `return_mode="edges"`, and `_select_neighbor_builder` in `sezm_model.py` states *"Both builders keep every neighbor within `rcut`, so the resulting edge set never depends on `sel`."* — both `vesin` and `nvalchemiops` retain every pair inside the cutoff.

**The defect (§3.4, `atom49_pes.py`;** atom 49 carries the largest force in every frame). s17097, 49–48 bond — r (Å): E (eV), F_radial (eV/Å), min r to any, to — 0.600: −81.7, −2782, 0.600, 48; 0.637: **+73.1**, −4450, 0.638, 48; 0.675: **+172.3**, −438, 0.675, 48; 0.712: +134.2, 1848, 0.712, 48; 0.825: −29.3, 1018, 0.825, 48; 0.900: −92.5, 659, 0.812, 33; **0.938: −111.7, +322**, 0.799, 33; 0.975: −116.5, −9.2, 0.788, 33; 1.050: −114.2, −18.1, 0.770, 33. `min r to any` = `r` throughout the peak: no third atom responsible. Other short bond 49–33, same frame — 0.600: −112.7, 16.6; 0.675: −115.2, 34.6; 0.750: −113.4, −54.2; 0.825: −116.1, 68.1; 0.900: −114.3, −25.5. Both H–H over the same separations: one a normal soft well, the other a **+285 eV peak above the −113 eV baseline**, forces to 4450 eV/Å — a many-body artefact set by the environment.

**How it kills the run (§3.4.1).** Peak at 0.6–0.7 Å, outer flank to 0.94 Å, MD working distance exactly there. r(49–48)/r(49–33): s17091 0.9501/0.9023; s17093 0.9651/0.8549; s17096 0.9618/0.8203; **s17097 0.9438/0.7969**; s17098 1.0971/0.8377; s17101 1.5903/1.0322. At r = 0.944 Å the scan gives F_radial = +322 eV/Å, matching |F|max 318. The pair enters the flank only at step 17090, seven steps before failure; 17093 is a shallower part of the same slope, absorbed by the thermostat; at 17097 an H atom gains enough velocity in 0.2 fs to double the temperature. Later distances 1.10, 1.26, 1.42, 1.59 = the pair blown apart.

**How common (§3.5, `spike_density.py`;** hill climb = random displacement in a 0.05 Å ball kept if the largest force grew, 40 evaluations) — frame: σ=0.02 Å median/max/P(>100); σ=0.05 Å median/max; hill climb — control_s5000: 4.8/10.3/**0%**; 7.9/16.2; 5.06 → 16.6 (3.3×). s17096: 17.7/**264**/**5%**; 12.6/**4222**; 28.55 → **8043** (282×). Not a single accident, not uniform: defects concentrate in the high-pressure, high-coordination region (~570 neighbours per atom). The σ=0.1 Å row reaches 1e9 eV/Å and is *not* evidence (pairs pushed below 0.5 Å).

**Training data (§3.6).** Fine-tuned from `DPA4-Neo-OMat24` for 10⁶ steps (checkpoint step 999999, final learning rate 10⁻⁶) on 14702 frames of 64-atom H cells (edges 4.40–5.71 Å, 1.33–2.4 Å³/atom, mean 1.96) plus 608 frames of 128-atom cells; validation 3675 + 152 (`temp/H_data`). The failed run at 1.579 Å³/atom is the first quartile: 4261 training frames at or below 1.60 Å³/atom. `h_train_scan.py`, 2941 training and 1838 validation frames: force RMSE 0.073 and 0.130 eV/Å, curvature ratio at most 0.29 and 0.40. The defect is invisible from the data.

**Isolated dimer (§3.7).** Pretrained models physical: `DPA4-Neo-OMat24` binds H₂ by 4.38 eV at 0.75 Å, `DPA4-Pro` by 4.14 eV, largest curvature outside the wall 7–14 eV/Å² (H₂ stretch constant 33). The user's fine-tuned model: **−4·10¹² eV**, forces 10¹⁶ eV/Å at 4 Å. Far from any configuration the hydrogen model meets in its own dynamics, so not the cause of §3.1's crash — a fast, deterministic, label-free instrument stating the reach problem: generators must produce environments of *lower* coordination. Well depth (eV; physical −4.4) at 10k/20k/30k/40k/60k/80k–120k steps — baseline 2·10⁻⁴ (`h_long_base`): —/—/−4.6·10⁶/—/−6.8·10⁷/−4.2·10⁹ (90k), −2.0·10⁹ (120k); logging twin / strategy 10 at 2·10⁻⁴: —/—/−11 / −2.2/—/−2.8·10⁶ / −41/(120k) −3.7·10⁸ / −8.2·10⁷, strategy 10 at 90k −1.7·10⁷; plain 10⁻³ (`h_fast_log`): —/−7.1·10¹²/—/−2.3·10¹⁰/−5.3·10¹²/−4.0·10³ (80k), −1.8·10³ (100k); strategy 10 dense: —/−1.6·10⁸/—/−1.2·10⁹/−9.6·10⁷/−2.0·10⁹ (80k); cap 1.0 / cap 1.5: —/−8.1·10⁷ / −5.9·10¹⁰/—/−2.8·10¹⁰ / −1.7·10⁹/−3.2·10¹⁰ / −1.4·10⁶/−2.2·10¹¹ (80k), −2.5·10¹¹ (100k); cap 1.0 + strategy 10 (`h_fast_both`): —/−7.8·10⁵/—/−4.9·10⁶/−7.7·10¹⁰; hinge with sparse generators (`h_fast_sparse`): −9.2/−0.63/−2.1 (curvature 1.3·10⁴ at 1.28 Å)/−3.3·10⁶ (spike 3.7·10¹⁰ eV/Å² at 1.27 Å); **reference consistency with Neo (`h_fast_ref`): −2.8/−3.1/−3.1 (curv. 5.6)/−3.1 (curv. 6.2, tail force 0.03 eV/Å, end of run)**.

##### Case B — OMat24 pretraining checkpoint (§4)

**The frame (§4.1).** `Pro/N_6-l_5-c_64x2/model_ema.ckpt-788000.pt` (6 blocks, `lmax` 5, 64×2 channels, 788k steps at 2·10⁻⁴ → 6·10⁻⁵, HybridMuon, mixed precision). Force MAE 0.107 eV/Å but RMSE 61 eV/Å on the 10% validation subset (`sub01val`, 107612 frames). `pro_scan_fast.py` (float32): frame 98341, Li₃Ge₄Au₄, 11 atoms, predicted force 118259 eV/Å against a label of 27; its 11 atoms carry 99.9998% of the total squared force error; energy 50 eV too low. Unstable: 107256, 118259, 125279 eV/Å in three float32 evaluations differing only in batching, so the subset RMSE reads 61–123 eV/Å (rounding changes it by 15%). Next-worst frame 14 eV/Å. Curvature ratio 12.3 per atom, 28 by energy probe; nothing else in the 1% subset (21742 frames) above 0.83, nothing among 540 further frames above 0.5. On 200000 random *training* frames: RMSE 0.151 eV/Å, largest frame error 44.5 eV/Å (a 5-atom frame with label force 43.8 eV/Å, underfit not spiked), none above 100 eV/Å.

**Where it sits (§4.2).** Au–Li contact at 0.916 Å. `mindist_scan.py`, 100000 random training frames: contacts below 1.0 Å in 0.35% of frames, all involving H, B, C, N, O or F; no heavier-element contact shorter than 1.0 Å; shortest Au contact 1.275 Å. Only frame in the 10% subset with an Au contact below 1.2 Å. `bond_scan.py`: smooth for r ≥ 1.05 Å, chaotic below — energies −21 to −155 eV, radial forces ±10⁴ eV/Å with a period of about 0.05 Å.

**How it grew (§4.3, `pro_scan.py --indices`)** — step (×10³): force error (eV/Å), ratio per atom, ratio energy probe — 200: 23, 0.09, 0.10; 250: 38, 0.39, 3.4; 300: 23, 2.5, 0.6; 350: 65, 2.6, 12.9; 400: 50, 14.9, 9.4; 450: 307, 6.6, 18.8; 500: 236, 14.5, 21.2; 550: 97, 9.5, 21.8; 600: 1533, 14.3, 26.8; 650: 1389, 11.1, 29.2; 700: 6129, 138, 25.1; 750: 149, 78, 130; 788 EMA: 119399, 12.2, 28.4; 806 EMA: 3296, 16.8, 27.8. At 200k wrong but smooth (profile flat at −30 eV from 0.6 to 1.1 Å, forces below 70 eV/Å). At 300k the ratio crosses 1 with the error unchanged. At 400k oscillations of ±170 eV/Å with 0.1 Å period, error still 50 eV/Å. From 450k the error grows two orders of magnitude and fluctuates by a factor of 40 between checkpoints, EMA included. **The criterion led the error by about 150k steps.**

______________________________________________________________________

## 10. Record — mechanism and physics statements

Every causal or physical claim the log established, with its evidence, labelled FACT (measured) or INFERENCE (interpretation), grouped by the period of the log in which it was made.

#### Mechanism and physics statements — the early record, §1–§10.12 (28 August – 3 September)

Excluded candidates (§5.1; `attn_stats.py`, parameter comparison with the pretrained model):

- *Attention saturation — excluded.* FACT: pre-softmax logits within ±1.9; largest attention weight per destination atom 0.03 (near-uniform over ~570 neighbours, entropy 4.97 of a maximum 6.3), identical on healthy frames, pathological frames and the peak configuration.
- *Radial-basis frequency drift — excluded.* FACT: trainable Bessel frequencies remain at `nπ/r_c` (≤ 8.2 Å⁻¹); no extra resolution learned.
- *Frobenius weight growth — excluded; operator-norm growth confirmed.* FACT: Frobenius norm fell 641 → 606 during fine-tuning, largest single-tensor growth 2.8× in small tensors. FACT (`weight_spectra.py`, Pro 200k → 806k): the largest singular value of every weight matrix grows monotonically — SO(2) mixing gate projections 1.5–1.8× (5.5 → 9.9 in block 0), SO(2) linears 1.3–1.6×, attention-logit weights 1.6×, radial embedding matrix 2.1× (9.6 → 20.0), normalization biases 2–3×; median 1.2–1.7× per family, no family shrinking. INFERENCE: the curvature of a composition is bounded by the product of per-layer operator norms, so 1.5× per layer over six blocks of several gated products is a bound growing two to three orders of magnitude — the order of the measured curvature growth (§4.3). Frobenius is insensitive because the growth sits in one direction while the rest shrinks slightly.
- *Novel activation directions — excluded.* FACT (`activation_subspace.py`, 36 linear maps with hooks): the data subspace carries 99% of activation energy (3–179 of 32–192 directions); pathological frames do not leave it — energy outside it 0.0096 (pathological) vs 0.0092 (held-out healthy); the most novel row is *less* novel on pathological frames in 35 of 36 maps (0.083 vs 0.133). Weight change from the Neo initialization is spread evenly over the data subspace and its complement (density ratio 0.86, above 1 in 11 of 36 maps). The log-alignment ratio of Vaswani et al. (arXiv:2605.28975) stays at the random-initialization value (0.50 → 0.52) and does not distinguish pathological inputs (0.52).
- *Capacity for 0.05 Å structure.* INFERENCE (text labels it "an inference about capacity, not a measurement of cause"): distance enters multiplicatively, many times in depth — per block `DynamicRadialDegreeMixer` multiplies edge features by radial weights, and each of `mixing_layers` SO(2) layers ends in a gated activation (`gate·sigmoid(gate)·value`); the Pro configuration composes about thirty such products, and the curvature of a product grows with the product of its factors' curvatures.

**What the two cases share (§5.2).** FACT: neither defect is at a training configuration — the hydrogen peak is a narrow, directional pocket inside a well-sampled thermodynamic state (29% of training frames at or below 1.60 Å³/atom; 1.579 is the first quartile) that isotropic sampling almost never enters; the Pro defect lies outside the sampled region. FACT: both were smooth early and became rough (Pro flat at 200k, oscillating at 400k, chaotic at 788k; hydrogen MD finds no defect near control frames but a dense field near the trajectory's end). INFERENCE: nothing pins the function where there is no data, so as parameters move to fit data elsewhere the function there accumulates high-frequency structure — **training longer makes empty regions rougher, and a larger model has more of them**; consistent with larger models trained hundreds of thousands of steps failing and with a small learning rate delaying it.

**Consequences (§5.3, INFERENCE).** (i) A remedy must act in empty regions while the loop visits only training frames, so every §9 strategy reaches an empty region from a training frame. (ii) Labels cannot help there; two label-free constraints remain — physical (bounded curvature, §7, holding equally where the model is wrong) and historical (the function the run started from, §2); neither requires accuracy — the first asks for smoothness, the second for staying put. (iii) Early in training empty regions are smooth for free, so keeping them so is a smaller intervention than repairing later.

**Reference-term physics (§10.11.2, §10.13).** FACT: the plain run's Cu–I repulsion is a pair property — 68 eV/Å whatever surrounds the pair, as a hard contact demands. FACT: the term's run learnt two functions for one pair, suppressed where the environment is thin, full where it is dense. INFERENCE: dilation by 1.3–2 carries a contact at 0.45 of the covalent distance to 0.59–0.9, still inside the wall, and deletion leaves it at 0.45 with its full 68 eV/Å; on both anchors the reference's force is near zero, so the hinge holds the model near zero while the data hold 33–67 eV/Å in the dense frame — the environment dependence that satisfies both is a steep feature, and where its boundary passes through a dense frame with one extreme pair, that feature is the spike. INFERENCE (§10.13): both production failures are "the function moves where the data do not hold it" — the plain run drifts and roughens (a needle in the pair function at the edge of the data), the fixed-random-reference run is pushed toward a reference carrying no physics. Both call for a reference carrying the physics the data taught — the model's *own state* at a later time, the random initialization being only the state at step 0 and a fine-tune's pretrained model the same thing, so pretraining and fine-tuning share one recipe.

______________________________________________________________________

#### Mechanism and physics statements — the refreshed reference, §10.13 first half (2–6 September)

- FACT: with a periodically refreshed reference the isolated-H2 hole deepens with the number of refreshes (−49 / −916 eV at 30000 / 40000 for refresh 10000; −135 / −7.5 eV for refresh 2000; −0.25 / −0.27 eV for the fixed random reference), while all three runs have the same validation force error to 0.5 meV/Å.
- INFERENCE: a fixed reference pins the reached region to one function for the whole run, so the extrapolation beyond it stays bounded because the weights that shape it never move; a refreshed reference lets the reached region move by one tube per interval, and the extrapolation beyond it is not bounded by that tube — every refresh legitimizes whatever the unreached region has drifted to. Evidence: the refresh table; `neo_rf10k`'s Cu-I pair alone 53, 100, 37 eV/Å at the three refreshes (plain 66, 53); `h_cont_acc10_rf10k` tail curvature 9-13 eV/Å² vs 0.4-0.5 for `h_cont_acc8`.
- FACT + INFERENCE: the testbeds differ in where the dimer sits — in OMat24 the small cells make the deletion anchors periodic dimers and single atoms, so the isolated pair lies inside the region the anchors reach; in dense hydrogen (64 atoms at 0.55-0.66 Å) no anchor resembles an isolated pair, so the dimer is extrapolation.
- FACT: under the random reference the dense anchors sit 0.1-0.3 Å from the data frames, where the true forces are large and the reference's near zero, so the contradiction of §10.11.2 acts at full strength next to the data (`neo_rf10kD` in-frame 0.62 eV/Å against label 33; hardest-13 4.40 vs 0.89; +11% RMSE on the testbed). INFERENCE: dense anchors are admissible only once the reference carries the data's physics.
- FACT: the walls are learnt by 6000 steps of the production run, and at 10000 steps the model still holds every probed contact independent of its environment and the H2 well at −3.7 eV. INFERENCE: `S` must lie after the learning of the walls and before the contradiction bites, between 6000 and 10000-20000 steps. The design is two fixed references in sequence, not a moving one; `S` is the single added parameter.
- INFERENCE: this differs from the freezing-step scan of §12.4, where the term was inactive before the freeze and the reference inherited 10000 unconstrained steps of roughness (end-of-run dimer curvature 285 eV/Å²); here the random reference holds the function until the hand-over.
- FACT: the plain run digs its hole within 20000 steps of the start; hand-over runs dig theirs 20000 steps after the hand-over (`S` = 10000 at 40000, `S` = 20000 at 30000; plain is the case `S` = 0). INFERENCE: after the hand-over the sparse-anchored run continues as the plain run does.
- FACT: every variant that later digs a hole has left the wall-curvature ceiling by 20000 steps, one or two readings before its well depth shows anything. INFERENCE: the hole is the late stage of a wall that has been oscillating for 10000-20000 steps; A2's monotone-wall requirement catches it first.
- FALSIFIED HYPOTHESIS: that the fixed random reference works through an *active* hinge (the model sitting inside a tube around a function the data contradict, so the term pulls at every anchor step and pins the weights). `rf10k1P`, with the tube closed at the hand-over, digs its hole *sooner* (−21.7 eV @0.500 Å by 30000, at the plain recipe's accuracy, 60.2 meV/Å at 20000).
- INFERENCE: a closed tube on the far anchors is harmful, not merely useless — the constant pull of an L2 term on every dilated and deleted frame outweighs the hinge of the near anchors, which acts only outside its tube, and the wall moves under it. Evidence: `rf10k1F` digs −8.6 eV @0.470 Å by 30000 although its near family supplies the same number of shell and collision anchors per step as `rf10k1D`, which stays bounded; `neo_rf10k1P` fails B5 at 20000 the same way.
- FACT: `rf10k1P01` (closed tube, weight 0.1) is still bounded at 30000 and digs −45.9 eV @0.420 Å by 40000, deeper than the tubed sparse run's −15.6 eV at the same step; its 30000-step wall curvature (6.3e4 vs 4.1e4) had already said so. INFERENCE: a weak pull delays the hole and then deepens it.
- INFERENCE (central): what separates bounded runs from collapsing ones is *where the anchors are*, not how hard they pull. The hole forms in the wall at 0.4-0.5 Å; the shell and collision anchors compress the pairs of the dense hydrogen frames (neighbours at 0.55-0.66 Å) into exactly that region, so the dense variants hold the wall with an ordinary tube; the dilated and deleted frames never contain a compressed pair, so the sparse variants leave the wall to extrapolation, tube or no tube.
- INFERENCE: the fixed random reference bounds the same wall by another route — its far field is flat, and a model held to near-zero forces on every stretched and thinned frame keeps a small far-field response everywhere, the compressed pair included; this is also why it contradicts the physics of the contacts (§10.11.2), flatness being the wrong target inside the wall and a learnt reference the right one only where anchors reach.
- INFERENCE: the two families anchor two different coordinates of the same wall — shell and collision fix the response to a compressed pair inside a dense frame, the deleted frames fix the response to a thin environment, and the isolated pair (a compressed pair in the thinnest environment) is held only where both are anchored; the environment dependence of the contact forces is exactly the freedom the deleted frames remove. Evidence: `rf10k1N` collapses by 30000 and reads −85.6 eV @0.490 Å at 40000; `rf10k1` digs at 40000; `rf10k1D` and `rf10k1C` stay bounded.
- FACT: with dilation kept and deletion removed (`rf10k1D_nodel`) the well moves into the wall and deepens reading by reading, and at 40000 the minimum sits at 2.1 Å with a tail curvature of 679 eV/Å². INFERENCE: the thinned (deleted) frames are the far anchors that hold the isolated pair; the stretched (dilated) frames alone do not.
- FACT: the package draws one generator per anchor frame (a deleted frame keeps the data's pair distances; a shell or collision frame keeps the data's density), while the prototype `train_curv.py` composes deletion, shell, collision and dilation on every anchor frame, placing compressed pairs inside thinned, stretched frames — the isolated dimer's own geometry. INFERENCE: with one generator per frame no anchor combines a compressed pair with a thin environment, and the wall of the isolated pair is left to extrapolation between the two families; the production twins run with one-per-frame anchors, consistent with their residual defects on the isolated pairs.
- FACT: on the testbed at 40000 `rf10k1N` holes (−85.6 eV) and `rf10k1C` is still bounded: the far anchors are necessary and the shell is not — the reverse of the production reading on the isolated pairs. INFERENCE: the shell's role shows on OMat24's contacts, which the hydrogen data do not contain.
- INFERENCE (cause of C9's failure): the shell and collision anchors are the training frames displaced by 0.1-0.3 Å, so on the rarest contact frames — where the 10000-step reference is wrong by up to 20 eV/Å — the hinge holds the model inside a tube of 1 + 0.1·|F_ref| ≈ 6 eV/Å around a wrong value, and the frames are too rare for the data to pull it back.
- Design (label-widened tube): `tube = tol + rel_tol · |F_ref| + |F_ref − F_label|` on near anchors, `F_label` being the label force of the source frame (the batch's own labels; no new data, no extra forward). Both forces are taken on the source frame, so the term measures the reference's error, not the physical change of the force under the transform; an atom keeps its identity under every generator (a deleted atom becomes a phantom and leaves the hinge); far anchors have no label and keep the ordinary tube. Ordinary atoms (reference error a few tenths of an eV/Å) keep their tube; rare contact atoms are released to the data's generalization while a collapse of hundreds of eV/Å stays far outside the widened tube. There is no gate or screening: the width is a continuous function of a quantity the batch already carries.
