- FACT: on hydrogen the label-widened tube flattens the wall at 20000 (force at 0.5-0.7 Å within ±1.5 eV/Å, E(0.45 Å) 0.4 eV below the separated atoms, wall curvature 630 eV/Å² — the shape of the fixed random reference's dimer, whose force at 0.5-0.6 Å is 1.5 and −0.2 eV/Å at that step), then opens at 30000 and holes at 40000. INFERENCE: the reference's error on the dense hydrogen frames is of the size of the wall's own forces, so the widened tube no longer holds the wall; hydrogen's only contact is H-H, so releasing it releases the wall itself, and the twin's OMat24 needles are the same release met on rare frames.
- FACT: the uniform widening to relative tolerance 0.3 starts a hole at once (minimum at 0.555 Å at 20000, F(0.6 Å) = −26 eV/Å, E at the physical bond length 1.5 eV above the separated atoms). INFERENCE: the wall's deviations from the reference at the start of a collapse are of order ten eV/Å, inside a 30% tube on the wall's own forces, so a uniform widening trades the bound for the release (an isolated pair with |F_ref| = 60 eV/Å may then deviate by 19 eV/Å instead of 7).
- INFERENCE (weak, explicitly qualified): the far anchors transmit the reference's far field, spurious tail force included — fixed random reference 0.22-0.40 eV/Å (3-4× plain), dense hand-over variants 0.10-0.25, sparse 0.04-0.15, near-only 0.04-0.05. The production twins show the O2 tail force scattering between repeats of one recipe by more than it differs between recipes, so the ordering is suggestive and not a mechanism.
- INFERENCE (E55/E56): closing one pair per anchor frame to 0.30-0.55 Å removes the isolated O2 wall within 10000 steps of the squeeze; the anchors copy the reference's function where the reference is untrained.
- INFERENCE (E66, from the curvature split of §10.13.17): the amplifier lies in the trunk, not in the fitting head.
- Engineering (E21 `dens`): the shared read-only statistics cache lacks the force RMSD of the `dens` mode (the run computes it into its own copy); the compiled `dens` path with the fused CUDA kernel under bf16 autocast hands a bfloat16 tensor to the fused SO(2) backward (fused kernels stay off for `dens`); under DistributedDataParallel the unused energy-mode head stalls the reducer (the launcher freezes the inactive head whenever the mode is switched); the `dens` path read the phantom atoms' type −1 of a mixed-size batch as a table index (out-of-bounds gather at the second iteration), which the energy path sanitizes — fixed in `core_compute_dens` with a regression test on a phantom-padded batch.
- Engineering (other): E32's job 274 died at the bound's first activation from a `retain_graph` backward on the compiled graph, fixed by taking the data gradient's norm from the clipping seam; E23's launches died from batch padding kept in the predictions handed to the batch-error log and from an OOM when a replayed frame wider than the batch padded the whole batch (a buffered frame now waits for a batch wide enough to hold it, checked on mixed-size OMat24 batches); E64's patch does not reach the attention aggregation, so that run did not test its question.

#### Mechanism and physics statements — the refreshed reference, §10.13 second half (3–6 September)

Numbers are in the table; each bullet names the run and the distinguishing reading.

- FACT -- The sparse anchors alone do not hold what the random phase seeded for the probed contacts; the dense anchors do. The twins are identical up to the hand-over, yet `neo_rf10k1` reads Cu-I 43/46/-1.7/-1.5 at 20000 and 1.7 eV/Å in frame vs label 33 at 30000 while `neo_rf10k1D` holds every probed contact; the sparse twin's reference was verified to be the 10000-step state.
- FACT -- The collisions hold the contacts; the shell anchors are what held the reference's error next to the data (`neo_rf10k1C`, no shell: every probed contact environment-independent at 20000 with the lowest C9 of all runs at that stage, where the sparse twin had collapsed).
- FACT -- The shell is not optional: without it the isolated Ag-Ag reads 74/87/74 vs 55/54/52 at 70000/80000/100000, O2 stays at -2.9 to -3.6 eV while the dense twin deepens to -4.7 eV, and C9 rises from 0.5 at 20000 to 1.02-1.26 by 70000.
- FACT -- A closed tube on dilated and deleted frames pins the model only on the frames actually drawn, and those contacts are drawn rarely (`neo_rf10k1P`; the hydrogen run `rf10k1P` says the same of the wall).
- INFERENCE (attribution) -- The dense twin's elevated C9 (1.0-1.6 eV/Å) is the price of the shell anchors, which hold the rarest contacts inside the tube of the 10000-step reference where that reference is wrong by up to 20 eV/Å. It sits at the fixed-reference run's level (1.33 at 90000, 1.98 at 100000) while the plain run falls to 0.34 at 100000 between its needles; every variant releasing those atoms sits 20-50% lower.
- FACT -- Releasing the contact atoms costs the two-atom limit (label-tube isolated Ag-Ag -12 to -27%; combination twin isolated Ca-H to -68%; near-only rerun isolated Ca-H swinging 24 -> 67 eV/Å between 130000 and 140000). The direction differs by recipe: the dense twin holds the same pairs **stiff** (Ca-H +22 to +39% from 140000).
- INFERENCE -- In the decay phase the far anchors' tube, at the hand-over reference's values, is the only thing holding the two-atom limit, and it holds it on the stiff side of the frames (dense twin 420000-540000, isolated Ag-Ag outside the band at four of the last five readings, dimers and C9 unchanged). The pair-term twins' limits swing soft instead, and no twin holds the isolated pair to the frame within the band over a whole run.
- FACT -- The O2 4-7 Å tail force is not attributable to the anchors: it fluctuates within one run (dense twin 0.68, 0.10, 0.36, 0.32 at 150000-180000) and between repeats of one recipe as much as between recipes (first near-only twin 0.26-0.41, its rerun 0.37/0.50/0.68/0.92 at 20000-50000); the near-only rerun has **no** far anchors yet carries the largest tails; on the hydrogen testbed the fixed random reference carries the largest tail and the near-only run the smallest. Its level, 0.1-0.9 eV/Å on O2 in every from-scratch run including the plain one, is a property of the from-scratch models at this stage of training.
- FACT -- A wall force below the plain run's at the first reading is the earliest warning the dimer gives: the fixed-reference run, which lost its wall at 92000, read 2.0-3.4 eV and 2.7-5.9 eV/Å from 20000, and the composed twin, holed at 70000, read 2.2-3.5 eV and 4.6-7.1 eV/Å over 20000-60000 -- below the plain run at every reading, from 50000 steps before the hole.
- FACT/INFERENCE -- The composed twin's H2 hole follows from its anchor placement: dilating every anchor frame after thinning and compressing it leaves no anchor holding a thin environment at the data's own distances -- the pure deletion frames the one-per-frame twins draw a quarter of the time, which the hydrogen dissection identified as the far anchors carrying the bound. Confirmed by `neo_S10k_all4_composed_nodil`, whose wall is the stiffest of any twin at 20000. The stretched anchors, not the composition, let the wall go; the dense twin, which has those frames, holds the isolated H2 pair through 280000 steps.
- FACT -- A transient hole on the unanchored isolated pair is a warning, not a falsification; a recurrence is (composed twin holed at 70000, monotonic at 80000, wall collapsing again at 90000).
- FACT -- At production scale the near anchors alone hold the contacts and the dimers through 190000 steps, where the hydrogen near-only run collapsed within 20000 steps of its hand-over; the difference is the data, which on OMat24 reach thin environments the hydrogen frames never contain.
- INFERENCE -- Two label-widened twins carrying three training-batch needles between them, with none anywhere else, is consistent with the widening releasing the contact atoms on which the model then overshoots a rare data frame. Baseline: the dense twin through 380000 steps, the pair-term and composed twins, and the plain production run through 750000 log no batch above 100 eV/Å. The E7 dump settled it on the plain diagnostic run: the needle is the model's -- a hole or an overshoot of the wall on a rare close contact of a data frame.
- FACT -- The needle storm is data-triggered: five of seven plain needles fall on four Li4V4P4O20 cells with a Li-O pair at 0.57 Å whose label forces are only 15-34 eV/Å where physics demands hundreds, and call 32090 stands in three logs at 110-123 eV/Å, the same frame held at that level by every anchored recipe while the plain run tears. Whether the label or the geometry is wrong, the family contradicts the rest of the data and the plain model's function tears at it a little more at each visit.
- FACT -- An off-data defect leaves no trace on the bulk subset: the plain twin's O2 hole at 40000 coincides with a normal scan (0.214/0.121/15.3/11).
- FACT -- The replay's accuracy cost lies in the replay loss's weight, not the share of frames replayed: thresholds 0.55 and 0.5 cost the same 39% with a tenfold difference in frames replayed, while the importance-weighted form costs 3.9% at weight 0.08 and 1.1% at weight 0.03.
- INFERENCE -- The deletion generator's reach stops short of the two-atom limit: the combination twin's isolated Ca-H fell to 18 eV/Å (-68%) while the same pair with 90% of the other atoms deleted still read 48, and the generator deletes at most 90%.
- FACT -- The combination twin's pair term does not see its own worst swing: the tube 5 + 1.0\*|f| eV/Å is a half-width of 61 eV/Å around 56 and admits the 18 eV/Å pair.
- FACT -- Each generator has one effect: the collision holds the contacts and keeps C9 low early on; the shell holds the isolated pairs and deepens the isolated wells, at the price of locking the reference's error on the rarest contacts (the C9 gap the label-widened tube addresses); the deletion anchors hold the isolated molecules and pairs near the hand-over reference's values, which is why the twins keeping them start with a shallow O2 (-3.57 eV at 20000) and deepen it as the data teach (-4.69 eV at 100000).
- FACT -- Every twin with the near anchors keeps the H2 wall stiffer than the plain run's (3.5-8.0 eV above the well at 0.45 Å against 2.9-3.6, and 5.6-19.0 eV/Å, the force below 8 eV/Å at five readings in 74) and the wells physical. The far anchors leave one dimer mark of their own (the dense twin's intermittent second minimum at 2.8 Å from 140000) and their absence another (the near-only twin's swinging two-atom limit).
- FACT -- The anchors cost nothing measurable on the bulk data: within 1% of the plain run on the two robust readings through 100000 steps, same energy error, same number of tail frames; 0.8% at 40000.
- OPEN -- Whether the far anchors are needed at production length is still open at 190000 steps: without them nothing has collapsed, but the pair with nothing around it is held by nothing; on the hydrogen testbed they are needed, and the deletion is the one that counts.
- OPEN -- Which direction (dense-recipe stiff, label-tube soft) the data's own value lies nearer is not settled by either twin.

#### Mechanism and physics statements — the optimizer and the candidate ledger, §10.14–10.15 (2–6 September)

- **The optimizer sets the rate of the drift, not its existence.** FACT (§10.14): the un-whitened momentum direction at a thirtieth of Muon's step still digs the hole, one checkpoint later and shallower than plain Muon; the damping costs nothing at the peak rate and at most 1 % in the decay …
- **The amplification chain.** FACT (§10.14.1): radial MLP ×6, SO(2) message ×12 per block, residual into the read-out ×8.4, read-out FFN ×73 — the square, because the read-out's grid product is quadratic in an input read without a norm (`sandwich_norm: [true,false,true,false]`) …
- **Where the amplifier is.** FACT (§10.13.17): head curvature per unit input 0.0006–0.075 eV per squared descriptor unit while |dh/dr| reaches 309–473 per Å on the metal wells — the trunk's radial chain (basis → block 0 → block 1) is the amplifier, and Si–O is the same mechanism an order of magnitude smoother …
- **The O₂ wall at 0.6 Å.** FACT: every dense-recipe twin has lost the wall; the plain twin between storms reads +53…+201 eV/Å; live and EMA weights agree, so it is not an averaging artefact …
- **A reference term cannot supply the wall.** INFERENCE from the tube's algebra (E34): F ≤ 1.1·F_ref + 1 eV/Å admits only geometric growth (≈6 eV/Å after five time constants) against +50…+200 eV/Å; E3's second hand-over has the same limit in discrete form.
- **The tail input of the radial bases.** FACT: a 4 Å pair injects 11 % of a bonded pair's input in the Bessel basis and 44 % in either flat-norm basis. The causal link to the early tail forces is INFERENCE and was **refuted by its own test** — E41 restores the Bessel tail input exactly and read 1.13 eV/Å at 10000, larger than E38's 0.67 …
- **Needles vs tears.** FACT (§10.13.6): the plain twin's 22 window events are 17 small (100–700 eV/Å) and 5 tears above 1000 (largest 8.9·10⁸); the tears dump torn frames and precede the holes, and every candidate acting on the amplification or on the data's pull removes the tears and leaves the small ones …
- **The late storm is the short-range pair function losing its smoothness below the data's contacts.** FACT: at the storm frames' geometries every checkpoint is within 2 eV/Å of the label; the isolated dimer's force is a smooth wall through 78000 and oscillates at 80000 over 0.33–0.48 Å with an amplitude matching the storm's 560–3680 eV/Å …
- **The live weights lead the averaged weights.** FACT (§10.13.10): the frames' compressed pairs at 0.40–0.54 Å needle first (E43's first draw: twelve between 61209 and 66511, 156–463 eV/Å), the isolated pair's function steps next (66000), the burst of tears follows (67131–67191), the averaged weights show the band last …
- **The data are not the cause.** FACT (E22): with the family's loss removed the storm came 3000 calls earlier and the O₂ hole 10000 steps earlier (−886 eV at 0.465 Å at 30000), and silenced frames' own errors ran to 3.0·10⁸ eV/Å with no gradient …
- **An anchor teaches the reference's function wherever it is placed.** FACT (E55/E56): within 10000 steps of the squeeze the isolated O₂ wall is gone on all four draws and holed on one, tails clean and no needle — the squeeze damaged exactly the geometry it labelled …
- **The sparse limit's amplitude.** FACT (§10.13.13): a node with one neighbour reaches the read-out at 2.65 against 0.91 with twelve — fewer neighbours, a larger descriptor — because the per-node norms with eps 1e-5 project the feature onto the unit sphere whatever its amplitude …
- **What eSEN and UMA do at the same seams.** FACT (verified in both code trees): the block norms are the same; eSEN differs in the bare neighbour sum with a constant rescale, a normalisation immediately before the head, and a head linear in the feature amplitude, where SeZM's read-out squares it twice over …
- **The second Codex review.** FACT: every verdict was checked against the sources before it was acted on; it corrected E63's scope, E62's second factor, E64's premise, E59's inertness, the survey's energy zero and the hydrogen tail's nature, set the statistics rule, ranked E62 first and proposed E66 and E67 …
- **The ZBL rule re-read.** FACT (§10.13.16): the DPA4C control (E65) holes 21 of 24 isolated pairs at 30000 while its training frames carry 4 small needles and no tear — the inward collapse below the data's contacts is what any descriptor trained on these data does, and the tears on training frames are SeZM's …
- **The basis cannot rise at contact.** FACT (§10.13.4): each φₙ tends to wₙ as r → 0 with a slope vanishing like −wₙ³r/3, so the force on an isolated pair is ∝ r at small r and the energy at zero separation is a finite 13.7 eV …

#### Mechanism and physics statements — the programme, §0 and §11.1–11.26 (6–7 September)

- FACT — The spurious tail force peaks at 4.9–5.4 Å on every pair independently of the elements, pointing at the radial machinery, not chemistry (§11.1).
- FACT — The envelope `E(x) = (1−x)⁴(1 + 4x + 10x² + 20x³ + 35x⁴)`, `x = r/rcut`, is 0.031 at 5 Å; its relative slope `d ln E/dr ≈ −4/(rcut − r)` is −4.5 per Å there and diverges at the cutoff (§11.1).
- INFERENCE — The envelope converts, it does not cause: Fe–O's 16 eV/Å at 5.41 Å implies a raw response of hundreds of eV, yet the same envelope in the plain Neo twin gives an H₂ tail of 0.05–0.15 eV/Å, so the envelope alone does not set the scale.
- FACT/INFERENCE — The unconstrained quantity is an isolated pair's response between 4 Å and the cutoff: such a pair never occurs in crystal data, where an atom at 5 Å always carries nearer neighbours (§§11.1–11.2, 11.7).
- FACT — Detaching the envelope from the coordinate graph (energies bit-identical) removes exactly `(∂E/∂env)(d env/dr)`; H–H over 4.0–5.9 Å reads 0.163→0.859 (pretrained), 2.393→1.806 (plain fine-tune), 0.077→0.026 (anchored) (§11.2).
- INFERENCE — The anchors removed the response itself (33× below the pretrained model's, 70× below the plain fine-tune's) rather than balancing two large terms.
- FACT — The anchors delay rather than prevent the short-range collapse, and once deep enough it contaminates the tail (§11.2).
- FACT — Tail and short-range hole are separate failures: both tail and response wander ±20 % over 240000 steps with no trend while the O₂ pair holes by 24 eV inside one 2000-step interval, so no trend-based warning can catch it (§11.4).
- FACT — Basis norm with envelope at 0.2/0.4/1.0/2.0/4.0/5.0 Å: Bessel 15.0/6.9/2.96/1.41/0.32/0.04, Gaussian 1.29/1.32/1.33/1.31/0.59/0.09. Bessel is `sin(w_n r)/r`, each tending to `w_n` as r→0, so sixteen reach ≈20 where the data sit at 1.3 (§11.5).
- INFERENCE — At the cutoff the ordering reverses: with `linspace(0, rcut, 16)` five Gaussian centres lie beyond 4 Å and one on the cutoff, each 0.4 Å wide, putting amplitude where the envelope's relative slope is steepest.
- FACT — The amplitude carried into the steep region is amplified with training (14.12 → 22.74 eV/Å between 10000 and 20000) (§§11.5–11.6).
- FACT — Confining the centres to 0–4 Å divides the tail by 34 and moves its peak to 4.00 Å, the outer edge of the new support (§11.7).
- INFERENCE — The three handles act on different factors of one product: the basis's support caps the response, the envelope's multiplicity scales the conversion, the tail term acts on the product.
- FACT — Division of labour: the anchors own the holes (18/24 → 0/24 at 20000 on OMat24); the basis support, envelope multiplicity and tail term own the tail (§11.7).
- FACT — On an isolated edge the normalized attention weight is `a = s²exp(l)/(s²exp(l) + z)`, so a large logit compensates a small envelope (at 5 Å: envelope 0.030656 with weight 0.999561 and logit 14.6883 on hydrogen.
- FACT (derivation) — `da/dr = a(1−a)[2s'/s + l']`, `d(sa)/dr = s'a(3−2a) + sa(1−a)l'`; for a constant logit the second is bounded by `9/8·|s'|`, removing the `s'/s` amplification. INFERENCE: this bounds that mechanism only.
- FACT — The large tail and the frame tear do not share a dominant gradient path: detaching the attention envelope input leaves 6558.34, logits 4378.76, all attention weights 4184.71 of 6712.79 eV/Å, and the output envelope makes it worse (§11.11).
- FACT — Splitting the fitting input as `h = m·u` (m = per-atom RMS) makes the amplitude path dominant: 7810.53 (amplitude) vs 1170.26 (direction) against 6670.05 original.
- FACT — Raw and EMA weights at the same step show the same failure, so the defects are not created solely by parameter averaging (§11.22).
- FACT — Deleting atoms in periodic cells of edge 4.39–5.71 Å retains their images (min coordination 37 after deletion, 30 after dilation). INFERENCE: calling these transformations sparse does not establish coverage of the isolated-pair limit (§11.16).
- FACT — Low-coordination anchors mostly sit inside the reference tolerance, so their gradient is zero (59/59 inactive §11.21; 4 of 241 §11.24; 3 of 131 and 4 of 374 §11.26); raising their statistical weight alone would not activate the constraint.
- FACT — The reference frozen at 10000 already carries defects, and raw and EMA curves select different principal wells; a healthy EMA survey is not evidence that the reference function is healthy (§11.24).
- FACT — All hydrogen adaptation anchors are transformations of hydrogen data and give no direct force constraint on configurations containing another element; cross-element tails reach 10¹¹–10¹² eV/Å (§11.23).
- FACT (§0) — The late inward collapse of isolated pairs is the drift of the three first-layer radial rows reading the channels centred at 0.0, 0.2 and 0.4 Å: their data gradient is 10⁻¹⁵–10⁻²⁰ of the largest row's.
- FACT (§0) — A frame tear is the visit of a frame whose geometry activates a direction of parameter space that no datum constrains. The 29394 tear reproduces in float64, its weight displacement is ordinary drift.
- FACT (§0) — The positive barrier beyond every isolated molecular pair's well, and the missing Au₂ well, are the descent to an untrained dissociation limit: the least-squares bias plus the fitting output on the isolated descriptor lie 0.2–1.3 eV per atom…
- FACT (§0) — Elements absent from the data (He, Rn, Po, At, Fr, Ra, transuranics beyond Pu: no frame; Ne, Ar, Kr, Xe: ~500 each) develop isolated-dimer wells of −10² to −10⁴ eV growing faster than linearly, in every model including the Bessel control.
- FACT — Removing the distance-independent type addition from the radial channel (same weights) reduces H₂/O₂/Fe–O tails to 0.1505/0.0943/0.6008 but loses the H₂ well (§11.14).
- FACT — At `h_ft_origref_both_srcclusters` 8000 the second block's focus signal has RMS 0.00415 while its normalization scale changes 7.35× over 4.0–4.3 Å.
- FACT — Other same-weight interventions (`runs/check_readout_20260907`) cut the tear but damage the curve: normalizing one operand of the readout's quadratic product gives 2361 eV/Å but raises a healthy H₂ tail 0.09462 → 2.9581.
- FACT — AMP gradient vectors differ from float32 eager by 1.49 % and 2.54 % (cosine 0.99990, 0.99970), and float64-vs-float32 relative gradient errors are ~1e-5 in both paths: numerical conditioning, not a kernel logic error (§§11.11, 11.22).
- INFERENCE — R/MAE = 8.5 on the Max model against 1.25 for a near-Gaussian distribution means a small population carries the square; 0.01–0.1 % of atoms at 10–100 eV/Å reproduces it.
- FACT — A 3-block 4 Å model has the same 12 Å path-length bound as a 2-block 6 Å model, though the accessible graphs differ; in a crystal intermediate atoms transmit longer-range information.
- Provenance restriction (§0) — The anchor-composition comparison must use the same radial basis and actual reference history; restarted runs that refreshed again at 30000 do not represent the one-handover recipe.

#### Mechanism and physics statements — the structural experiments, §11.27–11.43 (7–8 September)

- FACT: bounded gate values do not bound a GLU head or its input Jacobian — ∇ₕ[v(h)tanh(g(h))] = tanh(g)∇ₕv + v·sech²(g)∇ₕg keeps the unbounded linear value branch. FACT: a one-hidden-layer tanh head obeys |E(h)| ≤ ‖w‖₁ and ‖∇ₕE‖₂ ≤ ‖w‖₂‖W‖₂ for fixed weights, independent of input amplitude; stated limit — this does not bound the coordinate force without a bound on the descriptor's coordinate derivative.
- FACT: the radial-basis coordinate derivative opposes the remaining force at all four probed tail maxima, so a small Gaussian derivative does not imply a small tail.
- FACT: `edge_type_feat = type_src + type_dst` is added to every degree of the radial features with no radial or envelope factor and feeds the radial attention score and the value mixer even where the Gaussian features vanish, so confining the centres does not confine the edge representation (an implementation property, not a claim that the type channel explains every defect). FACT: the environment seed concatenates types with projected radial features, so zero projected radial input does not force its G network to zero; E89's gate makes G(0, type) = 0 exactly.
- FACT (by construction): the 4–7 Å tail of an isolated pair vanishes only when every input vanishes there — radial channels by their centres, the type channel by the other-neighbour fraction, the seed by the radial gate; with the type channel ungated the fixture leaves an isolated O–O pair 2.2·10⁻⁴ eV/Å at 4.6 Å.
- FACT: between 2.5 and 3.5 Å the binding energy per neighbour is positive at every coordination k = 1, 2, 4, 6 in the confined and full-range models alike, of the size of the isolated pair's barrier (O–O and H–H +0.3 to +0.8 eV). INFERENCE: the outer barrier is therefore not a property of the single-neighbour limit (not the normalization inversion of §10.13.13, not the null mass, not the seed or type paths) but the model's extrapolation to environments where every neighbour is at second-shell distance and none is bonded, absent from OMat24; its sign is what the data teach — anion–anion repulsion at 2.6–3.0 Å in oxides, hydride repulsion closer than about 2.1 Å in metal hydrides. INFERENCE: within the programme's rules it is not removable by the architecture or by anchors derived from the model itself; it needs labelled low-coordination data or a decision on the criterion. FACT: it is present at the hand-over step in every run, so the reference carries it and the anchors preserve it; a random reference is nearly flat there but is held only inside a tube of 1 eV/Å plus a tenth of its force, which a half-electron-volt barrier over one ångström passes.
- FACT: the 29394 tear reproduces in float64 on the CPU from the saved live weights (3.48·10⁶ eV/Å; frame energy −12356 eV vs −327 at 28000), so it is a real weight state, not mixed precision; the displacement from the 28000 checkpoint (norm 11.4) is exactly the run's ordinary drift (0.308·√N per checkpoint pair); along the straight line to the tear weights the frame error rises smoothly 1.9 → 64 → 1.5·10⁴ → 2.9·10⁵ → 3.5·10⁶ eV/Å — a ridge, not a pole; eight random displacements of the same norm leave the frame at 1.4–6.8 eV/Å while five realized 2000-step displacements tear it. INFERENCE: the danger lives in the low-dimensional subspace the optimizer travels, HybridMuon's orthogonalized momentum moving every singular direction at unit rate so unconstrained directions are traversed as fast as constrained ones.
- FACT: the tearing state is three-body — at the tear weights the isolated Li–H pair at 0.687 Å reads 82 eV/Å (51 at 28000) and any third atom at 1.0–3.5 Å raises it to 10²–10⁴ (3 atoms 5.8·10³, 6 atoms 1.8·10⁵, 18 atoms 1.8·10⁶), while H–H, O–O, Cu–Cu trimers read 8–74; the gates act where the other-neighbour fraction vanishes, i.e. the isolated pair, which does not tear, and the 24-pair survey and the EMA are blind to this state (39 and 32 eV/Å on the trimer vs 8947 live); the amplification is a product across the shared stack (second-block SO(2) convolution ×37, bilinear grid readout ×750, the fitting hidden layer) with no single tensor group carrying it. INFERENCE: the eligible atoms' errors at the tear were the derivative of the compressed atoms' energies with respect to their neighbours' positions, propagated through the interaction blocks, not defects of their own environments (they have ordinary sensitivity, 16–22, against 281–429 on the compressed pair).
- FACT: the anchor term is inert at the tear geometry — three of four generators put no trusted atom outside the tube on the captured batch and the 0.3 Å collision puts 0.2% (mean squared excess 0.004), while the 0.6 Å step puts 3.4% (1.42) and rel_tol 0 puts 3.5% (0.11); the frozen reference is smooth and inside the trust cap on compressed trimers at 0.30–1.00 contacts (−29 to +93 eV/Å) and is never consulted below 0.465 contacts, the smallest Li–H contact of the 200000-frame scan.
- FACT: the generators place no isolated pair below 1.0 Å in 4000 frames each (upper bound 2.5·10⁻⁴ per frame) and 0.0005–0.001 per frame at 1.0–1.5 Å, so the acceptance geometry is labelled by no anchor. FACT: the collapse is a random walk of the isolated-pair wall at 1.0–1.5 Å — the E95 control's wall force wanders by 8–97 eV/Å from 12000 on against its own raw reference (Si–O 13 → 61 → 97 → 12), the confined base runs to 10³ (C–C 3684 at 72000), and E91's composed anchors hold it in a stationary 2.5–31 eV/Å band; holed pairs changing between checkpoints is the signature of independent walks. FACT: E91's raw reference already carried the 2.2–2.9 Å wells position for position (C–C 1.770 Å, Si–O 2.124, Ti–O 2.855, Fe–O 2.060/2.845) at 0.1–1.2 eV/Å, inside the tube, so the hinge only removed the freedom to wash them out; E95's raw reference is five to nine times cleaner there (0.13 vs 1.15 eV).
- FACT: dilation invents separations no frame contains — it cuts anchor pairs below 0.8 contacts from 1.93 to 0.21 per frame and multiplies isolated pairs beyond 1.4 contacts by seven; on the E95 basis its only remaining purpose, reaching the tail, is served by the gate. A later hand-over was measured and rejected (raw checkpoints at 14000–30000 no cleaner in the 1.15–2.6-contact band, shoulders inside 0.6 contacts 11 → 39); an EMA reference was rejected because the held quantity fluctuates over 2000–6000 steps.
- FACT: with the type channel gated, one scalar function of r read from the 31 channels by `radial_embedding.net.0.matrix` (31×32) serves all 3533 element pairs, and the checkpoint-to-checkpoint change of the 24 curves is rank-one to 53–65% with positive loadings, which is why pairs hole and recover together. FACT: the channels centred at 0.0, 0.2, 0.4 Å reach 4·10⁻⁵, 9·10⁻⁴, 1·10⁻² of their peak at 1.2 Å (of 3533 pairs, 32 ever reach below 1.0 Å), so their rows' gradient is 10⁻¹⁵–10⁻²⁰ of the largest row's on ordinary frames and O(1) on a rare compressed one — a jump process — while their displacement per 2000 steps is 1.2–1.6 times that of the 2–4 Å rows from 14000 on. FACT: the descriptor of an isolated Li–H atom at 0.4 Å has norm 199 against 3–8 in crystals, which the one-layer GLU head turns linearly into +111 eV — a filter the data never trained drives the descriptor out of the head's range; nothing diverges.
- FACT: 200000 sampled frames of the 100.6 million training frames contain no He, Po, At, Rn, Fr, Ra or anything beyond Pu, and one frame each of Ne, Ar, Kr, Xe (about 500 frames of each in the whole set; one visit of such a frame every 370 steps; one visit of each per 186000-step epoch); Eu, the rarest benign element, has about 400000 frames. FACT: the embedding rows of Rn, Po, At, Fr, Ra keep their initial norms (0.42–0.55) bit for bit from step 2000 to 78000 while common rows grow (mean 0.46 → 1.40; H 0.71 → 2.22) and Kr's grows slowly (0.51 → 0.61). INFERENCE: the untouched rows become inputs unlike anything the shared network is shown and its response there grows faster than linearly as the network's gains grow; rarely present elements are held only where their frames reach (Kr's frames never place two Kr atoms 2.4 Å apart, so the Kr–Kr dimer grows a −830 eV well while the Kr embedding is trained); the anchors as designed cannot reach it, since every generator transforms geometry and keeps types.
- INFERENCE (unifying claim, §11.39): the three defects are one mechanism — a parameter, or a direction in parameter space, that no datum constrains is moved by the optimizer as fast as the constrained ones, and the function grows without bound wherever an input activates it: the innermost radial channels for the compressed pair, the embedding row for the absent element, the low-dimensional subspace the optimizer travels for the three-body state. The two remedies are to remove the unconstrained parameter or to constrain it.
- FACT: the type-axis defect reaches the tail criterion too — the confined base at 90000 fails A1 on five absent elements (Po, At, Rn, Fr, Ra). INFERENCE: the tail is clean by construction only for channels that vanish on an isolated pair; the response to an untrained embedding through the channels that do not vanish (the envelope mass every edge carries to the cutoff) grows until A1 fails.
- FACT: monotone convex input basis functions do not guarantee a single-well potential — the zero-centred scale family (each raw function bounded by one, zero slope at the origin, decreasing, convex for r ≥ 1 Å) still grows extra extrema by 4000 steps.
- FACT: validation accuracy and the extrapolation criteria decouple — the confined base's energy MAE falls monotonically 16.591 (60000) → 15.486 meV/atom (100000) while holed pairs grow to 9–19 and the first eligible tears appear; the E95 basis behaves the same.
- FACT/INFERENCE (routing, §11.41 — see Excluded): re-routing only the two radial-reading layers leaves the type axis worse than the control, while additionally re-routing the six readout matrices and the fitting network holds every absent element within 1.3 eV; the readout and the fitting network, on Muon, are where the response to an untrained embedding grows, and the Adam update bounds it without any anchor.

#### Mechanism and physics statements — the converged candidate and the isolated-atom limit, §11.44–11.47 (8–11 September)

- **FACT (measured). The dissociation limit is wrong by the pair's binding energy.** E_i = b(t_i) +
  f(x_i), b the per-element least-squares bias fitted on the training energies, f the fitting
  network. For an atom without neighbours x_i is the isolated descriptor x_iso(t), which no training
  frame contains, so f(x_iso, t) is an untrained constant and b(t) + f(x_iso, t) is the model's
  isolated-atom energy. On E118: b is 3.5 eV below E0 averaged over the 89 tabulated elements (83 of
  89 below; H −2.0, O −5.8, Si −4.4, Fe −3.5, Au −2.9) and f(x_iso) recovers only +1.8 eV for H and
  +1.7 for Au, leaving the limit 0.20 and 1.15 eV per atom too low. At the well every DeePMD model
  lies within the reference models' own spread (H₂ 0.0–0.5 eV from MACE-OMAT-0, references spread
  0.4; Au₂ 0.4–0.9, spread 0.6); at 7 Å every DeePMD model is 0.2–0.7 eV (H₂) and 2.1–2.6 eV (Au₂)
  below 2·E0.
- **FACT. Consequences.** The well disappears (Au₂ −0.03 vs −1.6…−2.3), the residue of the binding
  becomes a hump beyond the well (H₂ +0.26…+0.44 in every DeePMD model, zero in MACE), and the
  descent to the too-low limit is the force the surveys read at 3–6 Å. This identifies §11.37's
  "outer barrier of the isolated molecular pairs" — recorded there as a rule of the model at every
  coordination, not removable by architecture — as the untrained isolated-atom limit, and removable.
- **FACT (read by value). The 3 Å step is the outer gate's boundary.** The outer gate multiplies the
  channels centred beyond 3 Å by the other-neighbour fraction, zero on an isolated pair, so the
  pair's radial input beyond 3 Å is the tail of the Gaussian centred at 3.0 Å (width 0.2 Å) and
  nothing else; the descriptor reaches its isolated-atom value between 3.0 and 3.4 Å whatever the
  pair's physics, and the energy must cross to the dissociation limit inside those 0.4 Å. That
  crossing is the step (1.7–1.9 eV/Å on H₂ and Au₂, up to 9.4 on Fe–O); beyond 3.4 Å no input
  remains to produce a force, which is why the gated runs have clean tails. Without the gate the
  crossing spreads over 3–6 Å (0.9–1.2 eV/Å at 3.0, 0.2–0.6 in the tail, failing old A1 on 10 of 24
  pairs); the fade spreads it over 2.5–4.5 Å (3 of 24 above 1 eV/Å at 8000). The gate bought the
  tail criterion by compressing a transition that should not exist; the step is a property of the
  gate's boundary, not of the untrained region.
- **FACT (models and paper). How MACE and UMA avoid it.** MACE writes E_i = E0(z_i) + Σ_layers
  readout(h_i) with E0 a per-element table in the model (`atomic_energies` of mace-omat-0-medium:
  OMat24-functional isolated-atom energies, 89 elements, missing Po, At, Rn, Fr, Ra), bias-free
  readouts and message features that vanish without neighbours, so an isolated atom returns E0
  exactly (H −1.1173, Au −0.2880) and the pair dissociates to 2·E0 by construction. UMA's
  per-element references give the same limit to 0.1 eV (Au −0.184 vs its table's −0.185 at 7 Å).
  MACE-MP-0's table holds least-squares averages (63 of 89 below the DeePMD runs' fitted bias), so
  its curves were not used as a reference of the limit. UMA's scheme (arXiv 2506.23971,
  normalization appendix): E_ref = E_DFT − Σ_i [E_i,DFT − ΔH_f,i], with E_i,DFT the DFT energy of
  the isolated atom in a box computed with each dataset's own settings and ΔH_f the tabulated heat
  of formation, then a per-element least-squares reference for conditioning; UMA 1.2 also trains on
  single-atom data for every dataset and on diatomic data for OMol25 and OMat25, and answers single
  atoms at inference from the table.
- **FACT + INFERENCE. The two tables differ on open-shell elements.** They agree to 1 meV on
  main-group elements (H −1.1173/−1.1170, O −1.5484/−1.5480, Si −0.8264/−0.8262) and differ by
  0.1–3.5 eV on 62 of 89 (UMA − MACE: Fe +0.21, Ni +0.30, Cu +0.37, Co +0.51, Ce +0.47, Gd +0.80,
  Nb +0.98, U +1.06, Pu +3.46, Au +0.10; MACE lower in every large case). INFERENCE: different
  converged spin states of the isolated atoms; neither table documents its settings. The difference
  is a per-element constant the bulk energies absorb, moving only that element's limit.
- **FACT (verified). Pinning the limit is a per-element constant.** E_i = E0(t_i) + f(x_i) −
  f(x_iso, t_i); the bias of every tabulated element is overwritten with E0 after the from-scratch
  statistic (`set-by-statistic`), and every forward evaluates one atom of each type through the
  descriptor with an empty edge list and the fitting network (in SeZM's `apply_out_stat`, so the
  training model, the frozen reference and the EMA copy all obey it). The subtraction is constant in
  the coordinates — forces and virials unchanged — and differentiable in the parameters. Checks on
  E118's weights: every tabulated element alone in a box returns E0 to 10⁻⁵ eV, a pair at 7 Å
  returns 2·E0 with zero force, a dense Si₁₆O₁₆ cell's forces are unchanged to 10⁻⁷ eV/Å while its
  energy shifts by a per-element constant, untabulated Po unchanged; on a live checkpoint the pin
  holds to 5·10⁻⁷ eV and after 24 compiled steps to 4·10⁻⁸ eV; fused-kernel force-loss gradients
  agree with the reference path on all 94 parameter tensors. Untabulated elements keep the fitted
  bias and no subtraction; at fine-tuning `change-by-statistic` shifts the stored bias by the
  least-squares residual of the new data, so E0 follows the new data's energy zero.
- **FACT (measured). Cost of the extra forward.** f(x_iso, t) is coordinate-independent, so force
  and virial need no extra derivative; only the parameter gradient flows through a forward of one
  atom per element with no edges. Eager, 4000-atom batch: 15.8 ms vs 211.6 ms (7.5%, dominated by
  kernel launches). Compiled training graph, production batch (mix:10000 atoms, fused kernels, 60
  steps, same GPU): 0.4269 s per step vs 0.4240 s (0.7%); compile 174 s vs 136 s. At freeze time the
  constant folds into the bias, out_bias(t) ← E0(t) − f(x_iso, t), so inference and export carry no
  extra cost. The isolated forward carries two auxiliary atoms of type 0 joined by one edge because
  several kernels of the eager CUDA path reject an empty edge list (`reshape(n_focus, 0, -1)` in the
  SO(2) activation); they share no edge with the isolated atoms and their outputs are discarded.
- **FACT (source). PT vs TF at the time of the research.** TF has the rule as an option: with
  `atom_ener` the fitting output is evaluated once more on a zero descriptor and subtracted ("remove
  contribution in vacuum", `deepmd/tf/fit/ener.py`), then the assigned per-element energy is added.
  PT's `atom_ener`/`preset_out_bias` only assigned the bias and left f(x_iso) in place, and the frozen
  `.pt2` export differed from the patched PT model by Σ f(x_iso, t_i) over the frame (0.12 eV per
  frame after 24 steps) because `deepmd.pt_expt` was not patched. The repository landing on `outisli`
  (15 September 2026) is the fitting option `vacuum_ref` together with `preset_out_bias`: the fitting
  subtracts its own output on the vacuum descriptor of the atom's type under the atom's own
  conditioning, the descriptor supplies that vacuum descriptor by carrying one reference atom per
  type (neutral ground state) through the same forward, `preset_out_bias` fixes E0 without
  statistics in both training and `change_bias` (an element dictionary, a JSON file, or a bundled
  table such as `"omat24"`, UMA's OMat24 references shipped in `deepmd/utils/preset_out_bias_tables.json`),
  the freeze folds the reference into the bias, the pt/dpmodel backends no longer have `atom_ener`,
  and TensorFlow/Paddle reject a `vacuum_ref` model instead of ignoring the option.
- **FACT for the readings, INFERENCE for the cause. The pinned limit's energy cost and its
  closing.** At 40000 every pinned arm reads 4–10% above E118 with equal or lower force RMSE; the
  training-batch energy MAE (1000-step rows over 4000-step windows) is 31.4 / 23.9 / 21.6 / 20.1 on
  E118 at 10k/20k/30k/40k against 46–52 / 31–33.5 / 27–28.5 / 25.2–25.8 on the pinned arms (55% →
  27%), while the batch force MAE is within 2–4% of E118's from 20000 on. The excess is not
  concentrated on rare elements: grouped by the frequency of the rarest element in the frame the
  ratio pinned/E118 is 1.04–1.10 in every group above a hundred frames (s43: 15.55 vs 14.17 on the
  18813 frames whose rarest element appears in 2–5% of frames; 9.66 vs 9.07 on 1262 common-element
  frames; 21.5 vs 20.1 on 475 frames with an element seen in under 1%). INFERENCE: with the limit
  pinned every per-type constant direction of the network cancels in f(x_i) − f(x_iso, t), so each
  element's mean cohesive energy (−3…−8 eV per atom from the free atom) must be learned through the
  environment-dependent directions all elements share; a per-type constant switched on by the
  neighbours would restore the fitted mean but place a step of the cohesive energy's size wherever
  the switch turns — the wrong-limit hump in another form. The cost is one of convergence, not of
  design: the gap closes by about a fifth per 20000 steps and ends at +1.5/+2.3% (E139), +1.0/+1.8%
  (E142) and +1.2/+2.0% (E147) against E118, or +2.5/+3.0% against E137 (the same routing on the
  least-squares limit).
- **FACT. The fade-edge tail is the environment seed's type-only output.** The fade multiplies every
  radial basis value and the additive type feature of a nearly isolated edge by f_ij = g_ij +
  (1 − g_ij)·s(r_ij), so an isolated pair presents no radial or type input beyond 4.5 Å; but the
  seed network evaluates SiLU(radial + types), and its type rows give a distance-independent output
  per edge once the radial rows are faded to zero, which the downstream envelope removes only at the
  6 Å cutoff. Measured: E142 s43 at 80000 keeps K–F bound by −0.57 eV at 4.5, −0.36 at 4.75, −0.09
  at 5.0, zero from 5.5 Å, tail-force maximum 1.29 eV/Å at 4.78 Å against the unit-charge bound of
  0.63 there (0.75 at 40000; 0.09 eV of residual binding at 4.5 Å at 20000 — the type-only channel
  is learned into the ionic attraction as training proceeds). E133 and E147, the same fade with the
  seed gate — SiLU(radial)·sigmoid(types), which vanishes when the radial projection does — read
  K–F exactly zero from 4.5 Å at every survey; E139 without the fade is bound by −2.0 eV at 4.5 Å
  with 2 eV/Å at 5.3–5.7 Å. The seed gate is therefore the structural closure of the fade, not a
  compensation for the wrong limit. The feature is not only ionic: E142 s42 adds O–O from 90000 with
  a +0.14 eV maximum at 4.59 Å — the same channel as a barrier on a covalent pair.
- **INFERENCE (from where the maximum sits). The ionic pair against a 6 Å cutoff.** An isolated
  ionic pair's Coulomb tail runs to the ion–neutral crossing near 15 Å, so with the fade ending at
  4.5 Å it must reach the limit inside the fade window; as training deepens the ionic well (K–F
  −4.9 eV at 2.2 Å) its outer wall steepens against the fade's edge, and the tail-force maximum is
  always at 4.50 Å, the window's first point. MACE-OMAT-0 shows the same compression against its
  6 Å cutoff; E143 at 28000 reads K–F 0.34 eV/Å; the failure grows with training on every seed and
  spreads from K–F to Na–Cl, Ca–O, Li–O, Si–O, Zn–S, O–O. Recorded as a limitation of any pair fade
  shorter than the Coulomb range.
- **INFERENCE with measured support (11 Sep). Envelope compensation.** With the single C³ envelope
  (exponent 5; 0.43 at 4 Å, 0.25 at 4.5, 0.07 at 5) the network learns raw edge energies large
  enough to reproduce the crystals' second-neighbour interactions through the envelope's tail, and
  an isolated pair then shows that raw energy until the envelope collapses. Measured: the no-fade
  arms hold the physical ionic energy up to 4.5 Å (K–F E(4.5) − E(7) = −2.05 eV for E155 and −1.51
  for E156 at 8000, against −2.3 for a unit-charge pair) and release it inside the cutoff envelope's
  last ångström. The fade cuts the compensation at 4.5 Å; the seed gate halves it (E154 K–F F(4.5) =
  −1.2 at 6000) without holding it; the reference term cannot see it, because the hand-over freezes
  a reference that already carries the failing tails and its 1 eV/Å tolerance is above the whole
  tail scale — the reference term preserves rather than removes them. DPA4 no-fade tails are three
  to five times the reference models' at the same separations and grow with training, which is the
  defect. Two remedies remain that act where the compensation happens (neither launched): a direct
  bound on the isolated-pair force beyond 4.5 Å — E67's tail term with the unit-charge Coulomb bound
  as the hinge instead of zero force, a physical inequality supplied where no data exist — and the
  data route of E159, which does not touch the tails.
- **INFERENCE. The needle mechanism at batch scale.** The innermost radial rows receive gradient only
  from the rare compressed contacts; Muon's orthogonalized step spreads such a rare direction across
  the matrix at unit rate, whereas Adam's per-coordinate normalization gives the row a bounded step
  and its decoupled decay pulls the unconstrained rows to zero. The substitution, which adds
  compressed and re-typed anchor frames, adds to the rare directions and raises the count.
- **INFERENCE (six arms at 8000 steps, confirmed by every later survey). The extreme-short-range dive
  follows the fitting network's optimizer.** With the limit pinned the fitting network carries the
  whole cohesive energy (−3…−8 eV per atom from the free atom) instead of a residual around a
  least-squares mean; on the AdamW path its rows for descriptor values the data never visit are
  moved by their own tiny gradient history and by the decoupled decay, while on Muon its update is
  spectrally bounded across all rows. Measured: every pinned arm with the fitting network on AdamW
  carries 17–23 dived pairs before its hand-over and keeps 4–21 afterwards on at least one seed;
  E139, with fitting and readout on Muon, never exceeds 2.
- **FACT. What the pinned runs' "holes" are.** At 22000, of E133 s43's 18 flagged pairs two have a
  resolved minimum inside the grid (Fe–O −15.9 at 0.27 contact behind a +16 eV wall at 0.47), one is
  a rim hole (O–O −12.8 at 0.36 contact behind +10), and fifteen are flagged because the energy keeps
  falling at the scan's inner edge below a wall of 16–30 eV (Cu–Cu +26 at 0.41 contact, Au₂ +30 at
  0.49, Ni₂ +23 at 0.41) with no minimum on the grid. E134 reads 12–16 edge dives, 4–5 resolved dives
  of −5…−45 eV at 0.26–0.3 contact behind +8…+23 eV walls, and 0–1 rim holes. E132, the same gates
  without the reference, has none: its Au₂ wall is +44 eV at 0.39 contact and the energy keeps rising
  inside. So the reference does not open the rim (0.35–0.6 contact stays clean but for shallow
  Cr–Cr/Mo–Mo minima); it lets the energy fall again inside the wall, below 0.35 contact — the region
  the user has placed inside the future ZBL range.
- **FACT. Cr–Cr and Mo–Mo flags are real bonds.** Cr₂'s bond length is 1.68 Å and Mo₂'s 1.94 Å, which
  the survey's metallic-radius contacts (2.78 and 3.08 Å) place inside the 0.6-contact hole boundary;
  MACE-OMAT-0 shows the same Cr–Cr double well.
- **FACT for the construction, INFERENCE for the consequence. MACE-OMAT-0's architecture.** Cutoff
  6 Å, two interaction layers, 10 fixed Bessel functions with an Agnesi distance transform and a
  polynomial cutoff of order 5, a radial MLP [10, 64, 64, 64] per layer, correlation order 3, a
  linear readout on the first layer and a 16-unit MLP on the second, 9.06 M parameters. A layer's
  energy is a polynomial of degree at most three in the sum over neighbours, with no per-edge
  attention weight or gate — the structural reason a third atom cannot multiply a pair's force the
  way the SeZM attention and gated SO(2) chain can (§11.39's torn states).
- **FACT (paper). UMA appendix G separates smoothness from accuracy.** UMA 1 and 1.1 "struggled" on
  diatomic curves and on the OMat task "produced large energy fluctuations beyond the bonding
  region", cause unattributed. An unreleased UMA-1.2 trained "without any diatomic data" with the
  1.2 procedure and data ratios gives "qualitatively smooth" OMat and OMol curves (figure 8).
  Diatomic data were added for accuracy: OMat diatomic energy MAE 128.6 meV and force MAE
  279.6 meV/Å without them, 37.8 meV and 52.5 meV/Å with them (table 26). Two defects remain in the
  released model: the OMol asymptote is wrong beyond 5.5 Å because the global charge and spin are
  assigned to both separated fragments once the envelope has cut the edge, and a small spurious
  barrier at 1.8–1.9 Å on N–N attributed to training data. The 1.1→1.2 changes (A.7, unattributed):
  fp32 end to end, single-atom predictions with single-atom frames for every dataset, 32 instead of
  64 radial basis functions, task-specific heads, and OMat24 split into AIMD and rattled subsets
  with their own ratios and loss weights. The single-atom frames pin the isolated-atom limit in the
  loss the way this recipe pins it in the model. The diatomic data are not published (fairchem
  carries only `diatomic_tests.py`), so adding them would mean computing dimer curves with OMat24's
  VASP settings, excluded by the no-new-labels constraint of §1.
- **FACT. The fine-tune scenario differs from pretraining in two ways.** The reference is a trained
  model rather than a random initialization, so the reference term pins the anchors to a physical
  function from the first step and no hand-over exists; and the architecture is inherited from the
  base checkpoint, so the gates, the fixed basis and the single envelope enter only through the
  base. Only the training side can be varied. Every geometric generator — Gaussian shell, collision,
  dilation, deletion, the composed sequence — starts from a data frame and never produces an
  isolated pair at a compressed or stretched separation, so they delay and do not prevent the
  collapse; the pair anchor, which constructs that geometry and holds it to the frozen reference's
  own curve, keeps the base's dimer through 120000 steps. INFERENCE offered 9 Sep 01:45, not
  measured: the gates that bound the pretraining rim leave the isolated pair's energy to fewer
  channels, which an all-hydrogen fine-tune on compressed cells drives harder than the old
  architecture's shared channels.

## 11. Record — criteria and instruments as they were defined

The definitions, thresholds and units in force at each stage, including the acceptance table of the reference-term programme.

#### Criteria and instruments — the early record, §1–§10.12 (28 August – 3 September)

- **Curvature ratio** `|c|/κ` (per atom `|g_i|/κ_i`), dimensionless; ceiling `κ_i = k₀ + min(|F_i|, F_cap)/ρ₀`, `k₀ = 300 eV/Å²`, `ρ₀ = 0.1 Å`, `F_cap = 100 eV/Å`. Above 1 = a defect; healthy models read ≤ 0.83 on every set measured. Physical yardstick `k_phys ≈ 36 eV/Å²`.
- **Dimer scan** (`dimer_scan.py`): two atoms in a 20 Å cell, 0.4 to 7 Å in 0.005 Å steps. *Well depth* (minimum minus energy at 7 Å; true H₂ binding −4.4 eV); largest *curvature outside the repulsive wall* (beyond 1.2 Å; H₂ stretch constant 33 eV/Å²); *tail force*, the largest force in 4–7 Å where the true interaction is a few meV — **anything above ~0.05 eV/Å is an artefact** (pretrained values 0.02–0.06 eV/Å).
- **Hill-climb amplification**: factor by which a 40-evaluation search within 0.05 Å raises the largest force; 3× healthy, hundreds signal a defect.
- **`L_max`**: largest per-atom rate of change of force along the force direction, eV/Å².
- **Labelled-contact table** (`shortrange_scan.py`): force error and *bias* of the force component toward the nearest neighbour (positive = model pushes harder than DFT), binned by distance relative to the covalent-radius sum (0.62 Å H–H, 1.32 Å O–O); negative bias on the closest bins = softening.
- **Compression probe** (`compress_probe.py`); **dimer wall** (`dimer_wall.py`, against the ZBL universal repulsion, below which no realistic potential should fall); **anchor geometry** (`anchor_geometry.py`); **hinge activity** (`anchor_hinge.py`); **contact census** (`contact_census.py`); **frame inspection** (`inspect_frames.py`); **contact anchor probe** (`contact_anchor_probe.py`); **hard-contact series** (`hard_contact_series.py`).
- Other scripts: `repro_spike.py` (`--interp`), `locate_amplifier.py`, `frame_continuity.py`, `frame_jump_force.py`, `atom49_pes.py`, `spike_density.py`, `h_train_scan.py`, `h_spike_eval.py`, `pro_scan_fast.py`, `pro_scan.py`, `mindist_scan.py`, `bond_scan.py`, `attn_stats.py`, `weight_spectra.py`, `activation_subspace.py`, `curvature_signal.py`, `traj_curvature.py`, `amp_noise.py`, `heal_spike.py`, `heal_pro.py`, `eval_model.py`, `train_curv.py`, `train_refresh.py`, `arch_chain.sh`, `summarize_arch.py`, `make_zbl_dataset.py`, `neo_eval.sh`, `omat_eval_chain.sh`.

______________________________________________________________________

#### Criteria and instruments — the refreshed reference, §10.13 first half (2–6 September)

- **A1 (dimer tail force).** Largest spurious radial force on the isolated dimer in the 4-7 Å tail, where the physical force is zero; absolute bound 0.05 eV/Å. No from-scratch hydrogen run meets it, plain included (0.06-0.10); tail *curvature* is 0.1-1.2 eV/Å² in every such run, far below the ceiling, so readings are quoted relative to the plain run at matched steps.
- **A2 (bounded, single minimum, monotone wall).** Curvature must stay below the ceiling `κ = 300 + min(F, 100)/0.1`, which allows at most 1300 eV/Å² inside 1.2 Å. Wall = inside 1.2 Å; tail = beyond 1.2 Å; both in eV/Å².
- **A3 / A4** cover the well's position and shape (runs "fail A3" when the well is displaced; "A3 and A4 fail" when the short range is rough or needled).
- **Schedule (§12.5).** B5 and B6 at 10000 and 20000 steps; C9 and A2-A3 at 20000 and 30000 steps; the rest along the run.
- **Physical dimer reference.** H2 well −4.5 eV at 0.74 Å; on the hydrogen testbed this is extrapolation, so only A2 and A4 apply there.
- **Contact-anchor probe (§10.11.2).** Radial force on a frame's closest pair read in the full frame, in the frame dilated 1.3×, with 90% of the other atoms deleted, and for the pair alone; the dilated variant changes the distance and is expected smaller, the other three must agree.
- **Hardest-13.** Force RMSE on the 13 hardest frames of §10.11.2, given as the range over the checkpoints of the preceding 8000 steps with the largest single error in brackets, eV/Å.
- **F(0.6 Å).** Carried in the O2 cell from 5 September: radial force on the isolated O2 dimer at 0.6 Å, positive when repulsive. Plain twin +53…+201 eV/Å between its holes; dense-recipe twins −2…−28.
- **Label-widened tube.** `train_refresh.py --label-tube-after-refresh` runs the reference once more on the source batch at every anchor event after the hand-over, records the per-atom error against the label and subtracts it from the excess. Checked on a synthetic batch (the penalty reduces to the original hinge when no error is recorded, vanishes where the recorded error exceeds the deviation, decreases monotonically with it) and smoke-tested on a 40-step run.
- **Hand-over flags.** `--ref-refresh S --ref-refresh-max 1` (prototype); `train_refresh.py --refresh S --max-refreshes 1` (package). After-HO generator flags: `--dense-after-refresh`, `--squeeze-after-refresh 0.5`, `--collision-after-refresh`, `--near-after-refresh`, `--drop-after-refresh 0`, `--dilate-after-refresh 1.0`, `--families-after-refresh`, `--pin-sparse-after-refresh`, `--tol-after-refresh`, `--rel-after-refresh`, `--pref-after-refresh`, `--compose-generators`, `--refresh-steps a,b`, `--ensemble-refs`, `--anchor-descent N --anchor-descent-step X`, `--pair-squeeze lo,hi`, `--fstep-after-refresh`, `--label-tube-near-only`.
- **Instrumentation and launchers.** Batch-error log (`--log-batch-error`) and needle dump; verdicts quote window needle counts, tear-class events with magnitude in eV/Å and the step, and a gain probe (×477, ×4389 / ×38373, ×70088). Scripts: `slurm_twin.sh`, `slurm_h.sh` (cluster), `remote_twin.sh`, `remote_h.sh` (management node), `local_twin.sh`, `local_h.sh` (local).

#### Criteria and instruments — the refreshed reference, §10.13 second half (3–6 September)

- **Contact-anchor probe (§10.11.2)** -- the radial force on the closest pair of a frame, read four ways: full frame, dilated 1.3x, 90% of the other atoms deleted, pair alone. The dilated variant changes the distance and is expected smaller; the other three must agree. Probed frames Cu-I (16547), Ca-H (a 30-atom frame), Ag-Ag. Series logs `contact_anchor_probe_<frame>_series.log` (every 2000 steps) and `_series_late.log`.
- **B5** -- the isolated pair must agree with its frame value; working band **20%**. Failures are recorded with direction: stiff (isolated above the frame) or soft/collapse (below).
- **C9 (hardest frames)** -- force RMSE on the 13 hardest frames of §10.11.2, eV/Å, as the range over the checkpoints of the preceding 8000 steps, largest single error in brackets. Scatter: **±0.3 eV/Å between repeats of one recipe at 20000 steps**, and a factor 2 on one recipe at one step (E3 repeat 0.82 / 0.48 vs the dense twin's 1.30 / 1.03). A difference below a factor 2 between single readings of two runs is therefore not attributable to their recipes; a ranking stands only where a series holds over many readings, and comparisons are made on the series from 40000 on, never on one early reading.
- **A1 (dimer tail)** -- the largest spurious force in the 4-7 Å tail of the H2 and O2 dimers, eV/Å, H2 / O2; absolute bound **0.05 eV/Å**; tail curvature stays at 0.1-1.7 eV/Å^2, far below the ceiling. The plain run reads 0.05-0.15 on H2 and 0.29-0.40 on O2 through 142000 steps, so the absolute bound is out of reach of every from-scratch run and readings are taken relative to the plain run at matched steps. The curvature half passes in every run; the force half is failed by every from-scratch run, and no twin is distinguishable from the plain run within the scatter.
- **A2 (H2 wall)** -- `E / F`: energy at 0.45 Å above the well in eV, repulsive force at 0.6 Å in eV/Å, from the same dimer scans. Plain run 2.9-3.6 eV and 8-9 eV/Å. `F(0.6 Å)` on O2 is the radial force on the isolated O2 dimer at 0.6 Å, positive when repulsive. The O2 wall carries a dip high on its wall in every from-scratch run.
- **A3** -- wells must reach the physical values; working band **20%** on depth. **A4** -- no spurious minimum beyond the well.
- **Schedule (§12.5)** -- B5 and B6 at 10000 and 20000 steps; C9 and A2-A3 at 20000 and 30000 steps; the rest along the run. A dot in the tables is a value not recorded.
- **Bulk accuracy** -- from `subset_stats.py` on `fastscan_sub001val_<step>.npz` (1% subset), or `pro_scan_fast.py` on `sub001val` for single-step scans: force RMSE without the three worst frames (eV/Å), median per-frame force RMSE (eV/Å), energy MAE (meV/atom), frames with an error above 5 eV/Å.
- **Batch-error log / needle dump** -- per-call peak per-atom force error (and virial error) on the training batch; the cluster twins and the plain two-GPU twin share the data order, so logs are comparable call by call. Needles are counted above **100 eV/Å**.
- **Twin definition** -- the `-s` run of §10.12 (production configuration on OMat24, two GPUs, EMA checkpoints every 2000 steps to `runs/<twin>/ema_<step>.pt`) with the reference handed over at 10000 steps (`train_refresh.py --refresh 10000 --max-refreshes 1`). Up to the hand-over every twin is the `-s` run itself, whose 10000-step checkpoint holds Cu-I at 52/41/53/53 eV/Å and Ca-H at 50/18/54/62 with 0.89 eV/Å on the 13 hardest frames.
- **Pair term (E2)** -- at every anchor event the closest pair of every batch frame is evaluated alone in the frame's own cell and its radial force held to the label's radial force on that pair inside the frame: `relu(|f_pair - f_label| - 5 - 1.0*|f_label|)^2`. Fires on 0.1-0.4% of the closest pairs during the random phase; the tighter form (tol 1, rel 0.5) fires on 5%.
- **Label-widened tube** -- `tube = tol + rel_tol * |F_ref| + |F_ref - F_label|`, the widening being the hand-over reference's own error on that atom in the frame the anchor was made from.

#### Criteria and instruments — the optimizer and the candidate ledger, §10.14–10.15 (2–6 September)

- **A1 — tail force**: the largest spurious radial force in the 4–7 Å window of an isolated dimer; an absolute bound. Plain hydrogen 0.11–0.15 eV/Å; plain OMat24 O₂ 0.21–0.47 eV/Å. E67 targets < 0.15 eV/Å at 40000.
- **A2 — no hole**: no minimum of the isolated-pair curve inside 0.6 covalent contacts lower than anything outside it. Read on `dimer_survey.py` from 6 September (24 element pairs, 0.25–1.6 contacts; later scanned to 7 Å with the pair at 7 Å as the zero and a tail column). Exempt inside the range ZBL will cover.
- **A3 / A4 — well and smoothness**: the well at its physical place and depth (H₂ −4.5 eV at 0.74 Å; O₂ ≈ −4.5…−5 eV at 1.21–1.25 Å) and no spurious curvature feature; wall curvature below 1600 eV/Å² at 20000 was the hydrogen gate.
- **Window rule (28000–40000)**: counted over both ranks' `batch_max_error_rank*.log` on the largest per-atom force error per training step, threshold 100 eV/Å; revised §10.13.6 to **no tear (> 1000 eV/Å) and at most about five small needles (100–1000)**. Plain twin 17 small + 5 tears.
- **Accuracy rule**: within 2 % of the plain twin on the 1 % validation subset; at 40000 the limits are trimmed force RMSE 0.218 eV/Å and energy MAE 15.6 meV/atom. Reported as trimmed force RMSE (three worst frames removed) / median per-frame RMSE / energy MAE (meV/atom) / frames above 5 eV/Å, with the untrimmed value alongside from 5 September …
- **Hardest-13**: RMSE in eV/Å over the thirteen hardest legitimate validation contacts (pairs at 0.43–0.50 of the covalent contact), with the largest single per-atom error alongside. Dense recipe 1.0–1.6; plain twin 0.41–1.31 in its window; E43 0.34–0.48 at 40000.
- **F(0.6 Å), the O₂ wall column**: the radial force on the isolated O₂ dimer at 0.6 Å, positive being the sign a wall has; a standing readout from §10.13.1.
- **Inner band**: the largest force inside 0.62 Å and the number of sign changes of the force there on the isolated dimer; clean = at most 2 sign changes with the largest force below 100 eV/Å. Adopted §10.13.9 as the leading indicator the window count is not.
- **Compressed-probe gain (E33, `gain_probe.py`)**: the RMS force change on 64 label-free two-atom probes (each batch frame's closest pair alone in its cell, compressed to 0.45 of the contact) plus O₂ at 0.465 Å and H₂, over the RMS force change on the batch's own frames, under a random weight perturbation the size of one step; median of three draws …
- **Scatter threshold (E19)**: a difference is attributed only when it exceeds the spread of same-seed repeats over the same steps or holds as a series; the factor-2 spread is an empirical warning, not a statistical threshold; three draws to screen, five to promote; successive checkpoints of one run are correlated, not replicates.
- **Isolated-dimer reference table**: every survey minimum inside 0.85 of the true bond or deeper than the true dissociation energy + 3 eV is flagged; Ti–O −6.5 eV at 1.62 Å is the physical TiO bond and N₂ at 1.11 Å with −8…−10 eV is N₂.
- **Instruments**: `needle_summary.py`, `needle_probe.py`, `classify_needles.py`, `increment_probe.py`, `gain_probe.py`, `dimer_survey.py`, `pair_contact_stats.py`, `anchor_reach.py`, `h_contact_audit.py`, `penalty_step.py`, `curvature_split.py`, `probe_live.sh`, `twin_eval.sh`, `subset_stats.py`, `dens_to_ener_ckpt.py` …

#### Criteria and instruments — the programme, §0 and §11.1–11.26 (6–7 September)

**Track A** (OMat24 pretraining, `neo_1gpu_*`, 40000 steps ≈ 4–5 h, then continued). Pass: no tear outside both contact exclusions and no excess eligible needle count against the matched baseline; no hole or unphysical extremum outside both exclusions at any checkpoint (distance below 1 Å or below 0.6 covalent contacts is informational); tail ≤ 0.05 eV/Å; accuracy within 2 % of the matched baseline. A candidate holding to 40000 continues to 100000–200000, where the late failures of §10.13 appeared.

**Track B** (hydrogen from scratch, `h_1gpu_*`, 40000 steps ≈ 4 h). Measured at every 2000-step checkpoint: H₂ well position and depth, wall at 0.45 Å, inner band, 4–7 Å tail, mid-range curvature, validation error, twelve 1000 K Langevin runs. Pass: well within 0.70–0.78 Å and no hole outside the ZBL range at any checkpoint; tail ≤ 0.05 eV/Å; stable dynamics; validation within 2 %. It ranks candidates and never closes a track-A candidate, because the plain recipe itself collapses here (a −183 eV hole at 40000).

**Track C** (hydrogen fine-tuning, `h_ft_1gpu_*`, from the four-generator pretrained model at 660000 steps). Pass: hydrogen accuracy at least the plain fine-tune's; no needle above the plain fine-tune's; no pair the pretrained model had physical that the fine-tune holes; the tail unchanged.

**Named criteria.** A1 = tail ≤ 0.05 eV/Å, absolute and independent of the baseline ("the criterion is absolute and the baseline is context"). A2 = no hole; the heavier of the two ("a hole is what molecular dynamics falls into, a soft displaced well is a soft surface"). A3 = the physical bond is present (a well flattened out to 2.380 Å fails A3). Needle classes: small needle 100–1000 eV/Å, tear above 1000 eV/Å, from the batch-error log at every step with contact geometry for every large-error atom.

**Contact exclusions.** Two definitions: absolute nearest-neighbour distance below 1 Å, and covalent-contact ratio below 0.6. An atom outside *both* is unambiguously in the required region; a short contact at the worst atom does not exclude a different large-error atom in the same frame; the hydrogen bond-position check stays separate. Absolute distance alone does not establish that an event lies outside the future ZBL region (the 1.324151 Å Sb–Er contact is 0.4037 contacts).

**Isolated-pair (dimer) survey.** 24 element pairs, energy zero at 7 Å, maximum spacing 0.005 Å with exact boundary samples. `dimer_survey.py` lists every local minimum and maximum beyond 1.5 Å and counts the pairs carrying one — added 6 September after a false H–H minimum at 2.55 Å passed unnoticed, the summary line having reported only well, wall, inner band and largest tail force.

**Needle statistic.** After the restart-numbering repair the statistic is the whole event history to the run's current step, quoted with that step and compared at matched steps, not inside a fixed window. `needle_window.py` aligns each log session with its restart checkpoint (stored optimizer index = filename step − 1) and repairs pre-fix logs by treating a decrease in the step column as a restart.

**Accuracy instruments and budget.** OMat24: all 21742 frames (416677 atoms) of `/data/Datasets/LMDB/OMat24/sub001val.lmdb`, every index exactly once and finite; `matrix_accuracy.sh` writes per-frame arrays and a completion marker. Report untrimmed force RMSE and a bulk statistic on a fixed frame mask shared with the baseline (excluding baseline frame IDs 16346, 18579, 16547).

**Diagnostics named.** `diagnose/attention_response.py` (bit-identical forward; separately detaches envelope input, logits or complete attention weights — the differences are intervention effects, not additive contributions); `diagnose/needle_geometry.py` (exact periodic neighbour lists including central-atom images; closest absolute distance and smallest covalent ratio, not necessarily the same neighbour); `diagnose/tear_amplitude.py`; `diagnose/message_response.py`.

**Anchor baseline ("四件套", `rf10k1D`).** Random initialization as reference, replaced once by the model at 10000 steps, then fixed. After the handover each frame draws deletion (0–90 % of atoms), Gaussian displacement (σ 0.1–0.2 Å), collision (against the source force by up to 0.3 Å), or dilation (1.0–2.0×).

**Reading rules.** Place a cell beside the baseline at the same step before calling it a failure (§11.6). Notifications mark that a reading is ready; the file is the reading (§11.7). Hydrogen results do not rank OMat24 cells (hydrogen inputs use `edge_norm: false`, OMat24 `[false, true, true]`). A quiet but unbound dimer is not accepted.

#### Criteria and instruments — the structural experiments, §11.27–11.43 (7–8 September)

- **A1 (tail)**: maximum absolute spurious force of an isolated dimer over 4–7 Å ≤ 0.05 eV/Å; reported as failing tails out of 24 pairs per checkpoint plus the largest tail.
- **Exclusions**: an absolute-distance rule (inside 1 Å) and a contact rule (contact ratio = separation ÷ sum of covalent radii, below 0.6 contacts). Anything inside either is informational; criteria are judged outside both. A tear is eligible when its trigger sits inside the ZBL range but the damaged atoms do not (§11.36).
- **Holes / inward collapse**: spurious deep minima of an isolated pair inside the exclusions (Li–H −34.126 eV at 0.693 Å, 0.43 contacts; Na–Cl −20.824 at 0.765; Al–Al −50.976 at 0.735), counted as holed pairs per checkpoint; sustained growth is the inward collapse.
- **Eligible extra extrema**: local maxima or minima outside both exclusions and beyond the principal well, above a 1 meV prominence threshold; reported as the number of pairs carrying one, with prominence (eV) and position (Å).
- **Tear**: per-atom force error above 1000 eV/Å on an atom outside both exclusions. **Small needle**: an eligible 100–1000 eV/Å per-atom error. Batch counters: batches above 20 and above 100 eV/Å per interval, and per 1000 steps. `read_matrix.py`'s `tear>1A` column classifies by the frame's shortest distance and cannot see the propagation class.
- **24-pair dimer survey**: float64, 24 pairs, maximum spacing 0.005 Å, energy zero at 7 Å, every 2000 steps to 40000, on EMA weights; refinements at 0.001 and 0.00025 Å.
- **All-element homonuclear scan** (`dimer_survey.py`, `runs/<run>/homo_scan/`): 94 homonuclear pairs H–Pu at 0.01 Å spacing; readings are the number of pairs whose principal well lies below −15 eV, which must be zero (no real dimer binds by more than about 11 eV), and the largest eligible extra extremum over the 94 pairs. Joins every completed screen from 8 September.
- **Trimer probe** (`diagnose/trimer_probe.py`): for eighteen pair types, the force on a pair compressed to 0.45 contacts with one same-type neighbour 2.0 Å from one of its atoms, beside the pair alone, on every checkpoint.
- **Per-row drift** (`diagnose/radial_row_drift.py`): displacement of each row of the first radial layer relative to the hand-over, in units of its own norm, and per 2000 steps.
- **Shell response** (`diagnose/shell_response.py`): k neighbours of one element on a sphere of radius r (one; two opposite; triangle; tetrahedron; octahedron); binding energy per neighbour = [E(centre + k) − E(centre) − k·E(neighbour)]/k in float64 with the model's own isolated-atom energies; shell atoms are 1.73, 1.63, 1.41 times r apart for k = 3, 4, 6, so rows above r = 2.25, 2.3, 2.65 Å are pure centre–shell readings on the 0–3 Å support.
- **Parameter sensitivity**: Frobenius norm of the derivative of an atom's force with respect to every trainable parameter. **Tear replay** (`diagnose/tear_replay.py`): re-evaluates a captured batch in float64 on raw and averaged checkpoints around the event, the implicated pair alone, and the sensitivity.
- **Full validation**: `sub001val.lmdb`, 21742 distinct frames, 416677 atoms, each saved index present exactly once; energy MAE = frame mean of absolute energy error per atom (meV/atom), force-vector MAE and RMSE atom-count weighted (eV/Å), largest validation force error also reported. Budget within 2%; same-seed scatter ≈0.5% on the mean metrics; the force RMSE is frame-dominated; no frame is removed from a reported metric.
- **Anchor/reference machinery**: hinge tolerance 1 eV/Å + 10% of the reference force, trust cap 100 eV/Å; frozen reference = the raw 10000-step weights, handed over once at 10000 with the switch to dense and sparse generators; checkpoints audited for completed anchor events (200 per 2000 steps), none discarded, finite parameters, 154 reference tensors bit-identical to the run's own raw 10000-step model.
- **Screen and acceptance**: 40000 steps with all twenty surveys and complete validation before the GPU is released; acceptance requires clean eligible pair curves, no eligible tears, ≤2% accuracy cost and replicated 100000–200000-step trajectories. **Collapse-remedy rule (10:45, 8 Sep)**: no holed pair at 32000–40000, no imported well, validation within 2%. Preference rule: the simpler member of each pair (collision step over undilated composition; routing over substitution) when it holds the criteria to 100000 within budget.
- **Integration fixtures** for every new option: CPU float64 equation check, NumPy/PyTorch parity, numerical first- and second-gradient checks, exact restoration when disabled, independence of an existing model from later option changes, a 24-step compiled OMat24 run with `DP_CUDA_TRAIN=1` and `DP_TRITON_TRAIN=1`, a restart to step 36 preserving all 154 reference tensors and every structural flag, CUDA force-loss parity over all 94–95 trainable parameter tensors, and PT2 freeze/load parity reproduced by a process importing no experimental module (typically 1e-7 eV, 1e-9 eV/Å, 1e-9 eV). They cover the training and Python PT2 paths, not the C++ LAMMPS path, accuracy or prevention.
- **Instrument watchers**: `instruments_watch.sh` scans every new checkpoint of the continuations and named runs with the all-element scan and the trimer probe.

#### Criteria and instruments — the converged candidate and the isolated-atom limit, §11.44–11.47 (8–11 September)

- **A1 (original).** Over the 24 isolated pairs, the spurious force in the 4–7 Å tail must not exceed
  0.05 eV/Å. Retained in every survey record (`tail_pass`, `tail_max_force`) and the historical
  tables.

- **A1 (revised, 9 September 14:05).** From 4.5 Å to the cutoff the energy must approach the
  dissociation limit monotonically — no extremum with a prominence above 5 meV — and the force must
  nowhere exceed the force between two unit point charges, 14.4/r² eV/Å (0.71 eV/Å at 4.5 Å, 0.40 at
  6 Å). Rationale: an absolute force bound cannot separate physics from artefact, because MACE's
  ionic Coulomb tails (K–F 0.60, Na–Cl 0.43 eV/Å at 4.5–5 Å) are larger than the wrong-limit slopes
  of the ungated arms (≤0.3); what separates them is shape — a physical tail is the monotone decay
  of a well's outer wall, a spurious one carries wiggles (11–15 extrema in 4.5–7 Å on the ungated
  arms, none on the pinned runs) or exceeds any force two atoms can exert. Candidate rules compared
  (failing pairs of 24):

  | Rule                                                            | MACE | UMA | E134@2k | E133@2k | E118@100k | no outer gate@40k | gate-free fixed Gaussian@40k |
  | --------------------------------------------------------------- | ---- | --- | ------- | ------- | --------- | ----------------- | ---------------------------- |
  | 4.5–7 Å, \|F\| ≤ 0.05                                           | 20   | 24  | 20      | 0       | 0         | —                 | —                            |
  | 5–7 Å, \|F\| ≤ 0.2                                              | 3    | 12  | 0       | 0       | 0         | —                 | —                            |
  | 4.5–7 Å, monotone (no extremum above 5 meV) and \|F\| ≤ 14.4/r² | 3    | 14  | 0       | 0       | 0         | 7                 | 7                            |
  | 4.5–7 Å, monotone and \|F\| ≤ 0.3                               | 7    | 19  | 0       | 0       | 0         | —                 | —                            |

  Under the adopted rule MACE-OMAT-0 fails on 3 pairs of its own (6–21 meV bumps on Cr–Cr and Mo–Mo
  at 5.0–5.3 Å; K–F's Coulomb tail near 5.5 Å), UMA on 14, the ungated wrong-limit arms on 7, the
  pinned runs at 2000 steps on none. The pinned arms' K–F borderline is 0.71–0.75 eV/Å near 4.5 Å
  against the bound of 0.711, monotone — an excess of 0.005–0.035 eV/Å (1–5%). Written by
  `dimer_survey.py` as `tail45_*`, reported by `watch_run.py` as `tail45_failures` beside
  `tail_failures`, printed by `ext_read.py`; the old reading stays.

- **Hole (`inner_hole`).** The lowest energy inside 0.6 of the contact distance lies more than
  0.05 eV below the lowest energy outside it. Two sub-kinds from 9 September: a **rim hole** (a
  resolved minimum near 0.35–0.6 contact behind a wall) and an **edge dive** (the energy still
  falling at the scan's inner edge, 0.25 contact, no minimum on the grid), the latter inside the
  region the user places within the future ZBL range.

- **Tear.** Per-atom force error above 1000 eV/Å in a training batch, logged with the tear model and
  the batch. **Smaller events**: 100 < error ≤ 1000 eV/Å. A separate column counts tears beyond a
  1 Å contact.

- **Needle.** A batch whose largest per-atom force error exceeds 20 eV/Å; counted over 10000–20000,
  10000–40000 (of 30000 logged batches) and 10000–100000, with >50 and >100 eV/Å columns.
  Fixed-seed scatter ≈25 batches over 10000–40000; between draws ≈30%.

- **Extra extrema / hump.** Pairs with additional extrema beyond 1.5 Å; the hump is the largest
  energy above the 7 Å value at separations larger than the well.

- **Window readings.** Well (lowest energy beyond 1 Å relative to E(7 Å), the model's dissociation
  limit); hump; largest force in 3–4 Å (physical Au₂ 1–2 eV/Å, physical H₂ ≈0); largest force in
  4–7 Å (old A1); dissociation limit per atom against E0.

- **Accuracy budget.** 2% on energy MAE against the same-seed control.

- **Type-axis reading (information only from 8 Sep 19:20).** Deepest homonuclear well among the ten
  elements absent from OMat24 (He, Ne, Ar, Kr, Xe, Rn, Po, At, Fr, Ra — zero or about 500 frames of
  100.6 million) and the largest eligible extra extremum, from the all-element scan.

- **Scripts.** `dimer_survey.py`; `watch_run.py`; `ext_read.py`; `pair_window_read.py`;
  `scan_window_read.py`; `external_survey.py`; `diagnose/trimer_probe.py`; `diagnose/repro_spike.py`;
  the user's scanner `/nas/outisli/Research/aisi_workspace/scripts/dimer_scan.py` (isolated pair in
  a 30 Å box, 0.5–7 Å; figures `scan_h_h.png`, `scan_au_au.png` in the repository root);
  `pro_scan_fast.py`; `acceptance_chain.sh`, `run_recipe.py`, `instruments_watch.sh`,
