  `continue_run.sh`; `runs/prepare_final_20260908.py`, `runs/e0_20260909.sh`,
  `runs/launch_e0_20260909.sh`, `runs/launch_route_20260909.sh`, `runs/launch_rim_20260909.sh`,
  `runs/launch_route_fade_seed44_20260909.sh`, `runs/launch_e0_ablation_20260909.sh`,
  `runs/late_validate_20260909.sh`, `runs/launch_route_fade_seedgate_20260910.sh`,
  `runs/launch_e0_final_20260910.sh`, `runs/launch_e0_final_collision_20260910.sh`,
  `runs/prepare_h_ft_e147_20260910.py`, `runs/launch_h_ft_e147_20260910.sh`,
  `runs/launch_g16_ladder_20260911.sh`, `runs/launch_e158_atoms_20260911.sh`, `scan_pairs_*`;
  patches `isolated_reference.py` (`--isolated-reference TABLE`), `adam_route.py`, `pair_anchors.py`,
  `single_atom_frames.py` (withdrawn), `append_isolated_atoms.py`; checks
  `runs/check_isolated_reference_20260909/fixture.py`, `.../smoke.sh`, `pin_check.py`; tables
  `runs/mace_omat0_isolated_atom_energies.json`, `runs/uma_omat_isolated_atom_energies.json`.

- **Validation sets.** Fixed `sub001`, 21742 frames, for the programme runs; a 3000-frame random
  sample of the OMat24 validation subset for the external models.

- **Evaluation artefact (9 Sep).** `pro_scan_fast.py` answers an out-of-memory error by evaluating
  the batch one frame at a time and records a frame that still does not fit as NaN; 4718 frames of
  E134 s42 (289 whole batches, spread over the whole set and every atom count) were recorded that way
  on a card shared with one of the user's jobs, matching 4718 "skipped: out of memory" log lines. The
  same frames evaluate finite one at a time and in batches of 2–32 on a free card; E133 s42's
  validation died with a CUDA allocation error. Both re-run on free cards; watchers and validators
  keep off the user's cards 0–3.

- **Infrastructure failure (9 Sep 13:13:46).** `acceptance_chain.sh`, `run_recipe.py` and
  `instruments_watch.sh` were rewritten (the private commit of 13:15 replaced their inodes), so every
  chain started before that (jobs 518, 520, 521, 531) fails with a stale NFS handle the moment
  training returns and bash reads its next line; the watchers' loops were parsed whole and survive.
  Affected late validations (E130 both seeds, E131, E132, E133/E134 after 40000) were re-run locally.

#### Acceptance criteria (12.5) — conclusions, fallbacks and appendices, §12–13

Title: "Acceptance criteria for the converged scheme".

Preamble as written: the contact exclusions apply to every large-error atom and every sampled pair feature; a separation below 1 Å or a covalent-contact ratio below 0.6 makes that short-range observation informational; a short contact at one atom does not exclude another large-error atom in the same frame; outside both exclusions, holes, unphysical extrema and tears remain failures; A1 is the absolute 0.05 eV/Å limit over 4–7 Å, bond position and depth being separate measurements; validation errors include all frames, reported independently of the exclusions. Every criterion must hold at *every* checkpoint — peak-rate phase, decay, and for a pretraining past 100000 steps: the plain production run passes the hardest-contact criterion at 36000 and 42000 steps, fails at 38000, passes at 100000, fails at 110000. Baseline is the plain run of the same configuration and seed; "equal" means inside the two-seed testbed scatter (about 2%). Status columns are historical readings of the production pair (§10.12) and refreshed-reference candidates (§10.13), whose labels "cannot substitute for the current contact classification, absolute tail threshold, full validation, or complete trajectory audit".

Columns: criterion | instrument | plain run | fixed reference (`-s`) | refreshed reference. Criterion text and instrument are verbatim; status columns are condensed, abbreviated P (plain), F (fixed reference), R (refreshed reference).

**A1. Dimer tail without spurious force.** "Every required isolated pair has maximum absolute radial force no greater than 0.05 eV/Å throughout 4–7 Å, at every retained checkpoint." — `dimer_survey.py`, with full curves and adequate spacing.
*P:* curvature passes (0.1–1.2 eV/Å²), force fails — H₂ 0.05–0.15, O₂ 0.29–0.40 eV/Å over 30000–142000 steps. *F:* H₂ 0.16, O₂ 0.39 eV/Å at 98000; hydrogen testbed 0.22–0.40 against plain 0.06–0.10. *R:* curvature passes; H₂ 0.04–0.17, O₂ 0.1–0.9 eV/Å from scratch — fails the force half, no twin distinguishable from plain.

**A2. Dimer without a hole.** "Outside both contact exclusions, the energy has no unphysical hole or local minimum, and the repulsive branch toward the principal well is monotonic. Measurements inside either exclusion remain informational." — `dimer_survey.py`, with full curves and contact classification.
*P:* fails — O₂ hole −460 eV at 0.49 Å at 92000. *F:* fails — H₂ wall at 0.6 Å reads −0.3 eV/Å at 92000. *R (hydrogen testbed):* continuous refresh fails (−49, −135 eV at 30000); the sparse hand-over fails at every `S` (−165 eV at 20000, −15.6 at 10000, −10.2 at 2000, well deepening to −4.4 eV at 0.64 Å at 5000); the closed tube (`rf10k1P`, `rf10k1P01`) and family alternation (`rf10k1F`) dig −8.6 to −45.9 eV at 0.42–0.50 Å; `rf10k1N` collapses by 30000; dense variants stay bounded to 40000 (wall −0.7 to −1.4 eV, 660–3804 eV/Å²); every later-failing variant exceeds 4000 eV/Å² wall curvature by 20000. *Prod.:* H₂ wall monotonic for `neo_rf10k1D`, the near-only rerun and the label-tube twin; composed twin hole −6.41 eV at 0.535 Å at 70000; every from-scratch O₂ wall carries a dip 11–23 eV above the well.

**A3. Well position and depth.** "H₂ at 0.74 ± 0.03 Å and −4.5 ± 0.5 eV (the physical values); other elements within 20% of the depth and 0.05 Å of the position of the plain run's healthy checkpoints." — `dimer_scan.py`.
*P:* passes for H₂ (−4.15 eV at 0.76 Å). *F:* fails — H₂ −2.24 eV at 0.60 Å, O₂ half depth. *R:* `neo_rf10k1D` passes from 40000 (H₂ −4.11 eV at 0.740 Å, then −4.1 to −4.4 eV; O₂ −4.0 to −4.7 eV against plain −4.9); collision twin only −2.9 to −3.6 eV to 100000.

**A4. One minimum.** "Outside the wall the curvature changes sign once: no second minimum, shoulder or step, the precursor form of the collapse of §3.7." — `dimer_scan.py`.
*P / F:* pass H₂, O₂. *R (production):* near-only rerun passes both to 190000; label-tube twin passes to 200000 but for H₂ dips 0.06–0.08 eV at 2.8–2.9 Å; `neo_rf10k1D` H₂ carries an intermittent second minimum at 2.76–2.93 Å, 0.05–0.22 eV deep, at 140000–260000; `neo_rf10k1C` and `neo_S10k_all4_composed` fail on O₂ (2.06–2.24 Å, 0.03–0.28 eV deep); both stopped.

**B5. One pair, one force.** "On a hard-contact frame the radial force on the closest pair, read with 50–90% of the other atoms deleted and with the pair alone, agrees with the full frame within 20% and never changes sign; under dilation it decays as the plain run's does." — `contact_anchor_probe.py`.
*P:* fails in a transient at 36000–42000 (Cu–I frame / 90% deleted / pair alone −437 / −1297 / −1298 at 38000, back to 54 / 58 / 59 by 44000), passes otherwise (68 / 68 / 69 against 59 at 94000). *F:* fails — 31 / 23 / 53 / 83 against −109 at 94000. *R:* `neo_rf10k1D` passes 20000–130000 through all three plain excursions, then isolated Ca–H +18% to +39% at 140000–200000, within 15% at 240000–340000, isolated Ag–Ag +40%/+21% at 360000/380000; the collision twin, the near-only rerun, `neo_rf10k1P` and `neo_rf10k1` each fail on one isolated pair.

**B6. The same for the reference.** "With a refreshed reference, every reference (the model at the refresh steps) passes B5, read afterwards from the saved checkpoints — a measurement, not a gate inside training." — `contact_anchor_probe.py` on the checkpoints at the refresh steps. P —, F —, R pending.

**C7. No softening on labelled contacts.** "In the two closest relative-distance bins (below 0.7 of the covalent contact distance, labelled forces 8–16 eV/Å) the radial bias differs from the plain run's by at most 0.03 eV/Å and is not systematically negative." — `shortrange_scan.py`. P passes; F passes (+0.029 / −0.016 against +0.019 / −0.004 at 92000); R pending.

**C8. Compression of real frames.** "The closest pair of validation frames driven to 0.6 of its distance: energy rise and radial force within 5% of the plain run's, no attractive step, no energy drop." — `compress_probe.py`. P passes; F passes (identical to three digits at 60000 and 92000); R pending.

**C9. The hardest contacts.** "On the frames whose closest pair lies below 0.5 of the covalent contact distance (13 of the 21742 frames of `sub001val`): force RMSE at the plain run's healthy level (0.35–0.5 eV/Å), largest per-atom error below 10 eV/Å, and no excursions along the checkpoint series." — `hard_contact_series.py`.
*P:* fails twice — 18.2 eV/Å with a 475 eV/Å error at 38000, 22.9 with 601 at 110000. *F:* fails — 0.5–2.0 with errors 9–144 eV/Å from 30000 to 100000. *R (eV/Å):* `neo_rf10k1D` 0.75–1.56 (largest 18–40) to 400000 with no excursion through either plain needle, while plain falls to 0.34 by 100000 — fails the level; other twins 0.41–1.76; every twin above the plain healthy 0.35–0.45.

**D10. In-distribution accuracy.** "Force RMSE without the three worst frames, median per-frame RMSE and energy MAE equal to the plain run's at matched steps; the same count of frames with an error above 5 eV/Å." — `pro_scan_fast.py`.
*P:* baseline. *F:* marginal, 1% behind after 60000 steps. *R:* `neo_rf10k1D` within 1% of plain at every scan 30000–90000 — trimmed force RMSE 0.2227 → 0.2024 against 0.2242 → 0.2005, median 0.1263 → 0.1130 against 0.1262 → 0.1124, energy MAE 16.28 → 14.19 against 16.09 → 14.11 meV/atom, 14 / 14 / 12 / 12 frames above 5 eV/Å against 14 / 13 / 12 / 13; collision twin 3% behind at 70000, 1.5% at 90000; continuous refresh equal at 20000 (0.648 against 0.647).

**E11. Broad search and the Pro frame.** "Largest perturbed curvature ratio below 1, per-atom Lipschitz ratio below 1, no adversarial force above 50 eV/Å; the Pro frame 98341 below the ceiling after 60000 steps." — `pro_scan.py`, `eval_model.py`. P passes; F passes; R pending.

**E12. No spike on any validation frame.** "The force-direction curvature ratio below 1 on every atom of every evaluated frame." — `inspect_frames.py`. P fails at 110000 (frame 16547); F fails at 94000 (frame 16547, ratio 1.68); R pending.

**F13. Dynamics.** "Langevin runs at 1000 K and 2000 K from held-out frames, at least 1 ps: no explosion, force maxima and energy drift equal to the plain run's; on hydrogen the twelve runs at the failing density clean." — `eval_model.py`, `h_spike_eval.py`. P passes (2000 K, 1 ps: 0 of 60); F passes (0 of 60); R pending.

**F14. Long dynamics from hard contacts.** "At least 10 ps started from the hardest-contact frames, since 1 ps from ordinary frames did not separate the two production runs." — `eval_model.py` (to be extended). P, F, R pending.

P —; F passes at 20000 steps (bias +0.076 against −0.032 on 22 eV/Å, overall error 0.137 against 0.135; dimer −2.89 eV at 0.795 Å, plain twin collapsed to −296 eV); R pending.

## 12. Record — the reference-consistency method and the curvature criterion

The method's principle, recipe, cost, implementation and limits as written when it was settled.

#### Method: the reference-consistency term

**Principle (§2.1).** Labels constrain the potential at sampled configurations; updates also change its values and derivatives elsewhere. A frozen-reference force penalty resists that change on geometries generated from training frames — a soft constraint at sampled anchors that does not bound the unsampled function, exclude every false minimum, or establish that the reference is physically correct. Fine-tune reference = the pretrained function; pretraining reference = the random initialization for the initial sparse-anchor phase, then one handover to the trained model at 10000 steps. A near-zero random reference can conflict with true forces near the data. Periodic deletion retains images of the remaining atoms. No energy label reaches an isolated atom (force identically zero).

**Recipe (§2.2).** *Reference*: fine-tune — a frozen copy of the pretrained model (`--ref-ckpt init`); pretraining — the random initialization through the first 10000 steps, then one hand-over to a frozen copy of the model itself (`--refresh 10000 --max-refreshes 1 --dense-after-refresh`). *Anchors*: every 10 steps the whole batch is transformed (one generator drawn per frame), its anchor gradient joining that batch's data gradient in the same optimizer step. Generators: Gaussian shell 0.10–0.20 Å; every atom pushed 0–0.3 Å against its force (collision); isotropic dilation in [1, 2]; deletion of up to 90% of atoms. Fine-tune uses all four from the start; pretraining uses only the sparse generators kept away from the data (dilation ≥ 1.3×, deletion ≥ 50%) before hand-over and all four after — this rule does not guarantee valid reference forces on retained bonds. Composing every generator on every anchor frame is a separate variant (§10.13, ledger E19). *Term*: for real atoms with reference-force norm ≤ 100 eV/Å, average `max(0, norm(F_model - F_ref) - 1 - 0.1*norm(F_ref))**2` over the trusted atoms (absolute tolerance 1 eV/Å, relative 0.1); zero if no atom passes the trust mask. Prototype flags, fine-tune: `--ref-ckpt init --ref-pref 1 --ref-tol 1 --ref-rel 0.1 --fcap 100 --pref 1e-6 --freq 10 --sigma-list 0.1,0.2 --fstep 0.3 --dilate 1.0 --drop 0.9 --probe-frames 0 --sequential --probe-early --estimator force`; pretraining the same but `--sigma 0 --fstep 0 --dilate 1.0 --dilate-min 0.3 --drop 0.9 --drop-min 0.5`. `curvature_reg/train_curv.py` monkey-patches unmodified `dp --pt train` and reuses the curvature probe's anchor generator, so the probe runs at `--pref 1e-6` purely to generate anchors; the package implementation carries no probe.

**Cost (§2.4).** One force evaluation of the frozen reference (coordinate differentiation, no parameter gradients) plus one forward-and-backward of the model on the transformed batch, once every ten steps ≈ 1.3 ordinary steps per ten — an operation-count estimate of roughly **13% extra training time, not a measured upper bound** (runs shared cards with evaluations). With `--probe-early`, peak memory is the larger of the batch graph and one anchor graph, not their sum. The frozen reference holds one extra weight copy on device and travels with the resumable checkpoint; deployment artifacts (EMA snapshot, merged saves, frozen models) carry no safeguard state.

**Implementation notes (§2.5).** One sub-dict `safeguard` inside `loss`, off by default (`strength` 0, bitwise identity asserted by test); `{"strength": 1.0}` enables the recipe. Reference frozen once at `ref_step` (0 from scratch, loaded weights for a fine-tune); the hand-over lives in `debug/curvature_reg/train_refresh.py`, not a package option. Serves `pt` and `pt_expt` (graph path for DPA4/DPA4c), npy and LMDB, every batch rule (`auto`, `max:N`, `filter:N`, `mix:N`), no loss-type coupling, per-branch in multi-task with one shared reference. Array-API core (resolution, `transform_batch`, hinge) in `deepmd/dpmodel/utils/safeguard.py`; torch driver `TrainingSafeguard` in `deepmd/pt_expt/train/safeguard.py`; both trainers (`deepmd/pt/train/training.py`, `deepmd/pt_expt/train/training.py`) call it at construction, per step before the data forward, and in checkpoint save/load. Anchors carry the same tensors as training frames (`coord`, `atype`, `box`, plus `n_node` when ragged): npy `(nf, natoms·3)`, LMDB `(nf, nloc, 3)`, `pt` `mix:N` padded with tail slots typed `−1` (`PHANTOM_ATOM_TYPE`), `pt_expt` a flat `(N, 3)` node list. Dilation scales `coord`/`box` per frame; deletion sets types to `−1`, the supported removal path (`deepmd/pt/utils/nlist.py`; `compact_nodes` in `deepmd/pt_expt/utils/graph_builder.py`; the atomic model emits `mask`; the loss normalizes by real atoms). Neighbour lists are built inside the model forward in every path. Placement in the trainer, not the loss, because `pt`'s loss calls the model (`EnergyStdLoss.forward(input_dict, model, label, ...)`) while `pt_expt`'s is a plain callable. Driver: (1) a deep copy at `ref_step`, eval mode, `requires_grad` off, checkpointed like the EMA so a resumed run keeps the same reference and anchor random stream — copying a compiled model works because `SeZMModel`, its `SO2Convolution` blocks and `pt_expt`'s compiled task wrapper exclude per-process compilation state from copies and pickles, and under DDP the model compiles before the first step; the reference is restored after every structural mutation (weight loading, parameter sharing, LoRA injection, compilation). (2) Every `freq` steps the batch is transformed, reference and model evaluated, the hinge formed over real atoms with `|F_ref| ≤ F_cap` and summed over every force-like output (`force`; `force` and `force_mag` for spin, `spin` carried through). (3) The anchor `backward()` runs *before* the batch forward; gradients accumulate in `param.grad`, so one step applies exactly `(L_anchor + L_data).backward()` while the two double-backward graphs are built and freed in turn. Under DDP the anchor backward runs inside `no_sync()`; LKF and FSDP2 are rejected at construction. (4) The driver then reduces per-parameter absolute maxima on device and reads back one scalar (one host sync per event); on a non-finite value it zeroes the buffers in place, discarding exactly the poisoned event with a counter — zeroing rather than clearing keeps the set of parameters carrying a defined gradient independent of the discard, as `find_unused_parameters` requires. Keys/defaults: `freq` 10, `tol` 1 eV/Å, `rel_tol` 0.1, `fcap` 100 eV/Å, `ref_step` 0, plus `sigma`, `fstep`, `dilate`, `drop`; `generators: auto` = dense+sparse (`sigma [0.1, 0.2]`, `fstep 0.3`, `dilate [1, 2]`, `drop [0, 0.9]`) from `init_model`/`finetune`, sparse alone (`dilate [1.3, 2]`, `drop [0.5, 0.9]`) from scratch; a degenerate range switches a generator off. Parametrizing by *steps* sends exactly a `1/freq` share of effort to the safeguard whatever the batch composition. A restart re-resolves the section; the checkpoint stores the run kind; a restart without stored safeguard state freezes the reference at the restart step and warns. Tests: `source/tests/common/dpmodel/test_safeguard.py`, `source/tests/pt/test_training_safeguard.py`, `source/tests/pt_expt/test_training_safeguard.py`, `source/tests/pt/model/test_sezm_model.py`, plus two-rank DDP in `source/tests/pt/test_training_safeguard_ddp.py` and `source/tests/pt_expt/test_training_safeguard_ddp.py` (nccl on two GPUs, gloo on CPU). A two-GPU torchrun SeZM pretraining with the fused CUDA training kernels trains cleanly end to end.

**Limits (§2.6).** Zero value and zero gradient inside `1 eV/Å + 10%·|F_ref|`; outside, a finite penalty competing with the data objective — a sampled soft constraint, and a tube of that width permits changes larger than the absolute 0.05 eV/Å tail threshold. The cap excludes atoms above 100 eV/Å and does not recognize every hole (at a false minimum the force is zero; a broad wrong curve may have modest forces throughout); excluded atoms get no constraint. The transforms do not establish coverage of all short contacts or low-coordination environments. The §10.8 teacher has a physical H₂ well of −4.38 eV at 0.750 Å alongside extremely deep Si and Fe dimer wells (`runs/dimer_kink_pretrained.log`). A random initialization can conflict with true forces on near-data anchors; a later handover can freeze defects already formed (§11.24: the raw 10000-step reference contains an extra Cu–Cu well although its EMA counterpart selects the outer well as principal).

**Status (§2.3).** Fine-tuning settled; pretraining settled on the hydrogen testbed; pretraining anchors settled (dense anchors cost ~10% of force RMSE independent of tolerance; sparse anchors match plain on two seeds, 0.576 vs 0.587 and 0.599 vs 0.598 at 40000 steps). In the production pretraining the fixed random reference flattens the isolated H₂ and O₂ wells to half depth and, on the hardest contacts, teaches an environment-dependent pair force ending in a spike, so the pretraining reference is under revision (§10.11–10.13). Kept as measured alternatives, not part of the recipe: the curvature hinge with a sticky search; the operator-norm cap.

##### Curvature criterion (§6) and penalty (§7)

**Definition and scale.** `F = −∇_x E`, `H = ∇_x² E`, `û = F/‖F‖₂`, `c = û^T H û = d²E/ds²|_{x+sû}`, eV/Å² — displace every atom in proportion to the force it feels, by a total `s` in the 3N-norm; `c` is the effective force constant. The force direction is free (already computed) and is the direction the model declares steepest. Physical scale: H₂ stretch `ν̃ = 4401 cm⁻¹` with the reduced mass of two protons gives `k_phys = μω² = μ(2πc₀ν̃)² ≈ 36 eV/Å²`. Ceiling `κ = k₀ + F_max/ρ₀`, per atom `κ_i = k₀ + |F_i|/ρ₀`, `k₀ = 300 eV/Å²`, `ρ₀ = 0.1 Å` (`E ∝ exp(−r/ρ)` on a wall); constants chosen once and never tuned. Penalty `L_curv = λ Σ_i [max(0, log(|g_i|/κ_i))]²`: identically zero below the ceiling so it cannot bias the fit, the logarithm making the gradient scale with the relative excess (ratio 700 contributes `log² 700 ≈ 43`, not `700²`) and normalized by `|g|`; `u` and `κ` detached; `κ_i = k₀ + min(|F_i|, F_cap)/ρ₀` with `F_cap = 100 eV/Å`, else a runaway 10⁵ eV/Å would license 10⁶ eV/Å².

**First measurement (§6.1.3, `curvature_signal.py`, central difference ε = 0.02 Å, random-direction control)** — frame: F_max, c along F, c random, c/k_phys — control_s5000: 5.06, **40.1**, 9.2, **1.1**; control_s12000: 3.84, **35.4**, 9.6, **1.0**; s17091: 7.99, −588, 15.3, 16; s17092: 14.07, −350, 11.5, 10; s17093: 49.41, **25845**, 146, **718**; s17095: 17.23, −1265, 16.8, 35; s17096: 28.55, −1198, −0.1, 33; s17097: 318.02, **69382**, 188, **1927**; s17098: 5.48, 71.5, 10.4, 2.0. Healthy configurations reproduce the physical stiffness untuned (ratio 1.0–1.1); pathological ones exceed it 700–1900× with a clear gap; the pathology is directional (random 9–188); the signal appears six steps before failure (s17091 = 16 with thermo normal).

**Whole trajectory (§6.2, `traj_curvature.py`).** On the 1600 frames before step 16000: |c_E| median 12.6, 99% 33.4 eV/Å²; `L_max` min 6.0 / median 21.0 / 99% 64.4 / max 117.9 eV/Å²; `F_max` min 2.1 / median 3.6 / 99% 7.3 / max 9.8 eV/Å. `c_E` and `c_F` agree frame by frame; the median 33 eV/Å² is the H₂ stretch constant to within 10%; curvature grows with force (log–log correlation 0.82). Onset: `c_E` 34 at 17086, 20 at 17088, −198 at 17090, −660 at 17091, 18200 at 17093, 72675 at 17097 — seven steps (1.4 fs) before failure and nowhere earlier: a training-time signal, not a runtime warning.

**Ceiling separation (§6.3)**, healthy max ratio / pathological: hydrogen, 1600 MD frames vs steps 17090–17097 → 0.27 / 8 of 8, 1.3–31; hydrogen, 2941 own training frames → 0.29 / none; hydrogen, 1838 own validation frames → 0.40 / none; DPA4-Neo-OMat24, 1088 OMat24 validation frames → 0.65 / none; DPA4-Pro 788k, 21742 OMat24 validation frames → 0.83 / none; DPA4-Pro 788k, frame 98341 → 12.3 per atom, 28 by energy probe; DPA4-Pro 300k–806k, same frame → 2.5–138; models trained here on sparse OMat24 / water, held-out → 0.51 / 0.68, none. On OMat24 the highest healthy curvatures, up to 605 eV/Å², sit on rattled structures with forces up to 68 eV/Å; curvature/force there is 9–14 Å⁻¹, i.e. `ρ ≈ 0.1 Å`.

**Estimators (§6.4).** Both need one extra evaluation at `x + εu`, `ε = 0.01 Å`: `c_E = (2/ε²)[E(x+εu) − E(x) + ε‖F‖]` (per frame), `g = [F(x) − F(x+εu)]/ε ≈ Hu` (per atom). Under training precision (bfloat16 autocast in the descriptor, TF32 matmuls), `amp_noise.py`, 60 held-out frames: energy probe fp32 0.053 / 0.46 median/max ratio, 0% flagged → training precision **0.72 / 4.0, 33% flagged**, change 250 eV/Å² (median); force probe 0.032 / 0.28, 0% in both, change 0.34 eV/Å². **The force probe is the estimator.** The analytic Hessian-vector product (+158% per step, in edge space because of the NOTE 11 detach in `sezm_model.py`) is the exact alternative.

**Penalty cost (§7.2, OMat24 `mix:400`, 200-step wall times, baseline 25.3 s = 0.127 s/step).** Training frame every 5 steps +10%; + Gaussian 0.03 Å every 5 steps +40%; same every step +185%; hydrogen model, 0.10 Å anchors every 5 steps +39% (0.28 → 0.39 s/step). Each probe is one forward and backward through the double-backward graph; an anchor away from the training frame costs one more.

**Prototype (§7.3).** `train_curv.py` patches `EnergyStdLoss.forward`. Flags: `--pref --freq --eps --k0 --rho0 --fcap --estimator`; `--sigma --sigma-list --fstep --squeeze --strain --dilate --drop --md-steps`; `--buffer-size --buffer-frac --ascend-steps --ascend-eta`; `--ref-ckpt --ref-pref --ref-tol --ref-rel --ref-fscale`; `--probe-frames --probe-max-atoms --sequential --probe-early`; `--spec-cap --spec-every`. Columns `curv_excess`, `curv_frac`, `curv_buf`, `spec_frac`.

Amplitude, not penalty weight, sets the effect (10× weight changes nothing; doubling amplitude halves remaining curvature and hill-climb force). As anchors reach further: largest ratio 47 → 28 → 13 → 9 → 7, hill climb 8044 → 5100 → 3100 → 2400 at constant validation accuracy; the descent does not continue with steps — values wander within a band, the residual set by how often generators land on the core. Strategy 10 removes it only if started while the defect is large (hinge open on 25–50% of probes from the original model vs 0–3% from the worn-down one): *a tracker, not a finder*. MD counts are noisy (same checkpoint and seeds: one blow-up in twelve on one GPU, none on another).

10% subset: start 61–123 eV/Å; baseline after 4000 steps 4.29; deep anchors 0.52 (4000) and 0.678 (8000); strategy 10 13.8 (4000) and 0.57 (8000) vs 0.68 for fixed generators; next-worst frame unchanged at 14 eV/Å. Frames with max error above 1 / 5 / 20 eV/Å: 1773 / 65 / 1 (strategy 10) vs 1769 / 66 / 1 (baseline). Au–Li profile after 4000 deep-anchor steps: smooth from −74 eV at 0.70 Å to −25 eV at 1.2 Å, radial forces within ±400 eV/Å except one bump of 1100; baseline oscillates −186 to −21 eV with ±5·10⁴ eV/Å — still no repulsive wall, but no longer chaotic. Gaussian and mild-squeeze anchors never open the hinge on OMat24 training frames (the defect sits at 37% of a normal Au–Li distance), so the reference term on those anchors never sees it. All four hinge runs died of exhausted GPU memory at step 2450 (one unusually large frame under fixed data order, not a leak); fixed by `--probe-max-atoms`.

The baseline never formed a defect through 120000 steps, so this recipe cannot discriminate; the twin is identical at equal accuracy. The 30000-step models print identical digits because those frames are ordinary near-data configurations for a defect-free model: weights differ by 4.5% (baseline vs regularized) and 9.3% (baseline 30000 vs 60000) of the norm, forces on the seven frames by at most 0.03–0.09 eV/Å (below the 0.06 eV/Å data force error).

##### §10.6 accelerated twins (peak 10⁻³, warm-up 2%, 50000 at peak, cosine decay to 10⁻⁶, 100000 steps, batch 4, `runs/h_fast_*`)

Mechanisms: `h_fast_log` logging twin (probes at 10⁻⁶); `h_fast_adapt` strategy 10 at weight 1 (mixture 0.10/0.20 Å, collisions 0.3 Å, 64-buffer, one ascent step 0.05 Å); `h_fast_spec` strategy 11 `--spec-cap 1.0`; `h_fast_both` cap 1.0 + stronger search (70% buffer probes, 3 ascent steps 0.1 Å), probe-early; `h_fast_spec15` cap 1.5×. First batch: plain starts with every probed atom above the ceiling (excess 17), strategy 10 with 48%. At peak rate (3000–5600): plain 38% above ceiling, strategy 11 alone 11% (7% of matrices on their cap), strategy 10 0% — all at validation force error 0.146–0.148 eV/Å.

Cap costs ~5% throughput, search ~10% (`--freq 4`). At 40000 the capped run's energy error is 3.4 vs 1.3–1.4 meV/atom at the same force error — the first sign that `c = 1` binds.

##### §10.7 prevention on the Pro checkpoint (from 200k, `trn.lmdb`, peak 4.5·10⁻⁴, batches ≤ 400 atoms, `runs/pro_prev_*`)

Curvature ratio at frame 98341 (bold = above the ceiling); every run restarts the optimizer, adding a transient the original lacked.

| run               | mechanism / schedule                                                                    | 5k   | 10k      | 15k      | 20k      | 25k      | 30k      | end            | end: RMSE, E MAE          |
| ----------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---- | -------- | -------- | -------- | -------- | -------- | -------------- | ------------------------- |
| `pro_prev_plain`  | none; 60000, decay 2nd half                                                             | 0.08 | **6.76** | 0.77     | 0.10     | 0.08     | **3.36** | **11.8** (60k) | 0.181 eV/Å, 12.2 meV/atom |
| `pro_prev_spec`   | cap at 200k values (`--spec-cap 1.0`, no anchors); 60000                                | 0.12 | 0.13     | **1.95** | 0.20     | 0.55     | **1.30** | **7.9** (60k)  | 0.231, 12.3               |
| `pro_prev_both`   | cap 1.0 + search (one probe frame ≤ 120 atoms every 4 steps, probe-early); 60000        | —    | **3.39** | —        | **1.26** | —        | —        | —              | —                         |
| `pro_prev_ref`    | reference consistency with the 200k model (strategy 9), dense and sparse anchors; 30000 | 0.43 | **3.29** | **1.44** | 0.28     | 0.14     | 0.72     | 0.72 (30k)     | 0.184, 12.6               |
| `pro_prev_ref_sq` | as above + pair compression; 30000                                                      | 0.15 | 0.31     | **2.20** | **9.02** | **4.19** | **8.30** | **8.30** (30k) | 0.187, 12.7               |

The plain continuation crosses the ceiling within 10000 steps and returns below it at 15000 — ten times faster than the original run's 100000-step crossing. Every run crosses on two of six peak-rate scans; broad searches around 60 held-out frames flag nothing for any run (largest ratio 0.6–0.7, largest hill-climb force 45–51 eV/Å, identical across runs). At this reach the Pro frame decides nothing about prevention; one reading below the ceiling and one above from two runs differing only in a generator is scatter. Established: the reference term leaves ordinary-data accuracy untouched (0.184, 0.187 vs 0.181), the cap does not (0.231).

##### §10.8 single-mechanism fine-tuning runs (10⁻³ peak, 40000 steps, from `DPA4-Neo`, `runs/h_fast_*`)

New generators in `train_curv.py`: isotropic dilation ≤ 2× (`--dilate 1.0`), deletion of ≤ 90% of atoms (`--drop 0.9`, phantoms). Success = dimer physical (well ≈ −4 eV, curvature outside the wall below `k₀`, tail force 0.02–0.06 eV/Å) through peak plateau *and* decay, at the plain recipe's accuracy. Well depths are in the §3.7 table; end-of-run MAE (meV/atom, meV/Å): plain `h_fast_log` 1.32, 53.9; `h_fast_adapt` (hinge + search, dense) 1.43, 53.1; `h_fast_spec` (cap 1.0) 3.40, 54.0; `h_fast_both` (cap + search) 1.18, 53.5; `h_fast_sparse` (hinge + search, dense and sparse) 1.59, 54.5; **`h_fast_ref` (reference, same anchors) 1.24, 54.1** with curvature 5.5–8 eV/Å² and tail force 0.02–0.04 eV/Å at every checkpoint; `h_fast_ref2` (seed 2) 2.31, 53.9 with wells −2.8 / −3.7 / −3.2 / −3.95 eV, curvature 13, tail force 0.01 eV/Å, largest ratio 0.19. Pocket, hill climb and twelve MD runs are clean for every run. `h_long_ref` (reference on the §10.5 schedule, 2·10⁻⁴, 120000 steps): well −3.12 eV at 30k, −2.73 at 60k, −2.43 at 0.91 Å at 120k (baseline −2.0·10⁹), force MAE 54.7 vs baseline 54.7 meV/Å, twelve MD clean.

Only the reference term keeps the dimer physical through the decay; the hinge with sparse generators holds it bounded only while the learning rate is high (deleting 90% of 64 atoms in a doubled cell still leaves six atoms in a 10 Å periodic box). Both anchored terms cost accuracy at peak rate (60–67 meV/Å at 10000 steps vs 52–57 plain), none of it after the decay. Where two dense generators stack (0.2 Å shell on a 0.3 Å collision) anchors reach H–H 0.2–0.3 Å, where the pretrained model's forces are 10⁹ eV/Å and mixed-precision evaluation differs from full precision by more than the tolerance — the first attempt diverged (loss 10¹⁷ at step 1), hence `F_cap`. Throughput, steps 5000–15000: plain 9.0k steps/h, search 9.0k, cap 7.7k, cap + search 7.2k; sparse-anchor and reference runs 3.9k and 4.2k but shared cards (not measured cleanly).

##### §10.9 pretraining from scratch, hydrogen (10⁻³ peak, 40000 steps)

`h_scratch_log` / `h_scratch_ref` (`--ref-ckpt init`) — step: plain well (eV) and largest curvature outside the wall (eV/Å²); reference well, curvature; validation force MAE plain / reference (meV/Å) — 10000: −3.9, 2500; −0.9, 41; 73.3 / 73.2. 20000: −34, 4000; −0.7, 55; 62.2 / 62.3. 30000: spike 1.3·10⁵ (force 1054 eV/Å at 3.1 Å); −0.7, 22; 58.4 / 58.3. 40000 (after decay): −134, 2.3·10⁵ (force 532 eV/Å at 1.33 Å); −0.7, 43; 57.3 / 57.3. The reference well is shallow and misplaced (−0.7 to −0.9 eV at 1.3–2.4 Å): boundedness, not correctness; twelve MD clean for both. Production-configuration pair `h_scratchC_log` / `h_scratchC_ref`: plain −1.4 eV / 237 eV/Å² at 10000, −11.9 eV / 1613 at 20000, −153 eV with 9.4·10⁴ eV/Å² and force 1244 eV/Å at 2.0 Å at 30000, −502 eV with 2.6·10⁵ eV/Å² and force 3437 eV/Å at the end; reference −0.6, −0.27, −0.39, −0.19 eV with curvatures 13, 8.5, 21, 20 eV/Å²; accuracy 69.0 vs 68.6, 60.5 vs 60.1, 56.8 vs 56.3, 55.7 vs 55.4 meV/Å; twelve MD clean for both.

##### §10.10 OMat24 pretraining testbed (20000-frame subset `runs/data/omat_trn_1pct.lmdb`, 2–96 atoms, 40000 steps, peak 4.5·10⁻⁴, seed 42; `arch_chain.sh`, `summarize_arch.py`)

`archC` (seed 42, production configuration, plain with logging probes): 1% force RMSE 0.789 / 0.647 / 0.603 / 0.587 at 10000–40000; H₂ tail force 0.007–0.05 eV/Å; broad search flags nothing (0.34–0.44); Pro-frame ratio 0.03–0.67. `archC2` (seed 43): 0.721 / 0.621 / 0.603 / 0.598; crosses the ceiling at the Pro frame at 10000 and 20000 (1.72, 1.58) — a seed property at this scale. `archC_ref` (+ initial-state reference, dense and sparse anchors): 0.648 at 40000 vs 0.587, about 10% cost.

##### §10.11 short-range integrity

**10.11.1 anchor reach** (1500 OMat24 frames of `sub001val`; 800 hydrogen validation frames) — set: smallest distance min / 5th pct / median (Å); ratio to source min (min / median); frames compressed; anchors below the data's minimum — OMat24 source: 0.547 / 1.458 / 2.309. OMat24 sparse (dilation 1.3–2×, deletion 50–90%): 0.939 / 1.842 / 2.895; 1.000 / 1.36; 0.0%; 0.0%. OMat24 dense+sparse (shell 0.1–0.2 Å, collision 0.3 Å, dilation, deletion): 0.424 / 1.364 / 2.318; 0.523 / 1.00; 42.7%; 0.1%. Hydrogen source: 0.554 / 0.598 / 0.656. Hydrogen sparse: 0.575 / 0.659 / 0.914; 1.000 / 1.39; 0.0%; 0.0%. Hydrogen dense+sparse: 0.058 / 0.203 / 0.614; 0.089 / 1.00; 50.0%; 46.6%. The pretraining recipe never places an anchor atom closer to a neighbour than in the source frame.

**10.11.2 labelled contacts, production pair** (1500 frames, 28074 atoms; MAE / bias eV/Å) — bin (atoms; labelled force): 60000 plain; 60000 term; 92000 plain; 92000 term — below 0.6 (106; 16.0): 0.443/+0.037; 0.422/−0.006; 0.403/+0.019; 0.382/+0.029. 0.6–0.7 (974; 8.4): 0.279/−0.003; 0.284/−0.005; 0.267/−0.004; 0.279/−0.016. 0.7–0.8 (3606; 3.9): 0.193/+0.004; 0.195/+0.004; 0.187/+0.011; 0.190/+0.006. 0.8–0.9 (8395; 1.8): 0.136/−0.003; 0.136/−0.002; 0.133/−0.002; 0.132/−0.000. 0.9–1.0 (9013; 1.2): 0.116/−0.000; 0.116/+0.001; 0.111/−0.000; 0.113/+0.001. ≥ 1.0 (5980; 1.0): 0.125/−0.001; 0.124/−0.001; 0.121/−0.001; 0.120/−0.001. Compression probe (40 frames, closest pair median 2.39 Å, driven to 0.95/0.90/0.85/0.80/0.70/0.60), 60000 steps: energy rise 0.32, 0.94, 1.82, 3.16, 9.64, 24.4 eV (plain) vs 0.32, 0.93, 1.80, 3.17, 9.65, 24.5 (term); radial force 4.1, 6.3, 9.5, 16.6, 40.9, 82.4 eV/Å vs 4.2, 6.3, 9.5, 16.7, 41.2, 82.1; at 92000 identical to three digits, repulsive on every compressed geometry of both.

Dimer wall vs ZBL (eV/Å, positive repulsive). H₂ at 0.4/0.5/0.6/0.7 Å — ZBL 48/26/15/9.6; 30000: plain 55/23/9.2/2.0, well −4.19 eV at 0.75 Å; term 64/16/3.6/−0.9, −2.52 at 0.68 Å. 60000: plain 38/19/8.0/1.8, −4.22 at 0.77 Å; term 21/17/2.7/−1.9, −2.44 at 0.65 Å. 92000: plain 46/22/8.8/2.0, −4.15 at 0.76 Å; term 21/11/−0.3/−4.1, −2.24 at 0.60 Å. O₂ at 0.6/0.8/1.0/1.2 Å — ZBL 549/195/82/38; 30000: plain 68/68/38/2.7, −4.87 eV at 1.24 Å; term 53/62/12/1.0, −2.39 at 1.24 Å. 60000: plain 42/102/37/2.8, −4.90 at 1.24 Å; term 158/−3.1/23/2.1, −2.33 at 1.25 Å. 92000: plain −3000/109/39/2.3 with a hole of −460 eV at 0.49 Å; term 87/36/25/2.2, −2.39 at 1.25 Å. At 92000 the isolated metal dimers are chaotic in both (0.6/0.8/1.0/1.2 Å, plain vs term: Si −3139/530/37/77 vs 1437/109/72/95; Fe 1555/−126/−59/83 vs 2240/−217/−20/22; Cu 3578/−487/332/85 vs 293/−47/40/89; holes of hundreds to tens of thousands of eV at 0.40–0.49 Å in both).

Hardest contacts: `contact_census.py` over all 21742 frames of `sub001val` — 13 frames below 0.5 of covalent contact distance, 107 at 0.50–0.55, 539 at 0.55–0.60, 3519 at 0.6–0.7, 5926 at 0.7–0.8, 11631 at ≥ 0.8. Force RMSE at 98000, plain vs term: **0.746 vs 5.003** (13 hardest), 0.428 vs 0.416, 0.367 vs 0.372, 0.290 vs 0.294, 0.188 vs 0.191, 0.1424 vs 0.1433 — about 1% cost everywhere, an order of magnitude on the hardest 0.06%. Frame 16547 (Cu₁₈P₂₄S₂₄I₁₈, Cu–I at 1.230 Å = 0.45 of covalent contact, labelled radial force +33 eV/Å): largest per-atom error 15.9, 26.5, 7.9 eV/Å (plain) vs 24.6, 144.0, 73.7 (term) at 88000/94000/98000; at 94000 the term predicts an *attractive* −109 eV/Å with curvature ratio 1.68 above the ceiling (`inspect_frames.py`; plain 0.44). `contact_anchor_probe.py`, radial force on Cu–I at 94000 (eV/Å) — full frame; dilated 1.3×/1.6×/2.0×; 50%/70%/90% of others deleted; pair alone — plain 59; 46/9.3/−1.3; 68/68/69; 68. Term −109; 5.2/0.7/−0.2; 31/23/53; 83. Same pattern at 98000 on Ca–H (0.886 Å, 0.43): 90% deleted 10 vs 60 eV/Å, pair alone 9 vs 61; Ag–Ag (1.418 Å, 0.49): 90% deleted 26 vs 56. Growth: at 2000 neither has the wall (~3 eV/Å in every variant); at 6000 both do (term 48/41/53/53 vs plain 46/43/48/48); at 10000 they still agree (56/41/70/71 vs 58/46/61/61); at 20000 term reads 32/13/178/180 vs plain 60/46/66/66, and on Ca–H its 90%-deleted and pair-alone forces turn attractive (−5.7, −2.3 vs 56, 54). On the 13 hardest frames the term's best is 20000 (RMSE 0.365, largest error 3.5), then it wanders — 0.50, 0.77, 1.22, 1.33, 1.98 at 30000/40000/60000/90000/100000 with largest errors 9–50 eV/Å — while the 107 frames of the next group improve monotonically 0.28 → 0.236. The 40000-step testbed models show none of this (`archC` and `archC_refS2` read 27–29 and 22–24 eV/Å in every Cu–I variant): with `mix:400` an anchor event carries 25 times fewer frames than production.

**10.11.3 fine-tuning hinge activity** (`anchor_hinge.py`, 100 hydrogen training frames, 5640 anchor atoms, data nearest-neighbour minimum 0.574 Å; 40000-step checkpoint of `h_cont_acc8`) — bin (Å): atoms; reference force median (eV/Å); model force median; excluded by cap; hinge open; of open, model above reference — below 0.5: 276; 60.7; 36.9; 42.0%; 10.1%; 3.3%. 0.5–0.6: 226; 18.0; 14.0; 23.0%; 12.4%; 0.0%. 0.6–0.7: 429; 7.3; 5.9; 16.6%; 15.9%; 0.7%. 0.7–0.8: 766; 2.8; 2.2; 13.2%; 12.4%; 5.2%. 0.8–1.0: 1762; 2.2; 2.4; 12.6%; 11.0%; 8.2%. ≥ 1.0: 2181; 2.7; 2.5; 8.3%; 2.4%; 1.3%. The reference is *harder* than the fine-tuned model below 0.8 Å; the pretrained `DPA4-Neo` carries a radial bias of +0.88 eV/Å against DFT on the hydrogen validation set (MAE 1.01 eV/Å). The plain twin `h_cont_log40` on the same anchors gives the same picture (open hinge 11–17%, model above reference 1–8% below 0.8 Å). Labels, 800 hydrogen validation frames (51200 atoms): 256 atoms at 0.56–0.62 Å (7.6 eV/Å) plain 0.070 / +0.004 vs term 0.071 / −0.019; 4 atoms below 0.56 Å (12.3 eV/Å) 0.076 / −0.028 vs 0.097 / −0.070; all atoms 0.115 / −0.0035 vs 0.116 / −0.0077.

**10.11.4 adversarial fine-tune** (`make_zbl_dataset.py`: hydrogen data relabelled with an added ZBL wall between all H–H pairs below 1.2 Å, switched off smoothly 0.9–1.2 Å; forces and virial verified against finite differences to 10⁻⁸; every H₂ bond at 0.75 Å gains 7 eV/Å, added force RMS 6.9 eV/Å up to 24.6; the pretrained reference is softer than the data by 4.3 eV/Å on average and 13.7 eV/Å on the 256 atoms at 0.56–0.62 Å, labelled force 22 eV/Å). Twins from `DPA4-Neo` at production rate, 20000 steps (`runs/zbl_log`, `runs/zbl_acc10`; recipe of §2.2 with `freq` 10) — 256 contact atoms MAE / radial bias; all 51200 atoms MAE / bias; H₂ dimer: plain 0.092 / −0.032; 0.135 / −0.012; collapsed to −296 eV at 0.465 Å with a wall of 3·10⁶ eV/Å at 0.4 Å. With the term 0.120 / +0.076; 0.137 / +0.027; physical, −2.89 eV at 0.795 Å, curvature 11 eV/Å², wall 59 eV/Å at 0.4 Å. The safeguarded twin learns the wall the reference lacks (bias +0.076 vs the reference's −13.7; 0.3% harder than the labels, the plain twin 0.15% less); overall error equal; on the 256 contact atoms 30% larger error, 0.5% of the wall vs 0.4%. Criterion G16 of §12.5 passes.

##### §10.12 production pair: `DPA4-Neo` on OMat24, plain vs safeguarded

`N_2-s_3-c_32x2` under `Release/Neo`: full OMat24 training set, `mix:10000` batches, eight epochs at peak 8·10⁻⁴ on two GPUs, seed 42; safeguarded adds `"safeguard": {"strength": 1.0, "freq": 10}` with every other element default (sparse generators, 1 eV/Å + 10%, cap 100 eV/Å, random initialization frozen at step 0). EMA checkpoint every 2000 steps in `Storage/ckpts/DPA4/OMat24/Neo/`; matched checkpoints under `runs/neo_plain`, `runs/neo_safe`; evaluated by `neo_eval.sh` / `omat_eval_chain.sh` (five dimers; `sub001val` 21742 frames / 416677 atoms; Pro frame 98341; broad search; Langevin from 60 held-out frames).

| step  | RMSE without 3 worst frames, plain / term (eV/Å) | median per-frame RMSE | energy MAE (meV/atom) | frames above 5 eV/Å |
| ----- | ------------------------------------------------ | --------------------- | --------------------- | ------------------- |
| 30000 | 0.2242 / 0.2235                                  | 0.1262 / 0.1265       | 16.09 / 16.16         | 14 / 14             |
| 40000 | 0.2164 / 0.2158                                  | 0.1213 / 0.1212       | 15.31 / 15.43         | 13 / 15             |
| 60000 | 0.2075 / 0.2086                                  | 0.1157 / 0.1159       | 14.58 / 14.63         | 12 / 15             |
| 70000 | 0.2041 / 0.2061                                  | 0.1141 / 0.1143       |                       | 12 / 16             |
| 80000 | 0.2034 / 0.2047                                  | 0.1124 / 0.1133       |                       | 13 / 14             |
| 88000 | 0.2010 / 0.2026                                  | 0.1112 / 0.1123       |                       | 12 / 14             |
| 92000 | 0.2006 / 0.2030                                  | 0.1111 / 0.1122       | 13.93 / 14.07         | 12 / 16             |
| 98000 | 0.1999 / 0.2032                                  | 0.1108 / 0.1116       |                       | 12 / 17             |

Raw force RMSE at 92000: 0.2054 plain vs 0.2261 term. From 60000 on the term is 0.5–1.5% behind on both robust readings, inside two-seed testbed scatter but always on the same side; energy error and bad-frame counts equal. Broad search flags nothing (largest perturbed ratio 0.64–0.66, adversarial force p90 22.3–22.6 eV/Å, none above 50); the Pro frame crosses the ceiling in both at 30000 (2.12 / 1.98) and sits far below from 60000 (0.18 / 0.02; 0.14 / 0.13). Langevin at 2000 K for 1 ps from 60 held-out frames explodes in neither (largest force median / p90 / max 6.9 / 18.5 / 34.7 eV/Å plain vs 7.2 / 18.2 / 35.2 term; energy drift p90 1.17 vs 1.18 eV/atom). Dimers as in §10.11.2.

Hardest contacts (13 frames below 0.5, `hard_contact_series.py`) — step: plain RMSE (eV/Å) / largest per-atom error; term RMSE / largest — 6000: 1.26/15.6; 1.19/15.0. 10000: 1.05/24.5; 0.96/22.9. 20000: 0.90/20.2; 0.36/3.5. 30000: 0.78/17.6; 0.50/8.9. 32000: 0.92/20.5; 1.26/31.5. 34000: 1.05/25.3; 0.92/22.2. 36000: 0.81/15.8; 0.46/8.9. 38000: **18.22/475.4**; 0.54/10.5. 40000: 1.08/25.0; 0.77/15.8. 42000: 0.72/17.1; 0.91/19.9. 44000: 0.95/21.0; 0.53/9.0. 46000: 1.15/28.7; 0.73/15.4. 48000: 1.24/30.3; 0.54/9.6. 50000: 1.33/30.5; 0.53/10.0. 60000: 1.24/28.9; 1.22/27.4. 90000: 0.43/6.2; 1.33/33.5. 100000: 0.34/3.7; **1.98/50.2**. 110000: **22.89/601.4**; 0.37/5.8. 112000: 5.93/155.3; 0.50/7.8. 116000: 0.46/6.4; —. 120000: 2.14/54.9; —. 140000: 0.44/7.2; —. 164000: 0.47/7.0; —.

Both fail, in opposite ways. The term derails between 10000 and 20000 steps into the environment-dependent contact and wanders. The plain run is clean at coarse resolution until 100000, but the 2000-step series finds a first needle at 38000 — 18.2 eV/Å with a 475 eV/Å error on the Cu atom of frame 16547, gone by 42000 (the contact-anchor probe reads −437 eV/Å on that pair inside the frame and −1298 for the pair alone at the same checkpoint) — and at 110000 predicts 634 eV/Å on the Cu atom of frame 16547 (label 32; energy error 24 eV). Its anatomy is the opposite of the term's: the pair function has blown up at 1.23 Å in every environment (full frame 625, 90% deleted 977, pair alone 941 eV/Å against 32, 60, 58 at 100000) while the neighbouring distance is untouched (46 eV/Å at 1.3× dilation, unchanged) — a needle in the pair distance, environment-independent and transient (healthy at 116000, largest error 6.4; back at 118000–120000, 55–60; moderate afterwards, 7–13). The term's run at 110000–112000 is healthy on these frames: its pair-alone force at 110000 is 32 eV/Å against the plain run's 941 — the sparse anchors bound the pair function, but around the wrong value.

**Verdict.** With the fixed random-initialization reference the safeguarded production run is not better than the plain one: 1% behind on the bulk of the data, half-depth wells for the isolated pair, and a failure of the hardest-contact criterion of §12.5 from 30000 steps on, where the plain run fails the same criterion at 110000 steps with a far larger excursion. Neither is acceptable; the mechanism that bounds the pair function is the one to keep, with a reference carrying the right value (§10.13).

#### Experiments — the refreshed reference, §10.13 first half (2–6 September)

**Notation.** Hydrogen-scan cells: `well depth @ position / wall curvature / tail curvature / validation` = eV @ Å / eV/Å² (largest inside 1.2 Å) / eV/Å² (largest beyond 1.2 Å) / meV/Å, at 10000 · 20000 · 30000 · 40000 steps. `T` = criterion-A1 dimer tail force (eV/Å) at those four steps. Contact-probe cells = `full frame / dilated 1.3x / 90% deleted / pair alone`, eV/Å. HO = hand-over of the reference at step `S`. Generators (order: shell · collision · dilation · deletion): **sp** = sparse (dilation + deletion), **nr** = near (shell + collision), **dn** = dense (all four). `·` = not evaluated. Dates are day/9 (September 2026).

Aggregate: through 20000 steps every HO run is bounded, tail curvature 15-49 eV/Å² (the fixed reference's range), at the plain recipe's accuracy (59.7-60.1 meV/Å vs 60.1). No from-scratch run meets A1's 0.05 eV/Å, plain included (0.06-0.10). Wall curvature at 20000 separates them: bounded runs 30-1574 (fixed ref. at every reading), 660-1572 (`rf10k1D`), 719-2038 (`rf5k1D`), 1464-3804 (`rf10k1DS`); later-holing runs 4655 (`rf10k1`), 1.2e4 (`rf5k1`), 1.9e4 (`rf10k1P`), 2.1e4 (`rf10k1F`), 1.9e4 (`rf10k1N`).

#### Experiments — the refreshed reference, §10.13 second half (3–6 September)

Key. Lines 3501-3776 continue the table whose header (3329) is `| run, step | Cu-I (frame 16547) | Ca-H | Ag-Ag | hardest 13, eV/Å | H2 | O2 |`; each contact cell is four eV/Å numbers, **full frame / dilated 1.3x / 90% of others deleted / pair alone**, and from 5 Sept the O2 cell also carries `F6` = F(0.6 Å) in eV/Å, positive when repulsive. Lines 3798-3866 = criterion-A1 tail table, 3872-3938 = criterion-A2 wall table. Abbreviations: **C9** = force RMSE on the 13 hardest frames, eV/Å, range over the preceding 8000 steps (largest single error in brackets); **A1** = 4-7 Å dimer tail force, H2 / O2, eV/Å; **A2** = H2 wall `E / F` (eV above the well at 0.45 Å / eV/Å at 0.6 Å); **bulk** = trimmed force RMSE / median per-frame RMSE / energy MAE (meV/atom) / frames >5 eV/Å on the 1% subset. Well depths in eV at the stated Å. All runs are OMat24 pretraining, two GPUs, EMA checkpoints every 2000 steps, reference handed over at 10000 steps, unless stated.

#### Experiments — the programme, §0 and §11.1–11.26 (6–7 September)

Notation: **E/F/R** = energy MAE meV/atom / force-vector MAE eV/Å / force-vector RMSE eV/Å. **tail** = largest absolute spurious force in 4–7 Å, eV/Å. Distances Å, energies eV, forces eV/Å; `x@y` = value x at separation y. §0 also cites E118/E130/E131/E133/E134/E147/E148/E150/E152 and E86–E102, written up in §§11.27–11.47 outside this range; only readings stated inside lines 29–611 appear here.

Testbeds: **A** = OMat24 pretraining, **B** = hydrogen from scratch, **C** = hydrogen fine-tune, **dimer** = isolated-pair survey only. Format: run / ledger ID — testbed; changed factor. Readings. → verdict. (section; date)

- **`Release/Air/N_3-l_3`** (production Pro; dpa4, Bessel 16, rcut 6, 3 blocks, lmax 3, 64 ch, 469000) — dimer. Tails H–H 0.10@5.14, N–N 0.22, Ni–Ni 0.88, Cu–Cu 1.24, O–O 1.98, Si–O 2.07, Au–Au 3.43, Fe–O 16.2@5.41, every peak at 4.9–5.4 Å. (§11.1, 6 Sep 16:10)
- **`neo_1gpu_plain`** — A, p000 GPU 0, Bessel, no anchors; matrix baseline. Events 3362 454@1.165, 9746 882@0.565, first tear 10288 1005@0.575, **storm 12223 2.1·10⁶ with 64 atoms >20**, 21605 8969@0.389, 22649 5112@0.582; 20000 18/24 holed, 24/24 tails fail. → first tear at 10288, not 21605. (§§11.7–11.8, launched 6 Sep 15:22)
- **`neo_1gpu_all4` (+`_rep`)** — A, p000 GPU 1 & 4, Bessel; 4 generators one per frame. 20000 0/24 holed, 23/24 tails fail; `_rep` 100000 14.36921/0.136087/0.218108, 7 needles/3 tears, the 67801–67812 burst carrying 5, 0, 11 atoms >1000 outside both exclusions. (§§11.7, 11.11–11.12)
- **`neo_1gpu_gauss`** — A, p000 GPU 3; Gaussian alone (readings under `h_1gpu_gauss`).
- **`neo_1gpu_all4_gauss` (+`_rep`, `_rep2`)** — A, p000 GPU 2, cluster 320/333; 4 generators + Gaussian. `_rep2` 100000 14.39981/0.136074/0.226675, 6 needles/0 tears; two Gaussian controls at 40000 have O₂ wells −4.449 and −4.370. → 24/24 tails fail; working control. (§§11.12, 11.18)
- **`neo_1gpu_all4c_gauss` (+`_rep`…`_rep4`)** — A, cluster 324/334; 4 generators **composed** on every frame vs one per frame. 40000 O₂ wells −2.920 to −4.416, three with secondary minima near 2. (§§11.9, 11.12)
- **`h_1gpu_plain` / `h_1gpu_plain_control`** — B, p000 GPU 5, Bessel; baseline. 10000 well −1.15@1.595, tail 0.068 (response 0.0487); **20000 hole −125.6@0.400, band 4149**. (§§11.6, 11.15)
- **`h_1gpu_all4`** — B, p000 GPU 7, Bessel; 4 generators. 10000 well −0.22@2.290, tail 0.053 (response 0.0246). → anchors alone flatten and displace the well. (§§11.5–11.6)
- **`h_1gpu_gauss`** — B, cluster 335; Gaussian basis alone vs `h_1gpu_plain`. 10000 well −3.16@0.595, **tail 14.12@5.705, curvature 246 eV/Å²**; 20000 tail 22.74@5.875, curvature 789, well −20.6@0.520. (§§11.5, 11.10)
- **`h_1gpu_all4_gauss` (+`_rep`)** — B, p000 GPU 6, cluster 336; 4 generators + Gaussian. 10000 two draws F 73.6 meV/Å, wells −0.33/−0.35@0.70/0.68, tails 0.437/0.089; 20000 well −1.64@0.555. → anchors repair 32× of the Gaussian tail; still 6–8× worse than Bessel. (§11.5)
- **`h_ft_1gpu_plain`** — C, from `neo_rf10k1D/ckpt/model_ema.ckpt-660000.pt`, cluster 326; no safeguard. 10000 well **−6.7·10⁷@0.405**, band 3.7·10⁹ with 12 sign changes, tail 2.39, validation F 0.123, **no needle >100, largest batch error 6**; 20000 −4.2·10⁸, tail 2.52. (§11.2, 6 Sep 16:17)
- **`h_ft_1gpu_all4`** — C, cluster 327; + safeguard, 4 generators, pretrained reference. 10000 well −16.85@0.660, tail **0.077**, validation F 0.133 (+8 %); 20000 tail 0.069, cost 2.5 %, 12 stable MD runs, well −515. (§11.2)
- **`neo_rf10k1D` @660000** — pretrained reference: H₂ −4.264@0.750, tail 0.163; fine-tune validation F 0.32 at step 100 vs 1.76 from scratch. The 4-generator production model, 20000 steps before it holed. (§11.2)
- **`neo_rf10k1D` 440000–680000** — dimer, averaged weights every 2000; early-warning test. H₂ and O₂ tails and responses stay flat over 240000 steps (440000 0.134/0.676 and 0.643/3.387 … 678000 0.148/0.794 and 0.526/3.867 … 680000 0.152/0.817 and 0.515/3.771). (§11.4, 6 Sep 16:30)
- **Max `Storage/ckpts/DPA4/OMat24/Max/N_8-l_6-c_96x2`** (8 blocks, lmax 6, 96×2 ch, `so3_readout none`, Bessel 16, rcut 6, plain, 432000) — held-out OMat24 + dimer. E 9.58, F 42.0 meV/Å, **R 358.6 meV/Å** (R/MAE 8.5 vs 1.25); pair tails 0.296 (H–H) to **20.7** (Fe–O, 7 extrema). (§11.3, 6 Sep 16:45)
- **E67 tail term** (`neo_1gpu_all4_gauss_tail`, `neo_1gpu_tail`, `h_1gpu_all4_gauss_tail`, `h_1gpu_tail`) — A + B; 24 vacuum pairs per anchor event, separations 4–6 Å (lower end 1.6 covalent contacts), radial force driven to zero by `10·f_r²`, no label, no reference. (§§11.1, 11.6–11.7, 6 Sep 17:20–18:03)
- **`h_1gpu_gauss_inner`, `h_1gpu_all4_gauss_inner`, `neo_1gpu_all4_gauss_inner`** (`--gauss-inner 4.0`) — B + A; Gaussian centres on `linspace(0, 4, 16)`, width 0.267. Basis 1.31@0.2, flat through 3, 0.000@5 vs 0.093. (§§11.5, 11.7–11.9, launched 6 Sep 16:57)
- **`neo_1gpu_all4_gauss_inner_1env`, `h_1gpu_gauss_inner_1env`** — A + B; both structural handles together. Six anchored cells at 36800–42400 steps with no tear and largest per-atom error 99–179; `h_1gpu_all4_gauss_1env` 40000 H–H tail 0.06208. (§§11.7–11.8, 11.12, launched 6 Sep 18:03)
- **E72 envelope-weighted aggregation** — A + B on the Gaussian + bare-basis + 4-generator control; `n_atten_head` 1→0. Hydrogen 40000 F +1.771 %, E −3.888 %, H₂ min −39.114@1.342, tail **25.3303**; OMat24 R 0.24359 vs plain 0.23043 (+5.7 %), 23/24 fail. (§§11.9, 11.15, 11.20, 11.22)
- **E73 output (message) envelope** (`message_envelope.py`) — A + B on Gaussian + bare basis + 4 generators; edge weights × envelope (`s·a`). Same-weight on `h_1gpu_gauss` 10000 14.12245 → **0.59962** (still above A1), on the captured tear 6712.79 → 7422.23 (worse). (§§11.10–11.11, 11.15, 11.20)
- **E74 fitting-input RMS** — A + B, same base; `h / sqrt(mean(h²) + 1e-5)` after the readout, no affine. Same-weight: the captured frame's largest error 6712.79 → 48.7879, the Gaussian 67801 frame 161649.5 → 10.767; on a healthy checkpoint the H₂ well stays at 0.760 with depth −4.048 → −2.992. (§§11.11–11.12, 11.15, 11.18, 11.20)
- **E75 = E73 + E74** — A + B, two factors with simultaneous controls. Hydrogen 40000 E +13.899 %, F +1.296 %, deepest H₂ min −0.001181@4.942 (no well), tail 0.005095; OMat24 16.07758/0.149189/0.236233 (+1.438 %, within budget), 24/24 fail; at 10000 H₂ tail 0.23316 (FiLM removed 0.22139). (§§11.11, 11.14–11.15, 11.19–11.20)
- **E76 locality grid** (`neo_local_r4_b2` etc.) — A on E75's recipe; cutoff 6 or 4 Å × 2 or 3 blocks. 2-block 4 Å keeps H₂ +0.1721@2.392 and O₂ +0.1564@2.912 outside both exclusions at 39.31478/0.227966/0.334562; 3-block 4 Å 35.78485/0.214865/0.327366 with 16/24 extrema beyond 1.5. (§§11.14, 11.20)
- **E77 finite-cluster anchors** (`cluster_anchors.py`): `h_ft_origref_both_srcperiodic` vs `_srcclusters` — C on the original-teacher E75 student; half the anchor frames become finite subclusters (2 atoms→frame size, log-uniform), unwrapped, >2 cutoffs of vacuum. (§§11.16, 11.19, 11.23)
- **E78 fitting activation** `neo_outputenv_fitgate_silu` / `_sigmoid` — A on Gaussian + bare basis + output envelope + 1 generator; `sigmoid` vs `silu`. 40000 E 16.30852 vs 16.47021, F 0.149330 vs 0.149125, **R 0.230689 vs 0.240731 (+4.353 %)**; no tear, 4 and 7 small needles, both fail 23/24. (§11.18)
- **E79 cluster locality** `h_ft_origref_cluster_random` vs `_nearest` — C; cluster = nearest neighbours of a random centre instead of a random subset. Probe: random gives 59 coordination-one of 17569 eligible (0.336 %), all inside the tolerance with zero gradient. (§11.21)
- **`h_ft_origref_control` / `h_ft_original_plain`** — C, original preserved teacher definition; anchors vs none. 1.05022/0.102801 and 1.91342/0.102815/0.120851; the no-anchor baseline has severe H₂ defects including a sampled minimum outside 1 Å despite a normal validation average. (§11.21)
- **`h_ft_origref_*` four cells** (control, E73, E74, both), teacher = unmodified `neo_rf10k1D` 660000 via `original_reference.py` — C; student structural change only. 40000 O₂/Fe–O tails: teacher 0.50894/2.80950; original architecture + hydrogen anchors 2.26923e12/8.12351e9. (§§11.13, 11.23)
- **Reconstruction control** — C; `edge_norm` `[false,true,true]`→`false` and `sel` 256→416 at the pretrained weights, before any optimization: H₂ −4.264@0.750 with tail 0.1631 → −4.148@0.790 with tail 0.3242. → the earlier fine-tunes start from an altered initialization. (§11.13)
- **E80 replay** `h_ft_replay_control` vs `h_ft_replay_omat` (`replay_anchors.py`) — C; half the anchor frames replaced by complete OMat24 configurations. 8000 control O₂ −2.21686e6@1.440 with tail 161295 vs replay −6.829@1.2804 with 0.21684. (§11.23)
- **E81** `neo_cluster_phase_control` vs `_clusters` — A on E75's recipe; finite-cluster sources in half the frames from step 10000 (the handover). Audit: periodic 9110 atoms with 55 coordination-one (27 pair types), clusters 6241 with 266 (139 pair types). (§11.24)
- **E82** `h_ft_bias_control` vs `h_ft_bias_calibrated` — C, plain adaptation without anchors; data-derived hydrogen energy zero (`bias_only.pt`, only `atomic_model.out_bias` changed). Teacher residual +0.11829404 eV/atom on 512 frames, reduced to 0.04363095 with float64 forces bit-identical. (§11.25)
- **E83** `neo_initial_cluster_control` vs `_clusters` — A; finite-cluster sources in half the frames **from step zero**. The two stored initialization references are bit-identical and give zero energy and force everywhere; failing tails at 2000–10000 are 23, 24, 24, 23, 24 and 23, 24, 24, 24, 24. (§11.26)
- **E85 squared reference loss** — hinge tolerance replaced by a squared loss on the same trusted-atom mask; algebraic checks pass. → **shelved before launch**. (§11.26)
- **`neo_1gpu_plain_control`** (fresh plain OMat24 baseline) — A. 40000 16.46335/0.149880/0.232883; 100000 over 50 surveys 14.44699/0.136732/0.212908 with 49 events in 100–1000 and 12 above 1000 (all 26 atoms above 1000 inside at least one exclusion), 24/24 fail. (§§11.12, 11.18, 11.20)
- **Gaussian + bare-basis + 4-generator control (OMat24)** — A; the common control for E71–E75. 40000 16.07998/0.147759/0.230738 (R 0.22989 vs plain 0.23043 in the E71/E72 comparison); 24/24 fail. → not accepted. (§§11.15, 11.20)

#### Experiments — the structural experiments, §11.27–11.43 (7–8 September)

Testbed = OMat24 pretraining from scratch; "screen" = 40000 steps, twenty 24-pair dimer surveys every 2000 steps, then full `sub001val.lmdb` validation (21742 frames, 416677 atoms). **val** = (energy MAE meV/atom, force-vector MAE eV/Å, force-vector RMSE eV/Å); **tails** = pairs failing A1 out of 24 (largest tail in eV/Å); **holes** = holed pairs per checkpoint; **xp** = pairs with eligible extra extrema; **b>20** = batches above 20 eV/Å in 10000–40000. Tail quadruples = (H₂, O₂/O–O, Cu–Cu, Fe–O) eV/Å.

Fixed-weight counterfactuals (float64, 0.005 Å spacing; no training), tail quadruples as above:

- Bare-basis inner-centre model at 40000 (`diagnose/radial_response.py`): detaching the Gaussian coordinate path gives full / radial-basis / remaining = H–H 0.29882 / −0.32708 / 0.62590 at 4.320 Å; O–O 0.37053 / −0.41646 / 0.78698; Cu–Cu 0.52018 / −0.66137 / 1.18155; Fe–O 4.35932 / −0.08513 / 4.44445 at 5.295 Å (Fe–O tail after detachment 4.46987). Projecting the two centres above 4 Å onto 4 Å gives tails 28187.8 / 133892.7 / 235552.6 / 313281.9 with deep wells at 3.7–4.0 Å. Detaching the attention envelope input / logits / all weights gives Fe–O → 1.23458 / 3.35969 / 0.26229 and H₂ → 0.26187; at 5 Å in block 2 Fe–O has query–key logit 6.56183 and radial logit 2.64598 with weight 0.91919 despite envelope 0.03066, H₂ 0.25506 and 3.76069 with weight 0.05949, null mass 0.82417.
- §11.30 attention rules (inner-centre; E75) at 40000: 0.29882/0.37053/0.52018/4.35932 → cosine+unit null mass 1.37218/1.14164/1.50665/0.61858 → separate envelope mass 3.12628/2.49393/2.70984/0.54727; E75 0.16342/0.39870/0.24050/1.35402 → 0.41667/1.00811/0.23125/1.48470 → 1.43883/1.22536/0.22998/1.61516; separate mass also creates a −255.93 eV H₂ well at 2.325 Å. Neither repairs either checkpoint; `normalized_scores_e75` omitted E75's output envelope and is recorded invalid.
- Fixed-inner model at 20000 (`diagnose/seed_response.py`): detach seed / zero basis / both gives H₂ 0.31805 → 0.31347/0.32021/0.31584, Fe–O 3.80601 → 3.80569/3.80606/3.80574. Type addition: original 0.31805/0.41728/0.34349/3.80601; removed 0.38550/0.35614/0.10894/0.30006; multiplied 0.10813/0.21907/0.56512/0.08024; removed with basis zeroed 0.04925/0.11759/0.03359/0.02048. Trained E88 candidate at 4000: 0.09231/0.30045/0.11572/0.26144 → detach seed 0.05779/0.19193/0.08929/0.22049 → zero basis 0.03978/0.06764/0.00883/0.00539 → both exactly zero.
- §11.33 seed-gate factorial on E88 at 4000 (endpoint 4 vs 3 Å × original vs gated seed): 0.0923/0.3004/0.1157/0.2614; 0.0650/0.1991/0.0887/0.2240; 0.0397/0.0679/0.0090/0.0058; 3 Å gated 4.54e-5/5.88e-5/1.64e-5/1.95e-4 — all 24 tails pass, largest Fe–O 0.000195472, basis zeroed gives exactly zero force; H₂ keeps +0.464359 eV at 1.520 Å and a secondary minimum −0.010453 at 3.250, well −2.08086 at 0.765. On E88 at 16000 all 24 pass with largest Fe–O 0.00941636, H₂ barrier +0.788882 eV at 1.930 Å, Fe–O secondary minimum −0.311781 at 3.230; with the output envelope 0.00028937/0.00014548/0.00060023/0.00265939, with both factors 0.00013652/0.00019019/0.00074859/0.00228277, while fitting RMS alone raises the H₂ barrier to 1.16636 eV.
- Curvature (eV/Å²): H₂ maximum near 1.520 Å −3.870656 = +0.223549 (fitting) − 4.094205 (descriptor); Fe–O near 2.915 Å −4.093761 = +0.019573 − 4.113334; Gaussian second-derivative contribution −5.014512 (remainder +1.143856) and −2.819847 (remainder −1.273914). Trained E89 gated points (total = fitting + descriptor; Gaussian a separate cut): H₂ 4000 3.150 Å −0.334956 (−0.000913, −0.334043, −0.247054); O–H 4000 2.6375 Å −0.976507 (−0.003985, −0.972521, +0.502573); Cr–Cr 4000 2.185 Å −5.488681 (−1.053242, −4.435440, −3.757690); Pb–H 2000 1.082 Å −3.935347 (+0.002380, −3.937727, −6.593543); removing the radial MLP's Hessian changes these by +0.028290, +0.027582, −0.482125, +1.141275, every remainder still negative.
- FiLM (E89 gated 4000): median inverse RMS 3153.71 (scale), 3109.47 (shift) on 128 frames (2715 atoms), 100% of scale rows and 99.779% of shift rows below the native 1e-7 epsilon; omitting the divisor moves H₂ +0.008265 → +0.010600 eV, O–H +0.107632 → +0.182759, Cr–Cr +0.405578 → +0.426093. Separate envelope mass on E89 gated 8000: H₂ +0.181650 → +0.362621 eV, O–H +0.069606 → +0.202384, Cr–Cr +2.195204. Zeroing the geometric initial embedding leaves H₂ a +0.141934 eV outer maximum plus a new minimum at 8000.
- E95 checkpoints, zeroing the rows of `radial_embedding.net.0.matrix` (and the seed projection's) reading the 0.0/0.2/0.4 Å channels: holes 5 → 1 at 34000, 2 → 0 at 36000, 1 → 0 at 40000; all 24 tails identical to five decimals; bulk energies move ≤4·10⁻⁴ eV at r ≥ 1.5 Å; isolated Li–H force at 0.4 Å +1000 → −16 eV/Å. E88 cutoff check: an equilateral H₃ of side 5.99 Å is within 8.89e-16 eV of the isolated limit, maximum force 1.08e-14 eV/Å, both zero at 6 Å.

#### Experiments — the converged candidate and the isolated-atom limit, §11.44–11.47 (8–11 September)

> control. Energies in eV, distances in Å.

## 14. Record — the candidate ledger of 4–6 September

The brainstorm ledger E1–E67 with the status of every candidate.

#### Candidate ledger

Preamble (l. 4192–4194): the §10.13 recipe had a settled ceiling — a frozen copy of the model is a reference whose *shape* off the data is right, whose *values* are wrong by up to 20 eV/Å where the data are rarest, and whose *coverage* is whatever the random generators reach; one tube width serves all three. Confound: the hydrogen hand-over recipe itself collapses at 40000 (`h_pkg_S10k_all4_oneperframe`, −8.8 eV at 0.885 Å), so tube candidates are judged on OMat24 twins against the label-tube twin.

**Set aside by argument, not by experiment** (l. 4308): a moving reference bounds only the drift *rate* (the model leads it by one tube width per horizon; τ ≫ 6000 steps would freeze the hard frames at tube/τ per step). An uncertainty tube set by snapshot disagreement is backwards: disagreement is largest at the isolated compressed pair, where the tube must stay narrow; it may raise the anchor density (E4), not the tolerance.

## 15. Record — fallbacks and the conclusions as written before closure

#### Fallbacks

Section 13, "Fallbacks recorded, not pursued", with each rationale:

- **Geometric trust mask** (considered 2 September 2026): excludes from the reference hinge every anchor atom whose nearest neighbour lies inside the repulsive wall (below 0.8 of the covalent contact distance). Prototyped and validated against an independent neighbour-list computation, then set aside — "it treats the place where the contradiction shows rather than the contradiction itself, and under the refreshed reference of §10.13 the reference is right there."
- **Crash-and-distil**: run the production MD until it fails, take the pre-failure frames as label-free anchors, repair with the penalty while distilling the healthy predictions, repeat. The repair step works and generalizes across the defect's family (§8.1), but "it requires the failure to happen first and is therefore not the goal".
- **Dynamics-generated anchors during training** (strategy 6): "reaches what the dynamics reaches, at the cost of the dynamics"; prototype in `train_curv.py`.
- **Operator-norm cap** (strategy 11): "prevents nothing on its own (§10.6–10.8), and costs force accuracy on the Pro model". If wanted, the Miyato reparametrization (`W / max(1, σ(W)/σ₀)` in the forward, warm-started power iteration) is continuous, differentiable and free of the `--spec-every` interval.
- **Curvature hinge with the sticky search** (strategy 10): the repair tool of choice for a model that already has a dense-region pocket (§10.2, §10.5), but "as prevention it forbids sharpness but not drift (§10.8)".

______________________________________________________________________

#### Conclusions as written

##### 12.1 The problem, restated from first principles

A neural potential maps coordinates to an energy whose coordinate derivative gives the forces. Training constrains the function on sampled configurations; an update also changes energy and forces elsewhere. For parameters θ, coordinates x and update Δθ:

```math
\Delta E(x) \simeq \nabla_\theta E_\theta(x)\cdot\Delta\theta,
\qquad
\Delta F(x) \simeq -\nabla_x\nabla_\theta E_\theta(x)\cdot\Delta\theta.
```

Large off-data responses can therefore coexist with small average training errors. The observations establish no universal growth law in training length, learning rate or model size, and no single gradient path for every defect: the isolated-pair attention response, the fitting-input amplitude of captured frame tears and the opposing radial force paths of §11.29 are different mechanisms. Structural restrictions limit the admitted functions even on unsampled geometries; reference constraints act only on the geometries generated and only where the discrepancy exceeds their tolerance. A smooth or bounded initial reference need not be physically correct, and a later reference can already contain a false well. Neither a derivative bound for one component nor a successful reference intervention establishes the complete potential's shape and accuracy.

##### 12.2 What the measurements establish

1. The defects include smooth features of the learned energy: float64 reconstruction and independent energy differences reproduce selected large forces, and normal validation averages and short MD runs do not exclude them (§§3–6, 11.19, 11.29).
1. Reference constraints mitigate particular failures, but long trajectories retain false extrema, excessive tails and some frame tears outside both contact exclusions (§§11.12, 11.18); the method is not established as complete prevention.
1. Geometric coverage and effective constraint strength are distinct: finite clusters reach coordination-one atoms that periodic deletion misses, while most such atoms in the measured E83 probe stay inside the tolerance and contribute no penalty (§§11.16, 11.21, 11.26).
1. Structural components act differently on different probes: the output-envelope/RMS combination keeps the OMat24 accuracy budget on its measured draw but fails the pair criteria, its hydrogen counterpart also losing binding; shortening the direct cutoff suppresses the measured outer force while retaining inner false barriers and losing accuracy (§§11.15, 11.20).
1. Initialization, reference provenance, checkpoint averaging and actual evaluation equations must be checked independently: a same-weight intervention diagnoses a response but does not predict the accuracy or stability of retraining; the Gaussian-centre and fitting experiments retain this distinction (§§11.24, 11.27–11.29).

##### 12.3 Working baseline and unresolved decisions

The §2 reference schedule is a working control: pretraining starts from the initialization reference and sparse generators, replaces that reference once at optimizer index 10000 with the raw trained model, then samples one of four generators per frame; for adaptation the original pretrained function is the teacher. These choices permit controlled comparisons; no current recipe satisfies the complete acceptance criteria.

The active investigation tests radial support and centre motion alongside fitting architecture and activation. Remaining: the downstream response surviving radial constraints, the geometry and quality of the reference anchors, the accuracy cost of structural restrictions. A component advances through matched full-set validation and every retained pair/frame observation, then repeated long trajectories. "A quiet final curve or a passing average alone is insufficient."

##### 12.4 Historical calibration of the reference baseline

Framing: these bullets retain earlier calibration experiments and their setups; their conclusions, "including statements labelled settled, do not establish the broader acceptance criteria of the current programme"; later tests are in §11. Readings are OMat24-subset force RMSE (eV/Å) unless stated.

**The anchor set for pretraining (settled).** Loosening the tolerance does not remove the 10% cost: `archC_ref3` (3 eV/Å + 20%) 0.667 / 0.672 at 30000 / 40000 against C's 0.603 / 0.587; the 1 eV/Å run 0.652 / 0.648; force-scaled `tol = 0.5 + 0.1·|F_ref| + 0.2·F_max(frame)` (`archC_refF`) 0.678, 0.662, 0.677 at 20000–40000, 15% off at the end. Cause: dense anchors near the data pull the model towards its initialization there (a random initialization predicts forces near zero; 0.1–0.3 Å from an OMat24 frame the true forces are not small), not the tolerance. Sparse anchors only (`archC_refS`: dilation and deletion) 0.788, 0.657, 0.608, 0.600 at 10000–40000 against C's 0.789, 0.647, 0.603, 0.587 and second seed 0.721, 0.621, 0.603, 0.598 — inside the seed scatter throughout; dimer tails at C's values (H 0.06, O 0.13, Si 0.36, Fe 0.04, Cu 0.10 eV/Å at 40000); Pro frame 0.83. Refinement (anchors away from the data: dilation in [1.3, 2], deletion of 50–90%; `archC_refS2`, `--dilate-min 0.3 --drop-min 0.5`) 0.773, 0.642, 0.590, 0.576; H tail 0.02 eV/Å; Pro frame 0.69 — the pretraining setting of §2.2. Seed 43: `archC_refS_s43` 0.740, 0.624, 0.594, 0.580 and `archC_refS2_s43` 0.725, 0.638, 0.608, 0.599 against the plain seed-43 run's 0.721, 0.621, 0.603, 0.598 — identical at the end; H₂ tails 0.01–0.04 and 0.003–0.035 eV/Å; Pro-frame ratios 1.6, 1.1, 1.5, 2.0 inside the seed-43 scatter of §10.10 (plain 1.7, 1.6). Confirmed on two seeds. Both sit in the seed scatter, so the bounds are not load-bearing: they are adopted because near-data anchors are the harmful ingredient and the bounds exclude them by construction — factor 1.3 moves every pair distance by 30% (2.5 Å → 3.25 Å, 2.2 times the volume), clear of the bond-length distribution, and deleting half the atoms halves every coordination number.

**Short-range walls under the random-initialization reference (measured: none on the bulk of the contacts; the hardest contacts and the isolated pair fail).** Near a hard contact the true force is tens of eV/Å while a random initialization predicts forces near zero. The sparse generators never compress a contact (§10.11.1); labelled contacts and the production pair's compression probes show walls unchanged to 0.03 eV/Å on forces of 8–16 eV/Å (§10.11.2); on the 40000-step testbed the five dimer walls of `archC_refS2` are equal to or stiffer than `archC`'s. But dilation by 1.3–2 leaves a contact at 0.45 of the covalent distance inside the wall and deletion leaves it there, so the anchors carry the hardest contacts with a near-zero pseudo-label; at production scale the model answers with an environment-dependent pair force and, on the hardest frame, a spike, and the isolated H₂ and O₂ wells flatten to half depth. Remedy: the refreshed reference of §10.13.

**What reaches the Pro frame.** The dense Au–Li contact of frame 98341 is far from any anchor the generators produce from random training frames, so every mechanism crosses the ceiling there during the peak-rate phase and the end state is scatter: two reference runs on one 30000-step schedule differing only in a pair-compression generator end at 0.72 and 8.30 (§10.7). Open question: a generator reaching such contacts from the data — directed compression of the *shortest* pair of every frame, not a random pair — the pretraining analogue of the hydrogen collisions of §10.2.

**Which checkpoint the reference should be (settled: the first step).** Scan over the freezing step (`--ref-step`; term inactive before the freeze), hydrogen from-scratch testbed, 40000 steps, references frozen at 0, 400, 800, 1000, 3000, 5000, 10000: every choice keeps the dimer bounded, validation force MAE identical (55.5–55.7 meV/Å, plain 55.4). Trade-off: a later reference knows more chemistry (well at 5000: −1.77 eV at 0.665 Å; 10000: −2.46 eV; 0: −0.19 eV at 1.8 Å) but inherits the roughness the plain run has grown there, and end-of-run curvature rises with it — 20, 70, 129, 35, 328, 206, 285 eV/Å² for the seven steps, the 3000-step choice marginally above the 300 eV/Å² ceiling. On the OMat24 subset the freezing step changes nothing: 0.576, 0.584, 0.588 at 0, 800, 10000; dimers equal. The recipe keeps the first step, the only choice whose smoothness is guaranteed rather than inherited; the end of warm-up (800–1000 steps) is acceptable, buying a more physical well at 1.5–6 times the residual curvature.

**The loop form and the interval (settled: merged whole-batch accumulation, `freq` = 10).** Hydrogen from-scratch testbed; its plain run ends with a collapsed dimer (curvature 2.6·10⁵ eV/Å² at 40000 steps) at validation force MAE 55.4 meV/Å. *Few-frame accumulation* (probed frames joining the data gradient): insensitive over 32-fold — 2, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125, 0.0625 anchors per step (`f1n2`, `ref`, `f4n1`/`f8n2`, `f16n2`, `f32n2`) end at 55.2–55.7 meV/Å, dimer bounded, lowest rate marginal (curvature 199). *Alternation* (every Nth step trains the transformed batch, data loss zeroed): cleanest dimers, endpoint cost proportional to its share — 57.6, 57.0, 56.4, 55.9 meV/Å for N = 4, 8, 16, 32. *Merged* (whole batch transformed every N steps, its anchor gradient joining that batch's data gradient, §2.5): zero cost — N = 8 → 55.4/55.3 meV/Å (two seeds, curvature 16–20), N = 10 → 55.5 (30), N = 16 → 55.5/55.7 (20–45), N = 32 → 55.5 (132); N = 64 reaches 120 only after a mid-run excursion to 1884 (six times the ceiling) and N = 128 ends at 375 — both rejected by the full-run gate. OMat24: `archC_refS2_acc8` 0.592, `archC_refS2_acc16` 0.581 against plain 0.587/0.598, dimers, broad searches and the Pro frame clean. Fine-tuning at the production rate (2·10⁻⁴, 40000 steps from `DPA4-Neo`): plain twin 59.1 meV/Å with the dimer collapsed to −7.9·10⁶ eV, merged N = 8 59.6 meV/Å with it physical (well −1.77 eV, tail 0.13 eV/Å) — "prevention at noise-level cost". One instability: the accelerated 5×-rate fine-tune, where the plain merged form diverged with a sudden NaN twice of two (steps 8300, 14600; the probed curvature excess jumps within ~100 steps of failure) while the other forms survive. The non-finite guard of §2.5 settles it: twins `h_fast_acc8_g1`/`g2` survive 40000 steps two of two (one seed discarding eight events, the other none) and pass every gate at every 10000-step checkpoint — dimers physical (well −2.4 to −3.7 eV, curvature 6–16 eV/Å² outside the wall, tail ≤ 0.033 eV/Å), broad search clean, twelve 1000 K MD runs clean per checkpoint, final force MAE 54.1/53.9 meV/Å. Default `freq` = 10 is bracketed by the two-seed-validated 8 and 16; safe band from scratch `freq` ≤ 32.

**Generator amplitudes are set by hand.** 0.1–0.3 Å, dilation to 2×, deletion of 90%, chosen from the two cases; a universal model may need them per element pair. Dilation by 2 carries a 2–2.5 Å bond to 4–5 Å, into the 4–7 Å tail, every pair still inside the 6 Å cutoff (beyond 2 the anchors degenerate into isolated atoms); deletion to 90% spans dilute to near-isolated fragments. Only the dense amplitudes were scanned (0.05/0.10/0.20 Å shells, 0.2/0.3 Å collisions, §10.2); the sparse endpoints were validated only as the on/off runs of §10.8.

**Memory.** With `--probe-early` the probes run before the batch forward, so peak memory is the larger of the two graphs instead of their sum; frames above `--probe-max-atoms` are still skipped, leaving the largest frames unprobed.
